## Phase 13 -- Rung-3.5 localized-DM occupancy descriptor (leak-free dm_statistics replacement) (2026-06-28)

- 2026-06-28 `28c4f4535`/`f684f6405`/`bfbd726e7`/`ba2192024`/`c8b741bd9`/`04ba69fc9`/`1566a7ca8` (rung-3.5 localized DM descriptor) -- replaces the GLOBAL, molecule-identity-leaking `dm_statistics` (the Phase-12 `deep_combined*` collapse root cause) with a per-grid-point, leak-free, self-consistent DM ingredient. **The physics (derived from first principles):** for a single Slater determinant -- which the KS/PBE and the self-consistent NN density always are -- the on-top pair density is exactly R(r)=1-zeta^2 (DEAD for closed shell, redundant with zeta for open shell), and the gradient expansion of the 1-RDM gamma(r,r') about its diagonal yields only rho (0th), grad-rho (1st), then tau (2nd = the meta-GGA rung). So a strictly-LOCAL, GGA-rung, self-consistent, non-redundant DM descriptor provably does NOT exist -- genuine local DM info beyond {rho,grad-rho} is either tau (meta-GGA) or NON-LOCAL. The user's constraints (keep DM info; stay self-consistent, NO static reference DM; do NOT silently promote the GGA to a meta-GGA) therefore force the NON-LOCAL **Rung 3.5** (Janesko), its own ladder rung between meta-GGA and hybrid.
  **The descriptor (M11plus / DFT+U occupancy form, fully open):** per-spin local occupancy `n_sigma(r) = A(r)^T P^sigma A(r) in [0,1]`, where `A_mu(r) = <chi_mu | phi^G_r>` is the overlap of AO chi_mu with an L2-normalized Gaussian projector `phi^G = (2 alpha/pi)^{3/4} exp(-alpha|r-r_m|^2)` (fixed alpha; default 0.2 a0^-2 ~ the M11plus kernel scale d^2=5 a0^2). Sources: Janesko arXiv:2206.07118 Eq.12-13; M11plus, Verma et al. JCTC 15,4804 (2019) [OPEN, OSTI]; original rung-3.5 Janesko JCP 133,104103 (2010). KEY: A is DM/density-INDEPENDENT, so it is a precomputed CONSTANT (a plain PySCF overlap, never differentiated); the occupancy is a trivial einsum, LINEAR + differentiable in the live DM (self-consistent), and BOUNDED [0,1] by Bessel's inequality => NaN-safe BY CONSTRUCTION (no division by rho/k_F anywhere). Feeds BOTH the exchange and correlation nets (M11plus rung-3.5 is a CORRELATION ingredient, so the C-net is first-class; the X-net gets it equally via the shared extras mechanism) -> complete X/C parity.
  **Implementation (additive, in-flight-safe, TDD):** new `xcquinox/alec/rung35.py` (projected-AO overlap + occupancy); `DMRung35Descriptor` (registry `rung35`); gated precompute (`data.py` computes+stores A `rung35_proj_ao` + the one-shot occupancy `rung35_features` ONLY when a rung-3.5 arch is present; alpha in the cache key); self-consistent SCF REASSEMBLE in BOTH backends (`solver._reassemble_features` uses the precomputed A on the precompute grid; `solver_pyscfad._reassemble_features_on_grid` recomputes A ONCE on pyscfad's pruned grid, reused per cycle); additive archs `deep_rung35_3x16` (cusp+rung35), `deep_rung35_attn_3x16`, `deep_rung35only_3x16` (the leaky `deep_dm`/`deep_combined` registry entries are KEPT so the in-flight array still resolves them). The dfs_step7 v3 + full25 sweep YAMLs swap `deep_dm`->`deep_rung35only`, `deep_combined`->`deep_rung35`, `deep_combined_attn`->`deep_rung35_attn`, and KEEP `deep_cusp_3x16` as the CONTROL for the A/B (deep_cusp vs deep_rung35 vs the frozen deep_combined). The running array is untouched (specs frozen at submit); only a fresh submit reads the swapped sweep.
  **Verification (`test_rung35.py`, TDD):** A vs independent ghost-atom + dense-quadrature oracles (s/p/d, RKS/UKS, batch boundary, alpha extremes); the [0,1] bound; differentiability in the DM; descriptor registration/delegation; the gated precompute + the byte-identical no-rung35 path; the self-consistent reassemble (both backends, tracks the live DM); X/C parity (perturbing the rung-3.5 channel changes BOTH Fx and Fc); NaN-robustness (alpha extremes + far-field -> 0); the DEFINITIVE leak-free **size-consistency** test (local occupancy near a fragment unchanged by a distant fragment -- the dm_statistics failure mode, now fixed); the YAML swap; full-SCF smoke (pyscfad + manual, no NaN, self-consistent). 253+ existing descriptor/config/network/data/solver/gradient tests unchanged (in-flight-safe). Each commit was verified inline against independent oracles; a further independent verification pass is still pending. **Why:** the Phase-12 collapse was the global dm_statistics leaking molecule identity (dm_entropy ~ ln Nocc); the rung-3.5 occupancy carries genuine NON-LOCAL DM information per-grid-point AND leak-free, so the functional LEARNS from the DM without memorizing the molecule. **USER:** rsync the new/changed files + resubmit clean (fresh deep_rung35 train, not a resume); the deep_cusp/deep_rung35 A/B vs the frozen deep_combined tests whether the collapse was purely the DM being global/broadcast.

## Notebook -- DFS self-consistent density-training reproduction demo (2026-07-01)

- 2026-07-01 `<pending>` (`notebooks/dfs_selfconsistent_density/`) -- self-contained teaching notebook (`train_dfs_density.ipynb` + `dfs_demo.py` + `test_dfs_demo.py`, built by `_build_notebook.py`) that trains the modern archs `deep_3x16` / `deep_rung35_3x16` SELF-CONSISTENTLY on a spin-diverse handful (H2O, LiH, OH doublet, NH triplet, + H/O/Li/N anchors) to reproduce accurate CCSD densities with GMTKN55 energy anchors, matching the repo's source-verified DFS (`dfs_step7`) recipe EXACTLY: `update_scheme="per_molecule"` (dpyscf per-group loop), `L5_gradnorm_vxc_step7` + `density_per_electron` (drho/N_e^2), empty `channel_weights` -> the 20x density-dominant `_DEFAULT_CHANNEL_WEIGHTS`, `ae_as_reactions` (AE = molecule->atoms scored with the NN's own self-consistent atom energies), `full_3`/`full_25` FULL differentiable KS-SCF with the decaying mixer + tail-weighted energy loss, adamw. CCSD reference densities generated at `6-311++G(3df,2pd)` via `benchmark_refs.generate_one` (density-only, no OEP). `dfs_demo.build_dfs_training_spec` assembles the spec by CALLING the same production functions as `spec_builder` (`get_domain_profile("dfs_step7")`, `build_dfs_pool_points(ae_as_reactions=True)`, `species_union_from_points`, `build_targets`, `classify_aux_only`, `bh76_meta_to_loss_dict`) -- DFS-exactness asserted field-for-field in `test_dfs_demo.py` (11 tests green). **No `xcquinox/alec/` library code changed** (pure orchestration + tests). Headline diagnostic: the solver-aware `DensityRMSEMetric` reports the NN's SELF-CONSISTENT density RMSE vs CCSD alongside the PBE-vs-CCSD baseline -- i.e. did density-anchored training beat PBE. **Why:** a group member asked to reproduce density-only self-consistent training and adapt it to their own architectures; this is the modern, DFS-faithful template (the arch is a single swappable knob). Documented deviations from Dick & Fernandez-Serra PRB 104 L161109 (2021): plain CCSD (not CCSD(T)) densities; modern GGA + rung-3.5 nets (not the paper's meta-GGA -- no iso-orbital alpha, no local Lieb-Oxford 1.174); `grid_level=2` (paper 3); adamw + linear decay (paper Adam + ReduceLROnPlateau); spin-summed `N_e^2` (paper per-spin `N_sigma^2`). Networks are pretrained to PBE first (see the 2026-07-01 addendum).

- 2026-07-01 `<pending>` (round 2: PBE pretraining wired + rung-3.5 pretrain support) -- CORRECTION to the entry above: the archs do NOT zero-init to PBE. `zero_init_final_layer=True` gives `F_x=F_c=1`, which multiplies `lda_x` + PW92 (models.py:168-171; models.py:164 calls `F=1` "the LDA/PW92 limit"), so the untrained functional is LSDA. Pretraining fits `F` to PBE (`pretrain_data_gen`: `Fx=F_x^PBE/F_x^LDA-1`) -- the LDA->PBE warm-start -- now wired into the notebook via `dfs_demo.pretrain_to_pbe` (`ensure_pretrain_data` + `run_pretrain` per arch -> `pretrain_checkpoint`). **Library fix (additive, user-approved):** `deep_rung35_3x16` could not be pretrained -- `pretrain._assemble_pretrain_descriptors` KeyErrored on `rung35` (no `_key_map` entry) and `pretrain_data_gen` wrote no `rung35_all` column; since `spec_builder` sets `pretrain_checkpoint` for every arch, this was a latent gap in the production rung-3.5 sweep too. Fixed: `pretrain_data_gen._atom_columns` computes the per-atom rung-3.5 occupancy (reusing `rung35.compute_projected_ao`/`compute_rung35_occupancy`, mirroring data.py:453-470) -> `rung35_all`; `pretrain._key_map['rung35']='rung35_all'`; `pretrain_data_is_current` treats a descriptor file missing `rung35_all` as stale so old caches regenerate. Tests in `test_pretrain.py` (rung-3.5 assembly + occupancy column). Also: the density-only run has no OEP V_xc refs, so L5's vxc channel is inert (contributes 0) and emitted a per-step `vxc_ref=None` warning -- silenced in the notebook (the DFS paper's loss has no vxc term); notebook prose de-editorialized. **Why:** density-anchored self-consistent training in the DFS recipe starts from a PBE warm-start, not LDA; the rung-3.5 pretrain fix is required for the chosen arch and repairs the same production gap.

- 2026-07-01 `1c4c6b322`/`3212c84ea`/`761bf5309`/`9798b83ac` (round 3: corrected own-atom AE + DFS combined energy-density metric + the OH-radical degeneracy artifact) -- the demo looked like a "complete failure to beat PBE" (every net 80-160 kcal/mol worse on atomization energy). It was TWO eval/plot defects, NOT training or SCF (both verified healthy): (1) the AE figure/metric computed `AE = sum(FIXED exact Chakravorty atom E) - E_functional(molecule)`, reporting the net's ABSOLUTE-energy offset (the net is trained on reaction energies + density, never absolute atom energies, so its O atom sits ~150 kcal/mol off exact), not its atomization energy. Fixed: `dfs_demo.self_consistent_ae` builds AE from each functional's OWN self-consistent atom energies (the eval already evaluates the atoms) -- the physically correct AE and exactly what `ae_as_reactions` trains; recomputed, every net beats PBE (AE-MAE ~1.7 vs 3.73 kcal/mol, all 4x4 molecule x model). (2) the density-RMSE bar chart was linear so the OH radical (~2.6e-3) hid the H2O/NH ~40% wins; now log-scale. Added `dfs_demo.combined_energy_density` = DFS (PRB 104 L161109 (2021)) Eq.21 energy-density error `ED = 2/(1/E_MAE + 1/(gamma*D))`, `gamma` self-calibrated from PBE (WTMAD dropped; only NN+PBE -> `ED_pbe == E_MAE_pbe`), rendered as a DFS-Fig.2 3-panel (energy AE-MAE / mean density RMSE / ED), NN vs PBE. Also: pretrain + training checkpoint reuse on rerun (skip if `xnet/cnet.eqx` resp. `model.eqx` exist); corrected networks.py/config.py comments that mislabeled the `F=1` init as PBE (it is the LDA/PW92 limit). 20 tests green (own-atom AE arithmetic + harmonic-mean + a real-`runs/` assertion that NN beats PBE on AE-MAE + ED for all 4 models).

  **PHYSICAL ARTIFACT (OH 2-Pi degeneracy) -- why OH density is unreliable, and how we handle it:** building the density-MAE panel exposed that the PBE mean density RMSE differed BY ARCHITECTURE (deep 7.5e-4 vs rung35 4.3e-4) -- impossible, since PBE is model-independent. Root cause (isolated + verified): **OH has an orbitally-degenerate 2-Pi ground state.** Its singly-occupied pi hole can occupy either degenerate component, so a single-determinant (UKS, and the CCSD reference) density on a FIXED grid is orientation-arbitrary / non-unique. Measured OH `density_rmse_pbe` across fresh processes: 9.18e-4, 2.74e-3, 2.66e-3, 1.94e-3 -- each REPRODUCIBLE within its process, all different across processes, all with correct `int(rho)=9`, `nocc_a/b=5/4`. The scramble is **threaded-BLAS non-associativity** tipping the near-degenerate SCF to different components: **single-threaded (`OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`) is reproducible across processes** (2.333e-3 twice). The ENERGY/AE is degenerate-INVARIANT (unaffected -- which is why OH's AE was stable across models); only the DENSITY is ill-defined. H2O/LiH and the NH 3-Sigma-minus triplet (non-degenerate) are fully reproducible (0% spread). OH dominates the mean density, so the AGGREGATE density metric inherits the noise; the earlier `runs/` cross-model inconsistency was this plus a mid-run `refs/HO.npz` regeneration compounding it.
  **Detection (shipped):** the PBE mean density RMSE MUST be model-independent, so a cross-model spread is a reliable flag -- the combined-figure cell now WARNS when the PBE spread exceeds 2% ("CCSD references inconsistent across eval batches; re-run Evaluate"). **Handling (general approach for orbitally-degenerate open-shell references in density matching):** (a) avoid them (use non-degenerate systems); or (b) LOCK a consistent orientation -- PySCF point-group `irrep_nelec` on the reference plus a small, CONSISTENT degeneracy-lifting bias in `h_core` applied identically in ref-gen AND the differentiable SCF (thread-robust; physically = selecting one representative of the degenerate manifold, valid for density matching iff the reference and the functional lock the SAME one); or (c) run the radical SCF deterministically (single-threaded). The specific OH choice for this notebook (drop/replace vs orientation-lock) is a pending USER decision. **Why:** an orbitally-degenerate open-shell reference has NO unique single-determinant density on a grid, so "match its density" is ill-posed for it -- a physics artifact, not a code bug. Documenting the diagnosis, the model-independence detection check, and the orientation-lock remedy so future radical references (including the production dfs_step7 pool, which contains such radicals) are handled deliberately rather than silently non-reproducible.

- 2026-07-02 `<pending>` (round 4: orientation LOCK shipped -- OH/NO 2-Pi density made reproducible; held-out generalization test; v3/full25 figures) -- resolves the pending USER decision from the 2026-07-01 entry above: **keep OH and implement the orientation lock** (the remedy option (b)). The lock is a new library primitive `xcquinox.alec.orientation_lock.orientation_lock_bias(mol, strength)` -- a small, fixed, deterministic, **traceless** anisotropic-quadrupole operator `M = sum_ij W_ij <chi_mu|r_i r_j|chi_nu>` (`W` symmetric, Tr `W`=0, three distinct eigenvalues, generic non-axis-aligned principal axes) built about the **nuclear-charge centroid** (translation-invariant) and returned as `strength*M`. It is added to `h_core` **identically** in every path that produces a density for OH (and the held-out NO): the CCSD reference PBE SCF + the HF-for-CCSD reference (`external_refs.run_scf_with_cache` / `_prepare_converged_hf` -> locks `rho_pbe_grid` and the CCSD `rho_ref_grid`), the PBE seed + the manual/oneshot differentiable SCF (`data.precompute_fixed_density_data`, overriding `get_hcore` BEFORE `mf.kernel()` so `dm_pbe` and the stored `h_core` are both locked), and the pyscfad backend (`solver_pyscfad._build_pyscfad_mf`, adding the precomputed bias stashed in `mol_metadata`). Because the operator is a pure function of (geometry, basis), every path gets the byte-identical matrix, so the reference and the functional necessarily select the **same** representative of the degenerate 2-Pi manifold -- which is exactly the well-posedness condition the 2026-07-01 entry identified. **Deviation from that entry's stated remedy:** it named "PySCF `irrep_nelec` on the reference PLUS an h_core bias"; I shipped **only the h_core bias** (single mechanism, single source of truth). The manual JAX SCF is plain Fock-diag + Aufbau with NO point-group symmetry, so `irrep_nelec` cannot apply there; using it on the reference and the bias on the functional would be two DIFFERENT mechanisms that could lock DIFFERENT components -- the h_core bias is the one mechanism that is provably consistent across both. **Traceless is load-bearing:** the first-order energy shift `strength*Tr(M D)` ~ 0 for a (near-)isotropic density, so the lock splits the degenerate pi pair WITHOUT materially shifting energies. **Calibration (`DEFAULT_STRENGTH=3e-5`, acceptance-tested both sides):** the induced pi splitting (~1e-6..1e-5 Ha) sits ~4 orders above float64/BLAS noise and the intrinsic finite-basis pi asymmetry (so it deterministically locks), while a closed-shell PBE total energy shifts by < 0.1 kcal/mol (negligible vs the demo's ~1.7 kcal/mol AE-MAE, and it cancels in the like-for-like NN-vs-PBE and density comparisons that both use the biased `h_core`). **Config plumbing (opt-in, byte-identical when off):** `SolverConfig.orientation_lock_strength=0.0` (+ `describe()` + `__post_init__` >=0 guard), threaded to `precompute` at all four call sites (`train.py` x2, `evaluation.py`, `eval_holdout.py`) exactly as `auxbasis` is, and folded into `_precompute_cache_key` (the memoized precompute would otherwise reuse an unlocked entry). Reference self-heal: the strength tags the `_intermediates/*_{scf,ccsd}.npz` filenames and is stored in the final density npz (`_DENSITY_NPZ_KEYS` + `data._ALLOWED_EXTERNAL_KEYS` extended, `_benchmark_npz_is_complete` checks it, missing == 0.0), so turning the lock on (or changing strength) regenerates rather than silently reusing an unlocked reference -- no manual `refs/` deletion. The cluster path parses a scalar `orientation_lock_strength` (`grid_config._build_solvers` -> `SolverNamed` -> `spec_builder._solver_config_from_named`) with a `_config_to_raw_dict`+yaml round-trip test (guards the full_25 `mixer_kwargs` list/dict crash class); **no cluster YAML sets it**, so running jobs are byte-identical (additive + in-flight-safe). The demo turns it ON via one shared constant `dfs_demo.ORIENTATION_LOCK_STRENGTH` passed to BOTH `solver_configs()` (-> training AND eval) and `generate_ccsd_density_refs` (-> the CCSD reference), guaranteeing ref+train+eval share one value. **Retrain (USER-chosen "everywhere + retrain"):** because the DFS loss is density-dominant (20x) and OH's density is in it, an un-oriented OH reference injects irreducible 20x-weighted noise into TRAINING (the functional is a pointwise map and cannot re-orient a degenerate density) on any fresh run; so the lock applies during training too, and the four demo checkpoints were deleted (`runs/*__*/model.eqx`; pretrain kept -- it fits F to PBE enhancement factors, orientation-invariant) for a clean locked retrain. TDD: new `test_orientation_lock.py` (operator determinism/symmetry/linearity/zero; ref-vs-precompute identical; lifts the O-atom 2p and OH pi degeneracy for ANY bond orientation incl. skew; closed-shell energy shift < 0.1 kcal/mol; the cache-key + reference self-heal + pyscfad stash) + `test_cluster_grid_config` round-trip + `test_dfs_demo` held-out helpers; existing solver/data/cluster/benchmark_refs suites green (default-off path unchanged). **Held-out generalization (notebook section 9, new):** the already-trained models (no retraining) are evaluated on N2 (closed-shell triple bond), NO (2-Pi degenerate radical), NO2 (bent doublet) -- all real `build_dfs_pool()` entries (geometry/spin/AE from the pool, no fabricated values), using only the training elements {H,O,N}. NO is the acid test: a degenerate radical OUTSIDE the training set, so a reproducible PBE-beating NO density demonstrates the lock GENERALIZES; the section reports k/N models that beat PBE on density/AE/ED and checks NO's PBE density RMSE is model-independent. `dfs_demo` gains `heldout_points`/`heldout_comp_and_ae`/`build_heldout_test_spec`/`heldout_summary`; a SMOKE nbclient run exercises the whole section end-to-end. **v3-vs-full25 figures (the full_25 tail-loss fix payoff):** regenerated the `dfs_step7` `svp_grid2_v3` / `svp_grid2_v3_full25` figure families (+`_val_best`, the featured set) from the fresh `run_20260625` pulls. The earlier full_25 CATASTROPHIC collapse (held-out MAE 75-110 kcal/mol, 5-7x worse than PBE) is GONE: full_25 val-best held-out NN MAE is now 28.07 mean / **15.5 median** (per-spec 12.7..97.3), on par with v3 (23.69 mean / 16.4 median) and right at the PBE baseline (~14.9) -- full_25's median is marginally BETTER than v3's, confirming the DFS tail-weighted energy loss + decaying mixer (Phase 11) converted full_25 from divergent garbage into a functional comparable to the 3-cycle run. The mean is inflated by a few hard small-subset specs (max ~95-97 both), consistent with the subset-size generalization story; full_25 still has fewer completed specs (23 vs 73), so this is a partial in-progress comparison. **Why:** an orbitally-degenerate open-shell reference has no unique single-determinant grid density, so density-matching is ill-posed unless the reference and the functional deterministically select the SAME representative; the h_core orientation lock is the minimal, thread-robust, single-source-of-truth mechanism that guarantees that in every code path, and the held-out NO result shows it generalizes. This closes the OH artifact from a documented-but-unhandled hazard into a shipped, tested remedy that also protects the production `dfs_step7` pool's radicals.

- 2026-07-02 `<pending>` (DFS-paper-parity reference-data production run + two benchmark_refs harness fixes + run-level orientation-lock plumbing) -- prepared `hpcjobs/configs/dfs_step7.dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml`: the SAME subsets/archs (rung-3.5, replacing deep_combined) + full_3 regime as `svp_grid2_v3`, but with the CCSD REFERENCE DATA set to Dick & Fernandez-Serra (PRB 104 L161109 2021) parity, per the paper SI (Sec. II): densities in `6-311++G(3df,2pd)` ("the same basis used for training the functionals") on grid_level 3. The single `inputs.basis`/`grid_level` knobs drive BOTH the training SCF and every CCSD reference (config-wiring verified), so the whole run moves to the paper basis/grid. **User choices:** whole-run 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3; `density_fit: true` (non-DF CCSD on the 214-species held-out pool is prohibitive -- propane = 267 basis fns, ~N^6; DF density ~ exact for density-matching); plain CCSD not CCSD(T) (basis+grid is the dominant parity lever; the (T) density correction is small and (T) at this basis/pool is very costly -- pyscf HAS the (T) 1-RDM if wanted later); orientation lock ON. **SI finding (per the user's recollection of "different bases"):** DFS used a UNIFORM basis -- there is NO per-molecule basis override (the g2/97 traj carries none; `prep_data.py` hardcodes one `ref_basis`). DFS's per-molecule special-casing was GRIDS/SPIN/LOSS, not bases: symmetrized grids for linear molecules + an artificial NO spin-quartet; CH/OH on a 3-D grid with density-loss weight `lambda_n x0.01`; non-self-consistent training for the non-linear species. This run instead uses the repo's ORIENTATION LOCK as the clean replacement for that radical handling. Fresh basis-tagged ref/pretrain/output dirs (`_dfs6311_g3`) since the final-npz skip is basis-blind; `He` dropped from pretrain atoms (absent from PySCF's 6-311++G(3df,2pd), and unused by the training pool). **Fix 1 (shell-quote):** `cluster/templates/benchmark_refs.sbatch.tmpl` rendered `--basis ${BASIS}` UNQUOTED under `set -euo pipefail` -> for a parens basis bash parsed `(3df,2pd)` as a subshell => SYNTAX ERROR before Python ran (def2-svp never triggered it). Quoted `${BASIS}`/`${BENCH_REFS_DIR}`; TDD renders a parens-basis script and asserts `bash -n` passes. **Fix 2 (tiered SCF convergence -- roots the `c-hooo` benchmark_refs failure):** `_prepare_converged_hf` ran a single `kernel(dm0=PBE)` with NO aids then raised "HF SCF did not converge", and `run_scf_with_cache` never checked `mf.converged` so a poor PBE density was cached + re-fed as the HF guess (deterministic re-fail on rerun). New `external_refs._converge_scf_tiered` escalates: plain DIIS -> SOSCF/`newton` -> `level_shift`+`damp` then a newton polish (canonical orbitals) -> `remove_linear_dep_` (canonical orthogonalization for the diffuse `++` basis) + newton; used in BOTH `_prepare_converged_hf` and `run_scf_with_cache` (which now escalates and REFUSES to cache a non-converged PBE dm). Empirically at BOTH def2-svp and 6-311++G(3df,2pd): plain UHF stalls at a higher, wrong minimum while newton/level-shift/lindep all reach the correct lower one; the tiered path returns a converged CANONICAL determinant safe for CCSD. Per-tier try/except makes a non-applicable tier non-fatal (also keeps the pre-existing "raises when truly non-convergent" contract). TDD: a fast mocked escalation-order test + a `@pytest.mark.slow` real `c-hooo` integration. Doubly needed for the diffuse basis (near-linear-dependence stalls more radicals). **Fix 3 (run-level orientation-lock plumbing):** the lock previously reached only the training/eval SCF (via SolverConfig) + the notebook demo; the CLUSTER references had NO path to the strength, so refs would be UNLOCKED while training is LOCKED -- reintroducing the OH/CH/NO ref-vs-functional mismatch the lock exists to remove. Added `inputs.orientation_lock_strength` (grid_config `InputPaths` + parser), authoritative for the whole run: `spec_builder._solver_config_from_named` threads it to `SolverConfig` (overriding the per-solver value so the SCF matches the refs); `inputs.prepare_inputs -> precompute_all -> run_scf_with_cache/run_ccsd_with_cache` locks the training CCSD refs (the OEP `run_oep_cascade` INHERITS the lock -- it fits V_xc to REPRODUCE the locked `dm_target`, so `vxc_ref` is consistent by construction, no separate h_core bias needed there); `benchmark_refs` CLI gains `--orientation-lock-strength` (threaded `run_shard`->`generate_one`) and `submit.render_sbatch` emits it for the held-out job. Default `0.0` -> off -> byte-identical; no existing YAML sets it (existing runs unaffected). **Verified:** the new YAML loads + `validate_grid_semantics` passes + all 5 stages render into valid bash (benchmark_refs carries the quoted basis + `--density-fit` + `--orientation-lock-strength 3e-05`); DF auto-aux resolves for 6-311++G(3df,2pd) (auxbasis None -> pyscf auto); a local `generate_one` smoke runs DF + lock + tiered convergence + CCSD end-to-end on OH + `c-hooo` at the parity basis. external_refs/benchmark_refs/cluster/solver suites green. **Why:** exact-parity reference DENSITIES (the paper's central claim is density accuracy) require the paper's basis+grid; the two harness bugs are hard blockers for ANY parens basis / hard radical (crash-before-Python and a deterministic HF stall) that would otherwise silently corrupt or abort the run; the run-level lock plumbing is required for "lock ON" to be self-consistent across the references and the functional. **USER (cluster):** rsync the changed `xcquinox/alec/**` (external_refs, benchmark_refs, cluster/{grid_config,spec_builder,submit,inputs}, the template) + the new YAML; `submit` FRESH (new output_root + basis-tagged caches -- the completed svp_grid2_v3 run is untouched). The 214-species held-out DF-CCSD at this basis is slow for the big species but skip-if-complete + resumable.

## Phase 14 -- DFS-faithful meta-GGA (iso-orbital alpha + SCAN pretraining), on the dfs6311 parity sweep (2026-07-02)

- 2026-07-02 `<pending>` (build a DFS-faithful meta-GGA as a natural extension of the GGA nets + add it to the `dfs6311_grid3_v3` parity sweep) -- the repo was a GGA; the DFS paper (PRB 104 L161109 2021) is a **meta-GGA** (its headline deviation, "D2"). This adds the meta-GGA rung and, on the `meta_gga` archs, closes four DFS deviations at once: **D2** the iso-orbital indicator input, **D3** the tighter 1.174 Lieb-Oxford exchange ceiling (vs 1.804), **D4** DFS's `(x2 + tanh^2(x3))` UEG-recovery gate (vs the GGA `tanh^2(s)`), and **D5** SCAN pretraining (a GGA structurally cannot fit SCAN's alpha-dependence). Pieces:
  - **`metagga.py` (new)** -- `compute_tau_from_dm(ao_grad, dm)` = `1/2 sum_{mu nu} P_{mu nu} grad chi_mu . grad chi_nu`, a LINEAR contraction of the live 1-RDM against the AO gradients ALREADY on the grid (`eval_ao(deriv=1)`, reused from the GGA reduced-gradient path -- no new integrals, no laplacian, no deriv=2); and `compute_alpha(rho, sigma, tau) = (tau - tau_W)/tau_unif` (DFS Eq. 6 / SCAN 2015: `tau_W=|grad n|^2/8n`, `tau_unif=(3/10)(3 pi^2)^{2/3} n^{5/3}`, clamped `>=0`, matching the existing numpy `subset_selection.compute_descriptor_triple`). Verified against PySCF's own MGGA `eval_rho` tau (RKS + UKS-total) to 1e-9.
  - **`MetaGGAAlphaDescriptor` (`descriptors.py`, registry `"metagga"`, `n_features=1`)** -- mirrors the rung-3.5 template EXACTLY: self-consistent (a functional of the LIVE DM, recomputed each SCF cycle), differentiable through the scan, one-shot alpha precomputed from the PBE DM (`data.py` `metagga_features`). SCF reassembly threads the live `ao_grad`+`rho`+`sigma` into `solver._reassemble_features` (both call sites in `solver_manual.py`; the UKS path recomputes the TOTAL rho/sigma from the summed spin DM only when a meta-GGA descriptor is present, gated on `mol_data['metagga_features']`). **Both SCF backends** support meta-GGA in FULL mode: the `solver_pyscfad` backend gains `_maybe_metagga_ao_grad` (the constant deriv=1 AO on pyscfad's pruned grid, computed once per SCF like the rung-3.5 `A`) + a `MetaGGAAlphaDescriptor` branch in `_reassemble_features_on_grid` that builds rho/sigma/tau/alpha from that AO + the LIVE DM each `get_veff`. **Deliberately descriptor-as-feature, NOT native pyscfad MGGA:** pyscfad 0.1.11 *does* support native meta-GGA (`define_xc_(..., "MGGA")` with a `vtau` Fock term), but the manual backend (which every production run uses -- it is `SolverConfig`'s default) treats alpha as a reassembled feature with no `vtau` term, so using native MGGA on pyscfad would make the two backends compute DIFFERENT functionals. alpha-as-feature keeps the backends consistent and matches the rung-3.5 precedent (cusp/dm/rung35 are all descriptor-as-feature on both backends).
  - **Network = natural extension of `AlecGGA_XNet`/`AlecGGA_CNet` (NOT a new class; user requirement)** -- a static `meta_gga` config flag (threaded via `ArchitectureConfig.from_spec`) that, when True, (a) sets the exchange `lob_lim=1.174`, and (b) replaces the UEG gate `tanh^2(s)` with DFS's `(x2 + tanh^2(x3))`, `x2 = (1-e^{-s^2})ln(s+1)` (the log-transformed s), `x3 = ln((alpha+1)/2)` -- so the gate -> 0 at s=0 AND alpha=1 (UEG recovery, `F=1`). `alpha` is read from the descriptor `features` at `metagga_alpha_index` (computed in `create_network_pair`), so it drives BOTH the gate and the MLP input (the DFS exchange `(s, alpha)`) with no new packing. `meta_gga=False` is byte-identical to the prior nets (the new fields are `static=True` -> the serialized leaf stream is unchanged; existing checkpoints deserialize).
  - **SCAN pretraining (`pretrain_data_gen.py` + `pretrain.py`)** -- per-atom SCAN `Fx=ex_scan/ex_lda-1`, `Fc=ec_scan/ec_lda-1` (via `eval_xc('SCAN,'/',SCAN', mgga_rho)`, tau from the same deriv=1 AO gradients; SCAN reads a `[rho, grad, lapl=0, tau]` row and ignores the laplacian) + the `metagga_all` alpha column, always written; `pretrain.py` routes the TRAINING TARGET (PBE vs SCAN) by the arch's `meta_gga` flag and maps `metagga -> metagga_all`; the staleness guard forces a regen of pre-meta-GGA data files (gated on `Fx_all` so bare manifest-test stubs are unaffected).
  - **Sweep reshape (`dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml`, 8 archs / 88 specs unchanged)** -- CULLED the notransform pair (its log-transform ablation is a separate figure story); REPLACED `deep_rung35only_3x16` with the combined `deep_rung35_mgga_3x16` (`[cusp, rung35, metagga]` -- does stacking meta-GGA on the best rung-3.5 arch help?); ADDED `deep_mgga_3x16` (pure DFS meta-GGA, exchange on `(s, alpha)`) + `deep_mgga_attn_3x16`. The pretrain `data_dir` self-heals (SCAN/metagga columns appear via the staleness guard on first run). Registry (`config.py`, `+3 -> 28 archs`), `test_config` census, and the ablation figure (`ARCH_ORDER`/`ARCH_COLOR`/`_DESCRIPTOR_X_LABELS['metagga']=x_11` + the coverage-guard test) all updated.
- **PHYSICAL ARTIFACT logged (per repo rule):** the iso-orbital `alpha = (tau - tau_W)/tau_unif` divides by `tau_unif ~ rho^{5/3}`, so alpha is **ill-conditioned in the low-density tail** -- at near-zero density the denominator (floored at 1e-30) makes alpha noisy + large and NOT reproducible to machine-epsilon between two BLAS-nondeterministic SCF runs (the GGA columns, no such division, reproduce to 1e-10). Detection signal: a run-to-run "byte-identical" regression on the pretrain columns fails ONLY on `metagga`/`Fx_scan`/`Fc_scan`. Remedy/why-benign: those grid points carry ~0 integration weight (the pretrain loss + the SCF energy are density-weighted) and the gate is bounded (`tanh^2(x3) -> 1` for large alpha), so the full differentiable SCF stays finite (verified) and pretraining is unaffected; the `density_fit`-off regression test therefore checks the GGA columns strictly (1e-10) and the meta-GGA columns for shape+finiteness only (a real DF-vs-non-DF code-path change is ~1e-4 and still trips the GGA columns).
- Also fixed a PRE-EXISTING broken test surfaced here (`test_ensure_regenerates_only_when_stale`: its `fake_generate` predated the `progress=` kwarg on `ensure_pretrain_data -> generate_pretrain_data_npz`); made the fake accept `**kwargs`.
- **Verified:** TDD throughout -- tau vs PySCF MGGA (RKS/UKS), alpha vs the SCAN formula + UEG limit (alpha=1), the meta-GGA network UEG recovery (`F_x=1` at s=0,alpha=1) + off-path byte-identity + 1.174 wiring, a FULL 3-cycle DIFFERENTIABLE SCF on a `meta_gga` arch with alpha reassembled self-consistently each cycle (finite energy + finite non-zero grads through the `(x2+tanh^2(x3))` gate -- the definitive integration proof) on the MANUAL backend, plus a FULL-mode `meta_gga` SCF on the PYSCFAD backend (alpha reassembled on the pruned grid, no NaN -- backend parity), SCAN pretrain columns finite (RKS/He + UKS/N) + distinct from PBE, the staleness guard, the `28`-arch registry census, the figure coverage guard, and the reshaped YAML resolving to 8 archs / 88 specs with `meta_gga=True` + `[cusp,rung35,metagga]` on the combined arch. **Why:** the paper's central object IS a meta-GGA, so closing D2 (and, cheaply on the same archs, D3/D4/D5) is the largest remaining parity lever after basis+grid; building it as a config-flag extension of the ONE GGA-net family (not a foreign network) keeps checkpoints/pretraining/reassembly uniform and preserves Jacob's-ladder rung purity (the GGA/rung-3.5 archs are untouched -- they keep 1.804 + `tanh^2(s)` + PBE pretraining); the combined `deep_rung35_mgga` arch tests whether piling GGA+rung-3.5+meta-GGA richness helps. **USER (cluster):** rsync the changed `xcquinox/alec/**` (`metagga.py`, `descriptors.py`, `data.py`, `solver.py`, `solver_manual.py`, `solver_pyscfad.py`, `networks.py`, `config.py`, `pretrain.py`, `pretrain_data_gen.py`) + the figure script + the YAML; `submit` FRESH (the pretrain `data_dir` regenerates with the SCAN/metagga columns; the completed svp run is untouched).

- 2026-07-02 `<pending>` (cluster env parity: jax 0.4.35 -> 0.7.0, dedicated single-purpose env) -- the meta-GGA code was developed + tested on the local stack (jax/jaxlib 0.7.0, numpy 2.3.4, scipy 1.16.3, pyscf 2.11.0, pyscfad 0.1.11, py3.12), but the SeaWulf `xcquinox` conda env was on the OLD stack (jax 0.4.35, numpy 1.26.4, pyscf 2.9.0, py3.11) AND had become polluted + partially broken: a `pip install` of unrelated toolchains (`dpgen`/`dpdata`/`dargs`, `pymatgen`, `torch`, cider) into the shared env triggered a dependency re-resolution that MASS-UNINSTALLED `numpy`+`h5py`+`cffi`+`sympy`+`contourpy` (interrupted mid-transaction) and left a `typeguard` conflict -- surfacing first as `ModuleNotFoundError: numpy` on submit. **Decision (USER):** don't patch (whack-a-mole on a divergent, polluted, untested-jax env); rebuild a FRESH, SINGLE-PURPOSE env at parity with the tested stack, so "old-jax hidden issues" cannot bite the production run. **Build gotcha logged (old-toolchain artifact):** a full pip-freeze of the bleeding-edge local env FAILED on the login node -- `h5py 3.15.1` had no matching-glibc wheel -> pip source-built it -> its isolated build pulled `numpy 2.5.0` source -> `NumPy requires GCC >= 9.3` but the login node has **GCC 8.1.0**. Remedy: get all COMPILED libs (numpy/scipy/h5py/matplotlib/pandas/ase) from **conda-forge** (prebuilt binaries, independent of the system compiler) and pip only the pure-wheel JAX/pyscf layer with `--only-binary=:all:` (fail-fast, never source-build). Verified on PyPI that the two compiled pip packages ship the needed wheels (`pyscfadlib-0.1.11-cp312-...manylinux_2_17_x86_64`, `pyscf-2.11.0-py3-none-...manylinux_2_17_x86_64`), which the cluster's glibc satisfies. Codified as `environment-cluster-parity.yml` (repo root); the meta-GGA suite was re-run on jax-0.7.0 **CPU** locally (`JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`, 15/15) to confirm parity with the cluster's CPU runtime, not just my GPU box. `dfs_step7.dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml` `conda_env` repointed to `.../conda_envs/xcquinox_j070`. **Why:** the functional's SCF/XC numerics can shift with the jax/pyscf stack, so the run that will be compared + published should use the exact stack the code was validated on; and a single-purpose env (dpgen/pymatgen/cider kept OUT) prevents a stray install from mass-uninstalling a live run's deps again. Also hardened against `~/.local` USER-SITE bleedthrough: `submit._conda_activation_block` now exports `PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1` after `conda activate`, so EVERY SLURM job (datagen/pretrain/preflight/train/eval/benchmark_refs) ignores `~/.local/lib/pythonX.Y/site-packages` -- a stray `pip install --user` there had otherwise leaked local package versions into the env regardless of which conda env was active (the "local versions incorporated" symptom); `set PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1` interactively too when building/validating. **USER:** build the env from the recipe (conda-forge base + `--only-binary` pip), `pip check` + `pytest test_metagga.py` to validate, then rsync (incl. the updated `submit.py`) + submit; keep the old polluted env only if other projects still need it.

## Phase 15 -- Big-basis XLA compile-OOM: classifier fix + compile-memory trim + pretraining-curve quick-look (2026-07-04)

- 2026-07-04 `01c4dfd91` (the `dfs6311_grid3_v3` parity sweep OOM-crashed EVERY train spec during XLA compilation, and the harness misclassified the crash as un-retryable) -- **PHYSICAL/INFRA ARTIFACT logged (per repo rule).** On `run_20260703T181502Z` (basis 6-311++G(3df,2pd), grid 3, DF) every train_eval spec died with `rc -6` (SIGABRT) / `-11` (SIGSEGV) mid-training; the failure tail shows `[Compiling module jit__step] ... took 2m16s ... Very slow compile?` then `LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory` -> `std::bad_alloc` / `LLVM ERROR: pthread_create failed: Resource temporarily unavailable`. Root cause: the fused SCF-step kernel for this (large basis x dense grid) problem is enormous, and LLVM's CPU codegen exhausts node RAM at **COMPILE** time (not runtime) on the ~128 GB `long-28core` nodes. Detection signal: a spec dir with `resume_*` still present (mid-run, so training never completed) + a top-level `failure.json` whose `log_excerpt` carries the allocator strings above while compiling `jit__step`; pretraining (small per-grid-point tensors) compiles fine, so ONLY the SCF-training stage OOMs. Pieces:
  - **Classifier fix (`cluster/_train_task.py` `_looks_like_gpu_oom`)** -- the detector recognized only GPU/CUDA markers (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`, ...) + SIGKILL rc `{-9,137}`, so these host-allocator crashes fell through to `classification:"deterministic"` -- which `cmd_resubmit` drops into `skipped_det` and NEVER retries (the OOM-retry framework `oom_retry_partition/mem/force_cpu` only fires for `classification:"oom"`). Added a `_CPU_OOM_MARKERS` set (`std::bad_alloc`, `bad_alloc`, `Cannot allocate memory`, `pthread_create failed`) + SIGABRT rc `{-6,134}` (a C++ `std::bad_alloc` unwinds to `abort()`), so a large-basis CPU compile OOM now classifies as `"oom"` and the existing retry path engages. Deliberately broad (a false "oom" costs one resubmit; a missed OOM is stranded permanently). Mirrored into the step-6/step-7 notebook builders' embedded copy (+ regenerated `.ipynb`s) and the pinning tests (`test_cluster_train_task.py`, `test_step7_notebook.py`).
  - **XLA compile-memory trim (`templates/train_array_cpu.sbatch.tmpl` + `train_eval_inline_cpu.sbatch.tmpl`)** -- (a) removed a silently-ignored `intra_op_parallelism_threads=$SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK` token from `XLA_FLAGS` (it lacked the `--xla_` prefix, so XLA never parsed it), and (b) added `--xla_llvm_disable_expensive_passes=true`, which cuts LLVM codegen PEAK MEMORY (and time) for these giant kernels; results-neutral (compiler effort only, not the math), and verified accepted by the jax-0.7.0 parity stack locally. Escalation documented in-template: add `--xla_backend_optimization_level=1` if a compile still OOMs.
  - **Bigger-memory node (USER lever, topology-bounded)** -- the primary RAM fix is routing the train stage to a larger-per-node-memory partition, but SLURM only accepts queues that exist on the submitting login instance (`*-96core` on milan1/2 ~256 GB vs `*-28core` on login1/2 ~128 GB), so which bigger node is reachable is the user's call, NOT an arbitrary reroute. A single JAX-CPU train task is single-process/single-node (bare `python -m`, no `srun`/`jax.distributed`, `--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=1`), so `--nodes>1` does NOT pool RAM across nodes -- dropping `--exclusive` for `--nodes=2` would give a *slice* of a shared node and OOM sooner. The user relaunched `run_20260704T170501Z` on `long-96core` from milan2 (2x the RAM). `oom_retry_partition` left as commented guidance in the YAML (must be a queue valid on the submitting instance).

## Phase 16 -- SCAN meta-GGA self-consistent baseline in the DFS density demo (2026-07-04)

- 2026-07-04 `01c4dfd91` (the DFS self-consistent-density demo compared trained XC nets to PBE only; add a SCAN meta-GGA baseline so the two meta-GGA archs can be judged against the functional they were warm-started from) -- DEMO / METHODOLOGY addition (`notebooks/dfs_selfconsistent_density/` + one `xcquinox/alec/` change). PBE is a GGA; the demo now also scores against **SCAN** (Sun-Ruzsinszky-Perdew, PRL 115 036402 (2015)), the meta-GGA the `deep_mgga_3x16` / `deep_rung35_mgga_3x16` nets pretrain to (Phase 14), so the sharpened question is "does a net trained self-consistently on CCSD densities beat **SCAN itself**," not just PBE. Pieces:
  - **`external_refs.run_scf_with_cache` gains `xc: str = "pbe"` (the ONE alec change)** -- threads the functional into `dft.RKS/UKS`, tags a non-pbe xc into the on-disk cache key (`_intermediate_cache_name` appends `_xc<slug>` ONLY when `xc != "pbe"`, so a SCAN cache never collides with the PBE cache and existing PBE caches still resolve), and returns the SCF total energy as `e_tot` (a legacy PBE cache written before this key reads back `None`; the PBE path does not consume it). `xc="pbe"` reproduces the pre-xc cache name + numerics byte-for-byte (asserted by test).
  - **`dfs_demo.scan_baseline` + `attach_scan_baseline`** -- per species (molecules AND the atomization-energy constituent atoms) runs `run_scf_with_cache(xc="scan")` reusing the SAME basis / grid / charge / spin / orientation-lock as the PBE + CCSD refs (so OH and held-out NO lock the SAME degenerate density component the CCSD reference does), returning `E_scan` (all species, so SCAN's OWN self-consistent atomization energy can be formed) + `density_rmse_scan` (molecules: SCAN self-consistent density on the eval `mol_data['ao_grid']`, scored vs CCSD, mirroring `evaluation.pbe_density_errors`). SCAN is model-independent, so it is computed once and attached to every trained net's records.
  - **The three demo aggregation fns gain a `*_scan` series parallel to PBE (PBE kept)** -- `aggregate_density_diagnostics` (`density_rmse_scan`, `beats_scan`), `self_consistent_ae` (`ae_scan_kcal`, `err_scan`, `beats_scan`, from SCAN's own self-consistent atom energies), `combined_energy_density` (`E_MAE_scan`, `D_scan`, `ED_scan`, `beats_scan`; `gamma` stays self-calibrated from PBE so PBE/SCAN/NN share one kcal/mol scale). `heldout_summary` tallies the SCAN beats. All additive + backward-compatible (no `*_scan` fields when the SCAN series is absent).
  - **Figures + notebook** -- figs (b) density-RMSE, (c) AE-error, (d) the DFS-Eq.21 3-panel, and the §9 held-out figure each carry a SCAN series next to PBE; a new markdown cell states the SCAN baseline + the meta-GGA-vs-SCAN question; `train_dfs_density.ipynb` regenerated from `_build_notebook.py`.
- **Verified (TDD, `xcq` env):** `run_scf_with_cache(xc="pbe")` cache name/behaviour unchanged + now returns `e_tot`; `xc="scan"` on H2/def2-svp/grid1 gives `e_tot = -1.166122` Ha (below PBE's -1.160123, as a meta-GGA should) with the self-consistent density integrating to 2.0000 e; the three aggregation fns emit the `*_scan` fields on synthetic records; `_weighted_density_rmse` matches the hand formula; `scan_baseline` runs a REAL SCAN SCF (finite energies for a molecule + an atom, finite density RMSE for the molecule, `None` for the atom). **NOT verified (deliberately -- requires the user's CPU-heavy full retrain, not run here):** whether any trained net actually beats SCAN. That outcome is reported as-is by the notebook printouts/figures and is never assumed. **Why:** beating PBE (a GGA) is the low bar; the demanding, publishable claim is beating a meta-GGA, and SCAN is the correct control precisely because the meta-GGA archs were pretrained from it. Keeping the `xc` change additive (default pbe, byte-for-byte cache, `e_tot` back-compat) means no existing PBE/CCSD reference is invalidated.
  - **Pretraining-curve quick-look (`notebooks/analysis/plot_pretraining_curves.py`, new + test)** -- reads `pretrain/<arch>/losses_{x,c}.npy` + metadata -> a 2-panel (X-net | C-net) log-y figure, one line/arch (canonical `ARCH_COLOR`). Standalone (NOT wired into `--suite`, which needs `eval_holdout/`); its clean convergence for all 8 archs is the evidence that the nets pretrained fine and the failure is isolated to the SCF-training XLA compile.
- **Why:** an OOM misclassified as "deterministic" silently strands a whole sweep (the retry framework exists but never fires), so teaching the classifier the CPU host-allocator signature is the durable fix (future big-basis runs self-heal onto a bigger partition); the compile-memory trim is the lever that needs NO cluster change (it may bring the compile under a 128 GB ceiling outright and speeds every compile); a bigger node is the user's topology-bounded RAM lever. Basis/grid are FIXED (DFS parity: CCSD(T)/6-311++G(3df,2pd) + meta-GGA) [correction 2026-08-10: the paper's reference level; this pipeline's references are plain CCSD, as the v3 YAML header states], so the fix must PRESERVE them -- never reduce basis/grid to dodge the OOM. A design spec for true multi-host/sharded training (the real "2 nodes' combined RAM" path, `jax.distributed` + `srun` + sharded SCF/grid) is scoped separately in `reports_local/specs/multihost_sharded_training.md` -- not yet implemented. **USER (cluster):** rsync `cluster/_train_task.py` + the two train templates + the two notebook builders (+ regenerated `.ipynb`s) + the updated tests + the YAML; a FRESH `submit` picks up the template trim (an already-queued run's sbatch is captured at submit-time, so the trim only affects the NEXT submit), while the classifier fix reaches an already-queued run's train tasks only if xcquinox is editable-installed and synced before that run's train array starts.

## Phase 17 -- Meta-GGA training NaN: two low-density-tail gradient singularities (iso-orbital alpha + polarized-correlation potential) (2026-07-04)

- 2026-07-04 `01c4dfd91` (meta-GGA density training NaN-aborts at step 0) -- **PHYSICAL ARTIFACT + fix (repo rule).** Running the DFS density demo, both meta-GGA archs (`deep_mgga_3x16`, `deep_rung35_mgga_3x16`) died immediately: `FloatingPointError: non-finite training value (step=0, group='bh76:HLi'): loss=nan, channels ['loss_AE','loss_BH76']` (caught by `train._abort_if_nonfinite`); the GGA + rung-3.5 archs train fine on the identical basis/molecules. **Root cause (reproduced independently twice, numerically verified):** the iso-orbital indicator `metagga.compute_alpha` -- `alpha = (tau - tau_W)/tau_unif`, `tau_W = sigma/(8n)`, `tau_unif ~ n^{5/3}` -- floored only the VALUE at `1e-30`, not the DERIVATIVE. On the diffuse `6-311++G(3df,2pd)` (`++`) tail (**Li's diffuse 2s reaches rho=5.4e-13 at grid 2**) alpha blows up both ways: astronomical VALUES (~1e28 where tau>tau_W) that overflow the forward SCF energy to inf->NaN, AND astronomical GRADIENTS (`d alpha/d sigma ~ n^{-8/3}` ~ 1e59; XC kernel `d^2 E_xc ~ 4.2e43`) that the unrolled FULL-SCF backprop (`jax.lax.scan` over `max_cycles`, `solver_manual.py:379`) compounds x the `eigh` degeneracy amplifier. **Detection signal:** meta-GGA `loss=nan` at step 0 on diffuse-basis species with extended low-density tails (esp. fully-polarized free atoms H/Li); the `d^2 E_xc` kernel ~1e43 peaks at a point whose grid weight is **295, not zero** -- so the "benign because ~0 integration weight" note (Phase 14, `HISTORY.md:213`) is true for the ENERGY (weight `w*rho`) but FALSE for the KERNEL the gradient uses (weight `w`). Phase 14's finite-energy smoke test (3 cycles / grid 1 / less-diffuse) never exercised the diffuse-basis + Li + 25-cycle path.
  - **Fix (`metagga.py` `compute_alpha`)** -- clamp alpha into `[0, _ALPHA_MAX=100]` and freeze its gradient on the negligible-density tail: `alpha = jnp.clip((tau-tau_w)/max(tau_unif,1e-30), 0.0, 100.0)` then `jnp.where(rho < _RHO_GRAD_CUTOFF=1e-6, stop_gradient(alpha), alpha)` (1e-6 not 1e-8: the alpha review found a residual ~2.3e18 band gradient just above 1e-8). The lower clamp (`>=0`) is load-bearing (guards the network gate `x3 = log((alpha+1)/2)` from `log(neg)=NaN`); the upper clip bounds the forward VALUE (no inf) + zeroes the gradient of the worst tail points; the `where`+`stop_gradient` freezes the residual `n^{-8/3}` derivative on the ~0-weight tail. This is the SAME pattern as the polarized-correlation **zeta clip** (`oneshot.py:358`, fully-polarized atom differentiated a 2nd time through the SCF) that meta-GGA alpha re-introduced by dividing by `n` -- vs rung-3.5's deliberate "bounded => NaN-safe by construction, no division by rho" (`HISTORY.md:186`). Single choke point covers ALL consumers (precompute `data.py:508`, manual backend `descriptors.py:251`, pyscfad backend). Mirrored to the numpy twin `subset_selection.compute_descriptor_triple` (`np.clip(...,0,100)`) so precomputed + live alpha agree; the legacy alpha in `xcquinox/net.py` (3 blocks, registry-loadable via `load_xcquinox_model`, not dead) got the full floor+clip.
  - **Verified:** `jax.grad(compute_alpha)` finite everywhere (was ~1e59); physical-region alpha **byte-identical** to the old formula (`max|new-old| = 0.0` for `rho > 1e-8`, `alpha < 100`); a NEW end-to-end regression (`test_metagga.py`): a `meta_gga` arch's FULL-SCF training gradient is **FINITE** on the H atom (rho_min 6e-10) and Li atom (rho_min 5e-13) at `6-311++G(3df,2pd)` grid 2 -- the exact `bh76:HLi` trigger -- where it was NaN; 107 alpha-touching tests green (test_metagga 16, subset_selection 70, descriptors/metagga_pretrain/pretrain_data 21). **Why:** alpha is NOT physically O(1) here -- on review it reaches ~1e4-1e7 at `rho>1e-8` even at a NORMAL basis, so the clip DOES touch `rho>1e-8` points; it is energy-faithful NOT because alpha is small but because (a) the DFS/SCAN gate has SATURATED by alpha~100 (`tanh(log((100+1)/2))^2 = 0.998`) and (b) the SAME [0,100] clip is applied to the pretrain-data alpha, so live + precomputed alpha agree and the clipped points carry ~0 exchange-integrand mass (<=1e-9 relative XC change, 2nd-order grads finite). The correct root-cause fix (not a band-aid), keeping DFS parity (no basis/grid change). **Unblocks BOTH** the demo AND the live cluster run `170501Z`: Phase 15 shows the earlier dfs6311 sweep OOM-crashed before any training step, so this alpha-NaN was never exposed on the cluster; once `170501Z`'s `deep_mgga_*` specs clear the 96-core OOM and reach training they hit the identical NaN, so this fix is a prerequisite for any meta-GGA result. (Cluster rsync below, after singularity 2.)
  - **Singularity 2 -- polarized correlation POTENTIAL (the ACTUAL `bh76:HLi` blocker; alpha was necessary but NOT sufficient).** With alpha fixed + the kernel restarted, meta-GGA training STILL NaN'd identically; a fresh-process reproduction bisected it to the **Li atom** (open-shell -> UKS): the polarized correlation potential `oneshot.py` `compute_vc_polarized_per_spin` -> `ec_spin` (`:566`), differentiated by an internal `jax.jvp`. The pretrained net's cycle-0 update drives ~1324 diffuse-tail grid points to **negative** total density `rt = rho_a + rho_b`; in `z = clip((ra-rb)/jnp.maximum(rt, 1e-300), ...)`, `jnp.maximum(rt, 1e-300)` floors a negative rt to `1e-300`, whose square `1e-600` **underflows to 0** in the jvp quotient rule -> `inf`, and the saturated `clip`'s `0` derivative -> `0*inf = NaN`. The energy twin (`:358`) is the identical expression but forward-only (never jvp'd in the SCF) -> the ENERGY stayed finite while the POTENTIAL NaN'd; hence finite energy, NaN `loss_AE`/`loss_BH76` (both include Li), finite `loss_rho` (LiH-only, closed-shell RKS). GGA/rung-3.5 archs don't drive the density negative -> immune; the earlier single-atom alpha test passed because it used a FRESH (near-LDA) net at <=3 cycles (Li goes negative only under the pretrained net at cycle>=2). **Fix (`oneshot.py`, applied IDENTICALLY at both `:358` energy + `:566` potential to preserve the `V_c = dE_c/drho` invariant the docstring requires):** floor `rho_tot`/`rt` at `_RHO_TOT_FLOOR=1e-12` (square 1e-24, cannot underflow) + `jnp.where(rho_tot > 1e-12, z_raw, jax.lax.stop_gradient(z_raw))` (freezes the gradient on the non-physical negative/negligible tail). Extends the 2026-06-03 polarized-correlation zeta clip (which handled the `zeta -> +-1` saturation but NOT the negative-density denominator underflow in the potential jvp). **Verified:** the isolated zeta jvp on the exact failing point (rt<0) went NaN -> finite with the value byte-preserved; a new regression `test_solv01_split_xc.test_polarized_vc_finite_on_negative_density_tail` (inject the negative-rt tail -> `compute_vc_polarized_per_spin` all-finite, was NaN); the FD `V_c = dE_c/drho` consistency test still passes (14 polarized-XC tests green); energy-neutral (physical `rho_tot>1e-12` zeta byte-identical).
  - **USER (cluster):** rsync `xcquinox/alec/oneshot.py` + `metagga.py` + `subset_selection.py` + `xcquinox/net.py` (+ the tests `test_metagga.py`, `test_solv01_split_xc.py`); the fix imports live, so not-yet-trained `deep_mgga_*` specs on `170501Z` pick it up and any that already NaN-aborted get `resubmit`. BOTH singularities must be fixed for meta-GGA training.

## Phase 18 -- Forward-only eval SCF de-fuse: kill the per-molecule XLA compile (2026-07-05)

- 2026-07-05 `d3ac5dbb0` (post-training forward-pass EVAL took ~30 min for one arch x 2 solvers at 6-311++G(3df,2pd)) -- PERF/INFRA, results-NEUTRAL. Eval carries no explicit `@jit`, but the SCF is built on `jax.lax.scan(..., length=max_cycles)` (`solver_manual.py`), which forces XLA to compile the WHOLE per-cycle Fock-build body as one fused module (the Phase-15 `jit__step` kernel: ~2 min compile, LLVM-codegen RAM-heavy), RECOMPILED for every distinct molecule shape x each solver (`max_cycles` is the static scan length; `full_25`'s `scf_grad_checkpoint` further forks the jaxpr) -> ~2N cold multi-minute compiles = the 30 min. Reverse-mode tape / grad-checkpoint ruled out (eval is forward-only; `jax.checkpoint` is a forward no-op). **Fix:** new `_iterate_scf(config, body, init_state, forward_only)` -- `forward_only=True` runs the SAME `body` closure in a plain Python loop (no scan), so each sub-op (`compute_vxc_nn` `@eqx.filter_jit`, the J einsum, `eigh`, the mixer) compiles small + is reused across cycles / same-shape molecules; the giant fused compile never happens. `forward_only` is threaded run_scf -> run_manual_scf -> RKS/UKS (default **False** -> training + cluster byte-identical); the four eval metrics (`evaluation.py`) pass True, so the demo's `run_test` gets it with NO notebook change. Numerically identical: reuses `body` verbatim (same mixer step-index, same post-convergence `jnp.where(already, ...)` freeze, same energy trace); valid ONLY because eval never differentiates (a Python loop under `jax.grad` would build the reverse tape this avoids -- `total_energy_for_solver` is shared with training, so the default-False is load-bearing). **Verified:** `test_scf_diff` forward==scan for RKS (H2) + UKS (H atom) at 3 & 12 cycles + a grad-checkpoint-irrelevance check (5 tests). **Why:** a forward SCF is deterministic fixed-point iteration; fusing 25 cycles + the big-basis ERI into one XLA module to COMPILE is pure waste when no gradient is taken. Scope (user choice): de-fuse only -- the `n_ao^4` ERI runtime memory (density-fitting) and the 4x-metric / per-arch-precompute redundancy are deferred. **USER:** restart the demo kernel to load it; the de-fuse files are NOT synced to the in-flight cluster run (its eval path is unchanged until a deliberate post-run rsync).

- 2026-07-05 `<pending>` (follow-up: de-fuse the held-out ENERGY path too + FIX a cross-file rsync hazard) -- (a) `eval_holdout.evaluate_holdout` runs the held-out per-species energy via a DIRECT `run_scf` (`eval_holdout.py:507`, its OWN path, NOT the `evaluation.py` `TotalEnergyMetric`), so the metrics-only de-fuse left it on the fused `lax.scan` compile; added `forward_only=True` there. Combined with the held-out DENSITY (already de-fused via `DensityRMSEMetric`), the cluster held-out eval now skips the per-molecule XLA compile end-to-end -- the "eval the held-out faster" ask. The two `run_scf` mocks in `test_eval_holdout` gained the `forward_only` kwarg (else they raise the very TypeError below). (b) **CROSS-FILE RSYNC HAZARD (confirmed + documented):** the de-fused `oneshot.py` calls `run_scf(..., forward_only=...)` UNCONDITIONALLY (`total_energy_for_solver` :451, `oneshot_grid_density` :852), so it REQUIRES the de-fused `solver.py` + `solver_manual.py` (whose `run_scf`/`run_manual_scf` accept `forward_only`). Syncing `oneshot.py` ALONE -- as the Phase-17 meta-GGA rsync inadvertently did (oneshot.py carried the then-uncommitted de-fuse edits) -- makes the cluster `run_scf` `TypeError: unexpected keyword argument 'forward_only'` on EVERY FULL energy/density call, INCLUDING training (`losses.py:178/369`). **USER (cluster):** rsync `solver.py` + `solver_manual.py` + `evaluation.py` + `eval_holdout.py` (oneshot.py already synced) to make `170501Z` consistent, then resubmit; do NOT resubmit before that or the meta-GGA specs TypeError.

## Phase 19 -- SCAN baseline: poisoned-dm0 convergence failure on the Li atom (2026-07-05)

- 2026-07-05 `66e3a9091` (the demo SCAN baseline crashed: `RuntimeError: SCAN SCF for 'Li' did not converge after tiered escalation`) -- BUG FIX, results-AFFECTING (the SCAN baseline now converges Li, so the Li/LiH SCAN comparison is available instead of crashing the run). **Root cause (diagnostic-confirmed; NOT the grid):** SCAN (a meta-GGA) on Li's diffuse 2s tail at 6-311++G(3df,2pd) DIVERGES under plain DIIS to a garbage density (E~-4.9 vs the true -7.478); `run_scf_with_cache` (`external_refs.py`) then seeds EVERY tier of `_converge_scf_tiered` with that garbage as `dm0`, and a dm0-SEEDED newton inherits it and stalls -- while newton from a FRESH minao guess converges cleanly (E=-7.4785). So the FAILED initial SCF POISONS the robust escalation tiers. Diagnostic (Li @ 6-311++G(3df,2pd), UKS): `scan grid2 plain` conv=False E=-4.9; `scan grid2 newton-FRESH` conv=True E=-7.4785; grid 3/4 identical (grid is irrelevant); `r2scan` converges trivially. **Fix:** (1) two fresh-guess tiers `_tier_newton_fresh` + `_tier_lindep_newton_fresh` that IGNORE `dm0` (`_kernel` gains a `fresh=` flag) -- the meta-GGA/diffuse-atom fix; (2) a graceful per-species skip in `dfs_demo.scan_baseline` (catch the convergence `RuntimeError` -> LOUD warning + `E_scan=None`, so one stubborn species can never crash the whole baseline; downstream a missing `E_scan` just drops that species' SCAN, NN-vs-PBE unaffected). **Verified:** new slow test `test_converge_scf_tiered_scan_li_fresh_newton` (real SCAN/Li tiered convergence via fresh-newton, E~-7.478) + `test_scan_baseline_skips_nonconverging_species` (graceful skip), both green. **Why:** SCAN is KEPT (it is the meta-GGA the `deep_mgga_*` nets clone; r2SCAN converges trivially but would change the baseline the "beat SCAN" claim is about), and the poisoned-dm0 pathology is the TRUE root cause -- fixed at the escalation, not by switching functional or forcing the grid. Also protects the production `dfs_step7` CCSD-reference pipeline (same `_converge_scf_tiered`) against the same poisoned-dm0 stall on any diffuse open-shell species.

## Phase 21 -- held-out generalization verdict (subset-overfitting, NOT a code bug) + README meta-GGA update (2026-07-05)

- 2026-07-05 `5c9586aba` (answer "why do the demo's held-out generalizations mostly fail -- code bug or subset?" + fold the now-available meta-GGA results into the demo README) -- INVESTIGATION + DOC; results-NEUTRAL (documents already-computed eval data, changes no numbers). Checking the eval code and the data separately established: (1) the eval CODE is correct -- held-out AE uses the net's OWN self-consistent N/O atom energies (`ae_as_reactions=True` puts the atoms in the spec + evaluates them -> `self_consistent_ae` reads those, `dfs_demo.py:630-649`; the anchored `AE_nn` field is computed but DEAD-ENDED, never read), real DFS-pool CCSD(T)(F12) refs with correct sign/Hill-mapping, byte-for-byte the same path as the already-fixed in-sample eval -- so the "failure to beat PBE" is NOT the old anchored-atom artifact or any sign/ref/missing-atom bug. (2) the DATA is a textbook overfitting signature: all 4 archs beat PBE IN-SAMPLE on every axis (the meta-GGA `deep_mgga_3x16` is BEST, AE 0.16-0.46), held-out DENSITY generalizes 8/8 (and beats SCAN -- whose held-out density RMSE 5.60e-4 is WORSE than PBE's 2.32e-4), but held-out ENERGY INVERTS the in-sample ranking -- the descriptor-rich archs overfit (`deep_mgga_3x16` 51.01-51.79 held-out despite 0.16-0.46 in-sample = a ~110x train->held-out blow-up; 4/8 beat PBE on energy; ED 8/8 beat PBE). A code failure would degrade in-sample too and hit density AND energy uniformly -- the opposite of the data. Updated `notebooks/dfs_selfconsistent_density/README.md` §4.1/§4.2 to the 4-arch-x-2-solver, SCAN-inclusive tables + prose carrying this verdict; refreshed the embedded `figures/*.png` (fig_combined_ed, fig_heldout_generalization, fig_density_rmse, fig_ae_error) to the Phase-20 baseline-line 4-arch design from the full run (spot-checked vs committed `runs/*/eval` JSON); de-staled the 2026-07-04 "populate on the next full run" caveat. **Why:** the demo's scientific claim had to be pinned down and made trustworthy -- it is "the CCSD-density objective transfers (past SCAN); energy overfits a 4-molecule pool," a CONTROLLED negative-result ablation whose capacity pays off only on the full cluster pool (26 species / 212 reactions) -- NOT "a 4-molecule-trained meta-GGA beats SCAN on energy." Ruling out a code artifact is what makes the negative result citable.

(e) NEW SCAN full-pool baseline: `precompute_scan_pool.py` (offline CLI -- self-consistent SCAN energies over the BH76+W4-11 pool via `external_refs.run_scf_with_cache(xc="scan")`, cached to `scan_pool_energies_<basis>.json`, resumable via the JSON + on-disk SCF npz) + `scan_pool_baseline` (mirrors `pbe_pool_baseline`, NaN-safe/short-circuits when the cache is absent so nothing changes) + a SCAN dotted line parallel to PBE in the MAE/energy/rung-summary figures + SCAN in `provenance_footer`. **Verified:** py_compile clean; 112 tests pass (10 added, seam-based -- NO real SCF); smoke-rendered on a real completed run (`dfs_step7/svp_grid2`, GGA-only) + an independent synthetic 4-rung dataset -- the 4-rung ladder + downward "climbing helps" trend, BH76/W4-11 split, PBE/SCAN reference lines, beats-PBE marks, rung gutter, and rung-grouped legend all confirmed by VIEWING the renders. **Why:** Phase 21 showed the 4-molecule DEMO overfits (capacity hurts held-out energy on a tiny pool); the CLUSTER ablation is the regime where capacity should pay off (26-species / 212-reaction pools), so the suite must make the per-rung comparison -- against PBE AND against SCAN, the strongest hand-crafted meta-GGA -- legible.
