## Phase 23 -- verification of the meta-GGA held-out result (overfitting, NOT a bug) + data report + C-net fidelity finding (2026-07-05)

- 2026-07-05 `f527080fa` (verify that the meta-GGA nets' held-out over-binding -- AE-MAE 51.79 vs 0.46 in-sample -- is genuine overfitting and NOT a code bug that misfits the mgga functional; ship a data-regenerable report) -- VERIFICATION + DOC; results-NEUTRAL (no training/eval numbers change). The eval code was mapped, then 'overfitting' was tested against every discriminator in the data. **Verdict: OVERFITTING of an under-constrained N/O atom-energy null space + legitimate meta-GGA capacity, NOT a code/numeric bug.** Evidence: (a) training HEALTHY -- byte-identical scheme to GGA, zero NaN/Inf, smooth convergence, mgga fits in-sample BEST on energy (0.46) AND density (4.21e-5); (b) mechanism reproduces to <0.1 kcal/mol -- only H/Li anchored (`losses.py:1273-1275`, Dick & Fernandez-Serra 2021 SI, domain-level + arch-independent), so N/O drift freely (mgga N +158/O +192 kcal/mol) and compound on held-out stoichiometry (N2=2N, NO2=N+2O); (c) converged-but-wrong, NOT numeric breakage -- both solvers agree, all full_25 SCF converged, no NaN, held-out DENSITY beats PBE (impossible from a broken SCF; a density-affecting bug would show since alpha->V_xc->density); (d) the exchange gate `x2+tanh^2(x3)` is a byte-for-byte replication of DFS's XC_L (vendored `dpyscf/net.py`) -- the unbounded x2 CANNOT breach the Lieb-Oxford ceiling because the LOB is a sigmoid squash bounding F_x to (0,1.174) for ANY input; (e) the alpha clamp/mask carries ~0.001 kcal/mol (40000x too small to matter) and live-vs-precomputed alpha agree to 2.8e-14; (f) open-shell N/O atoms computed correctly (UKS, correct spin/occupations). NEW artifacts: `notebooks/dfs_selfconsistent_density/verify_overfitting_report.py` + `test_verify_overfitting_report.py` (6 tests) -- regenerates a data report from `runs/**/eval/` (bug-vs-overfitting discriminators + verdict); the report CONTENT is committed as README §4.3, and the generated `OVERFITTING_REPORT.md` is GITIGNORED (regenerable, not committed). **Corrections from the review:** (1) README §4.2 takeaway "overfits MONOTONICALLY with descriptor richness" was OVERSTATED -- the combined rung3.5+mgga arch generalizes 2nd-best (NOT a clean monotone; the GGA-best/mgga-worst extremes hold) -> corrected. (2) DFS-FIDELITY finding: the CORRELATION net's meta-GGA gate (`networks.py:354-360`) reuses the X-net (XC_L exchange, raw x2) form, DEVIATING from DFS's C_L which applies tanh to x2 (`tanh(x2)+tanh^2(x3)`, vendored `net.py:820`); the "DFS Eq. 13" comment OVER-CLAIMED fidelity -> corrected to state the deliberate X/C unification + the deviation. NOT the cause of the held-out over-binding (F_c is LOB-bounded, smaller than F_x); comment/fidelity fix only. Whether to restore DFS's C_L `tanh(x2)` is a checkpoint-invalidating methodology decision FLAGGED for the user (needs a retrain + controlled experiment), NOT changed here. **Why:** the demo's negative result ("density transfers, energy overfits a tiny pool") had to be proven trustworthy -- ruling out a meta-GGA code artifact is what makes it citable; the report makes it regenerable from the eval data. **Pending (plan Phase B):** the DECISIVE empirical ablation -- anchor N/O -> held-out must collapse ~52->~10 with no training-code change -- would convert "strong evidence" to proof; not yet run (retrain, compute-scheduled off the user's kernel).

## Phase 24 -- dfs6311 train-array pthread_create exhaustion: idle BLAS/OMP pools starve LLVM codegen (2026-07-06)

- 2026-07-06 `497f0a40b` (dfs6311 meta-GGA run `run_20260704T170501Z` had 71/88 train specs fail after the 256 GB relaunch fixed the compile RAM-OOM) -- CLUSTER-HARNESS fix; a resource artifact, not a code bug. **Artifact:** the train_eval tasks crashed with `pthread_create failed: Resource temporarily unavailable` + `LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory` (rc -6/-11, classified `oom`) DURING LLVM codegen, AFTER training had already run (spec_0038 reached step 50/200). This is thread/process-budget exhaustion (`RLIMIT_NPROC` / thread-stack memory), distinct from the first run's pure compile-time RAM-OOM that the 256 GB node fixed -- the pthread error surfaces only BECAUSE compilation now succeeds. **Root cause:** `train_eval_inline_cpu.sbatch.tmpl` exported `OMP_NUM_THREADS = MKL_NUM_THREADS = OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS = $SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK` (=96 on the milan node); those three BLAS/OMP pools are pre-spawned but IDLE (train_eval's linear algebra runs through XLA/JAX), and at 96 each they pre-consume the per-user thread budget so LLVM's parallel codegen cannot `pthread_create` for the big 6-311++G(3df,2pd)+grid3 meta-GGA 25-cycle-unrolled kernel. `oom`-retry could not help (the exhaustion is deterministic on the template). **Remedy:** the three pools were re-sized to a small NODE-SCALED slice computed in the sbatch -- `BLAS_THREADS = max(1, SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK / 12)` (8 on the 96-core node, 2 on a 28-core node), so it scales evenly across node sizes rather than a fixed constant that only fits one node; the idle pools then leave the thread budget for XLA's runtime pool + LLVM codegen. `ulimit -u unlimited || true` was added for extra headroom where node policy allows. The XLA compile-memory flags (`--xla_llvm_disable_expensive_passes=true` etc.) were KEPT unchanged -- they are what made the compile fit on 256 GB; stripping them would regress to the RAM-OOM. **Detection signal for next time:** a `failure.json` carrying `pthread_create`/`Resource temporarily unavailable` after a non-empty train-step log = thread starvation (shrink the BLAS/OMP slice); a bare LLVM `Cannot allocate memory` with NO training steps = compile RAM-OOM (bigger node / keep the compile-memory flags). 170501Z's sbatch was captured at submit time, so the fix needs a fresh submit; datagen/pretrain checkpoints are reusable.

## Phase 25 -- dfs6311 compile-OOM: loss-agnostic per-molecule de-fuse of the training step + validation forward-only leak (2026-07-10)

- 2026-07-10 `<pending>` (the Phase-24 thread-cap relaunch `run_20260706T221448Z` still failed 84/88 train specs, worse than the 71/88 it replaced) -- COMPILE-MEMORY fix; part physical artifact, part BUG. **Evidence (pulled run):** every `failure.json` classified `oom`; the last-logged training epoch is MONOTONIC in the spec's molecule count (nmol 3 -> completed or died epoch 100-125; nmol 21-30 -> died epoch 25-50 or at the epoch-0 compile), a memory-accumulation signature, splitting the failures into two independent compile events. **EVENT B (~73 specs) -- BUG:** the in-loop held-out validation `train.py::_validation_reaction_mae` called `oneshot.total_energy_for_solver(...)` WITHOUT `forward_only=True`, so it defaulted to the differentiable `jax.lax.scan` path (`solver_manual.py`) and compiled a SECOND large fused-scan SCF kernel over the held-out molecules at the first `validate_every`=25 boundary, on top of the resident training kernels -> host-RAM OOM. Every OTHER energy-eval site already passes `forward_only=True` for exactly this reason (`eval_holdout.py`, `evaluation.py`; Phase 18); the in-loop validation was the lone leak. Validation needs only the forward energy, so `forward_only=True` is correct and results-neutral (verified: the converged energy matches the scan path to 0.0 on a real FULL SCF). **EVENT A (11 specs, all the largest subsets on the three attention archs) -- ARTIFACT:** `_run_per_molecule_loop._step` wrapped the whole group loss in one `eqx.filter_value_and_grad`, whose energy term (`losses._compute_energies` / `_compute_energy_trajectories`) stacks every molecule's SCF into one graph, so an N-molecule BH76 group fused N SCF forward+backward passes into a single kernel whose LLVM CPU codegen scales with N and exhausts 256 GB at the epoch-0 compile.

- **The obvious XLA-flag mitigations were empirically ruled out, so they are NOT the fix.** On a codegen-heavy CPU kernel under jaxlib 0.7.0 (the cluster env), peak compile RSS moved <=5% for `--xla_backend_optimization_level=1/0`, and `--xla_cpu_parallel_codegen_split_count=1` moved it 0% while adding ~50% compile time and NOT reducing the thread count. The documented "add `--xla_backend_optimization_level=1` if it still OOMs" escalation would not have saved the run. `--xla_backend_optimization_level=1` was still added to both train templates (`train_eval_inline_cpu`/`train_array_cpu.sbatch.tmpl`) as a free, results-neutral marginal; `split_count` was measured and deliberately left OUT. The real lever is structural: on the same synthetic kernel, computing 8 molecules per-molecule (reused by shape) instead of fused cut peak RSS 2.9-3.1x.

- **Fix (results-neutral, parity-tested):** (1) `_validation_reaction_mae` now threads `forward_only=True` (EVENT B). (2) A loss-AGNOSTIC per-molecule de-fuse of the training step (EVENT A): new `energy_override.py` (a request-scoped injection seam consulted by the two shared energy-stack builders) + new `defused_grad.defused_value_and_grad(loss, model, batch, channel_weights, relative)`, and `_step` rewired to call it (its monolithic `@eqx.filter_jit` REMOVED -- the group-wide fusion is exactly what is dropped). The utility computes each molecule's SCF energy in a per-molecule `eqx.filter_jit` (one small compile per molecule SHAPE, reused across groups/epochs), injects the stack so the loss's channel assembly consumes it as an array, differentiates the total loss wrt (energy stack, model) to get the per-molecule energy cotangents + the cheap non-energy (V_xc/density) model gradient, then a per-molecule seeded vjp reconstructs the energy gradient (`dL/dtheta = sum_i (dL/dE_i)(dE_i/dtheta)`; the reaction/AE coupling enters only through the scalar energies). It uses only the shared energy hook + the loss's `compute_components`/`solver_config`, so it works for ANY `AlecLoss` (the training loop's per-target update granularity is unchanged -- still one optimizer step per group). (3) Two latent bugs found while tracing the failure and fixed: `cmd_resubmit` (`cluster/__main__.py`) hard-coded `train_array.sbatch`/`eval_array.sbatch`, which an inline-eval run never writes, so a resubmit silently pointed `sbatch` at a nonexistent file -> now fails loudly and directs to a fresh submit; and `workers/{train,pretrain}_worker.py` unconditionally overwrote `XLA_FLAGS` with a mis-prefixed no-op `intra_op_parallelism_threads` token that dropped the compile-memory flags -> now `setdefault` (respects the launcher's flags) with the correct trims.

- **Verified:** gradient parity de-fused-vs-fused on REAL PySCF-backed groups -- ONESHOT/scalar form, the real FULL differentiable-SCF/tail-trajectory form, a case with all four L5 channels non-zero, and a DIFFERENT loss class (`A_atomization`) -- all matching loss AND every parameter-gradient leaf to float64 round-off (max |dgrad| ~1e-17); the validation forward-only energy neutrality; and a real end-to-end `run_training` over a BH76 group that drives the optimizer to finite, decreasing loss and writes `model.eqx` (`tests/test_defused_grad.py`, 6 tests). The full alec suite is green (231 core + 502 cluster tests, 0 failures; one pre-existing stale mock in `test_cluster_inputs` -- unrelated to this change -- was updated for the `orientation_lock_strength` kwarg). **Why:** the DFS-parity basis/grid (CCSD(T)/6-311++G(3df,2pd) + grid 3) are FIXED [correction 2026-08-10: the paper's level; this pipeline's references are plain CCSD] and 256 GB is the SeaWulf single-node ceiling, so the fix had to REMOVE the N-molecule fusion rather than buy more RAM or trim compiler effort; de-fusing per molecule makes the compile cost independent of a group's molecule count while leaving the physics, the gradients, and the update scheme identical. **Next (USER):** fresh `submit` (NOT `resubmit`) of the dfs6311 sweep after a single-spec probe on the largest attention spec confirms the epoch-0 compile now fits; datagen/pretrain checkpoints are reusable.

## Phase 26 -- source-level review: training-reference orientation lock, harness recovery, de-fuse review, parity + citation corrections (2026-07-11)

A full source-level review of the `xcquinox/alec` subpackage checked physical correctness and
citations, parity with Dick & Fernandez-Serra (PRB 104 L161109 (2021)) and its SI, and residual code
quality (comment/docstring accuracy, dead code, test coverage). The functional physics verified
correct against the paper and the standard literature: the UEG exchange (`utils.lda_x`), PW92
correlation (both spin branches, incl. the `-alpha_c` sign and `f''(0)`), the `F_x`/`F_c` enhancement
assembly and the `I_a` Lieb-Oxford map, the SCF decaying mixer (`0.3^i+0.3`, against the vendored
dpyscf source), UKS spin-scaling, the Wu-Yang OEP, density fitting, the orientation lock, and the
Phase-25 de-fused per-molecule gradient. One results-affecting defect was found (the unlocked training
references, below); the remaining changes are documentation accuracy, harness robustness, and test
coverage.

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (dfs_step7 training CCSD references were generated WITHOUT the orientation
  lock) -- **results-affecting.** For the `dfs_step7` domain (`domain.ccsd_species_from_ledger=False`),
  `inputs.prepare_inputs` built the training CCSD density references via the non-ledger `else` branch,
  which OMITTED `orientation_lock_strength`, so `external_refs.precompute_all` defaulted it to 0.0 --
  the training references were UNLOCKED while the functional's SCF (`spec_builder`) and the held-out
  references (`submit`) were locked from the run-level `inputs.orientation_lock_strength` (3e-5 in
  `dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml`). For the orbitally-degenerate radicals (OH, CH, NO) this matched the
  functional's locked density against an orientation-arbitrary reference in the density channel -- the
  exact artifact the Phase-13/14 lock removes -- and contradicted the Phase-13 statement that this path
  locks the training references. Fixed: the else-branch now threads
  `orientation_lock_strength=cfg.inputs.orientation_lock_strength`; `test_cluster_inputs` gains an
  assertion that `dfs_step7` reference-generation receives the configured strength (the prior test
  double accepted-and-dropped the argument, which is why the gap was invisible). **Why:** density
  matching against an orientation-scrambled radical reference injects irreducible noise into training;
  the lock must reach the training references, not only the functional and held-out references.
  **USER:** affects the next dfs6311 submit (fresh reference generation picks up the lock).

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (harness: recover no-disk-evidence train failures) -- `cmd_resubmit`
  classified a `dependency_never_satisfied` / `unknown_sacct_purged` train index (a NODE_FAIL / cancel
  that left no `model.eqx`, `resume_state.pkl`, or `failure.json`) as `deterministic` and skipped it,
  directing the operator to a `failure.json` that does not exist -- a recoverable task was stranded. A
  `no_evidence` retry class was added (fresh relaunch, bounded by the per-index attempt cap);
  `_classify_failure` routes the no-evidence sacct outcomes to it (`test_cluster_cli`). **Why:** a
  materialized-but-cancelled train task whose preflight succeeded was previously unrecoverable by both
  `resubmit` and `resubmit-preflight`.

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (worker compile-memory flags reach the live launcher; mis-prefixed XLA token
  dropped) -- the Phase-25 `XLA_FLAGS` `setdefault` + compile-memory trims had been applied to
  `pretrain_worker`/`train_worker` (no live cluster caller), not to the live `eval_holdout_worker` (via
  `_holdout_parallel.run_workers`) nor the real launcher `parallel._thread_env`. The trims
  (`--xla_llvm_disable_expensive_passes`, `--xla_backend_optimization_level=1`) are now emitted by
  `parallel._thread_env`, and the live workers `setdefault` (respecting the launcher). The mis-prefixed
  `intra_op_parallelism_threads=<n>` token (no `--xla_` prefix, silently ignored by XLA) was removed
  everywhere; intra-op width is bounded by the OMP/MKL/OPENBLAS caps. Results-neutral (compiler effort).

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (Phase-25 de-fuse: review, documentation, coverage) -- the loss-agnostic
  de-fuse was reviewed and confirmed to reproduce the fused `value_and_grad` on real BH76 groups.
  Corrections: the "cheap, one-shot V_xc / density" docstring was wrong -- under a FULL solver the
  density channel runs its own per-molecule SCF (`_grid_term -> oneshot_grid_density -> run_scf`),
  separate from the injected energy stack, though it still compiles per-molecule (eager, no group-wide
  jit); the "works for ANY AlecLoss" claim was narrowed -- the injected energy FORM follows the solver's
  tail setting (matching L5, but not the scalar-only A/B/C/D losses), so a scalar-only loss under a
  FULL+tail solver raises loudly (a non-production pairing). `test_defused_grad` gains an n_mol=1
  gradient-parity case, a trajectory-form-with-nonzero-one-shot-channels case, and the loud-failure guard.

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (`compute_vxc_nn`: `v_rho` evaluated at the true density) -- the `safe_rho`
  sanitizer was gated on `rho_ok & sigma_ok`, so at a high-density point with `sigma == 0` exactly
  (rho_ok but not sigma_ok) the KEPT `v_rho` was evaluated at the substituted `rho=1`, breaking
  `V_xc = dE_xc/drho` there. `safe_rho` is now gated on `rho_ok` alone (matching `v_rho`'s own output
  mask and the correlation path); `safe_sigma` keeps both guards (the `sqrt(sigma)` tangent is the
  `0*inf` source). Benign on standard Lebedev grids (no exact zero-gradient high-density points); a
  `test_oneshot` regression pins that `v_rho` depends on the true density at `sigma == 0`.

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (parity + citation documentation corrections) -- documentation-only, no
  numerics changed. The BH76 docstrings (`dfs_pool`, `training_points`) misattributed the
  reaction-energy training CHOICE to the DFS SI, which (Sec. I) uses BARRIER HEIGHTS for those three
  reactions -- corrected to state the deliberate deviation. The H2O/C2H2 atomization-energy anchors were
  sourced only to the internal step-6 notebook (circular) -- re-cited to their true primary source,
  GMTKN55-W4-11 (Karton, Daon & Martin, CPL 510, 165 (2011) = DFS ref [29]), verified against
  `data/w411_full_pool.json` by a non-circular test. Explicit deviation notes were added for the
  confirmed DFS departures: pretraining on per-atom grids (H,He,O,N) vs the SI's 21-molecule molecular +
  regular `(s,alpha)` grids; `weight_decay` 1e-4 (100x the SI's L2 1e-6) and decoupled adamw vs coupled
  Adam-L2; the C-net density-feature transform and the meta-GGA network's raw-alpha input vs DFS's
  log-transformed `x3`. The `_PER_SPECIES_OEP_OVERRIDES` citations remain flagged UNVERIFIED (a required
  pre-publication check).

- 2026-07-11 `760feb5aa` (cleanup: dead code, stale docstrings, residual design-doc/ticket references,
  test quality) -- grouped non-results changes: the previously-dead `VALID_ON_PRECOMPUTE_FAILURE` /
  `VALID_BH76_MODE` submit-time validation was wired; the `require_atom_anchors` config knob is now
  honored (was hardcoded False); dead code was removed (`_purge_stale` unused `width`, a dead
  `import pickle`, the never-called `energy_override_active`, an unreachable `or "(none)"` made
  reachable); stale docstrings were corrected (the SLURM graph is 5-stage not 4; the cluster `__init__`
  export list; `reduce_outcomes` resolution order; the `_converge_scf_tiered` tier order); residual
  internal design-doc cross-references ("THE SPEC section N") and ticket IDs were removed from shipped
  comments and user-facing messages (legitimate published-literature `§` citations preserved), and two

  (plain `import pickle` + a `# noqa` note). Test quality: the stale golden spec snapshot was

## Phase 27 -- dfs6311 relaunch hardening + local verification of the Phase-25 de-fuse (2026-07-14)

The pulled `run_20260711T181706Z` (the first dfs6311 run after the Phase-25 de-fuse and the Phase-26
locked-reference fix) again lost 80 of 88 train specs to compile-time XLA/LLVM OOM, with survival gated
EXACTLY at `subset_size == 1` and time-to-death inversely proportional to subset size -- the FUSED
path's O(N) signature, not the de-fused path's O(1). The run's conda env was found to contain the
de-fuse source, but that fingerprint is the fused kernel's, and the de-fuse was committed only ~8 min
before submit while the cluster runs the INSTALLED package (deployed by manual rsync, no reinstall), so
the executing copy predated it. Rather than re-diagnose on the cluster, the Phase-25 de-fuse was
verified LOCALLY to resolve the OOM (all probes forced onto the XLA CPU backend, mirroring the CPU-only
submit queue), and three launch-hardening levers were added so a relaunch cannot repeat the loss. A
separate RESULTS-AFFECTING bug was also found (below): the training CCSD references were still generated
UNLOCKED despite the config, because the final per-species reference cache was lock-blind.

- 2026-07-14 `599d64726` (results-affecting: the training CCSD references were STILL unlocked despite
  the config, via a lock-blind final reference cache) -- the Phase-26 fix threaded
  `orientation_lock_strength` into `precompute_all`, which locked the SCF/CCSD INTERMEDIATES (their
  filenames carry an `_ol{strength}` tag). But the FINAL per-species reference -- `run_oep_cascade`,
  which writes the `{name}.npz` carrying the `rho_ref_grid` the density channel trains against -- was
  keyed only by `(name, basis)`: it did not receive, record, or check the lock, so its skip-if-cached
  predicate silently reused any prior UNLOCKED `{name}.npz` from the shared
  `external_refs_dfs_6311ppg3df2pd_g3/` cache and never consumed the locked CCSD intermediate. So every
  dfs6311 run since the 2026-07-02 basis switch -- INCLUDING 181706Z (recorded as "FIRST with LOCKED
  training refs") -- trained the degenerate radicals (OH/CH/NO) against orientation-arbitrary densities,
  the exact artifact the lock exists to remove. Fixed: `run_oep_cascade` takes `orientation_lock_strength`,
  writes it into the `{name}.npz` (surviving the two-phase `save_vxc_ref` merge like `grid_level_used`),
  and its skip-if-cached predicate reuses a cache only when `cached_ol == wanted_ol` (absent -> 0.0, so
  an unlocked run stays byte-identical); both `precompute_all` OEP-cascade call sites thread the
  strength. A load-time backstop was added to `data._load_external_data`: when a reference records the
  lock and the consumer passes one, a mismatch raises loudly (mirroring the `grid_level_used` gate) so a
  cache-key gap can never silently pass again. The npz field reuses the already-allowlisted
  `orientation_lock_strength` key, so no loader-allowlist change is needed. Regression tests:
  `test_run_oep_cascade_cache_keys_on_orientation_lock` (locked-run + unlocked-cache regenerates;
  matching reuses; unlocked stays byte-identical) and
  `test_load_external_data_guards_orientation_lock_mismatch`. **Why:** a locked functional matched
  against an unlocked degenerate-radical reference injects orientation noise into the density loss; the
  lock has to reach the FINAL reference the training loads, not only the intermediates, and the cache
  key has to encode it or the reuse silently defeats the fix. **USER:** the fix AUTO-invalidates the
  stale caches (the lock mismatch forces regeneration), so the next datagen regenerates the training
  refs locked -- no manual cache deletion -- but that datagen runs longer while the OEP references
  regenerate.

- 2026-07-14 `599d64726` (lever: whole-node memory under `--exclusive`) -- `submit.render_sbatch`'s

  (a fraction of the node) despite owning it; `--mem=0` claims all node RAM. Applies to every exclusive
  stage (`test_cluster_submit`). **Why:** the failed run reserved no memory, so a DefMemPerCPU cap could
  have throttled the task well below the node's RAM regardless of `--exclusive`.

- 2026-07-16 `599d64726` (results-affecting: the preflight compile-smoke gate false-blocked the entire
  train array by probing a harsher environment than production) -- the relaunched
  `run_20260715T030651Z` reached the gate, which FAILED on the heaviest cell (`deep_attn_3x16`,
  `subset_size=26`) with `rc=-11` and `LLVM compilation error: Resource tracker ... became defunct`, so
  `_preflight` exited 1, the train array's `afterok` was never satisfied, and all 88 train tasks
  (`2087298`) were stranded `DependencyNeverSatisfied`. The failure is a FALSE POSITIVE. The gate's
  probe subprocess (`_compile_smoke_impl` -> `_train_one_spec --smoke`) ran with no explicit
  environment, so it inherited the preflight shell's `OMP/MKL/OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS = SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK`
  (24) with no XLA compile-memory flags, whereas production training runs the identical de-fused
  per-molecule kernel at `BLAS = SLURM_CPUS_ON_NODE/12` (8 on the 96-core node) with the
  `--xla_llvm_disable_expensive_passes` trims and `ulimit -u unlimited`. At 24 BLAS threads the heaviest
  attention cell's LLVM parallel codegen could not spawn its threads (`pthread_create failed` -- the
  Phase-24 exhaustion signature), which the classifier correctly reads as an OOM marker and blocks on;
  production, at a third the thread count, does not reach it. Three controls confirm an environment
  artifact rather than a genuine ceiling: pretrain (same `OMP=24`) completed cleanly because its
  per-atom compile is small; the matched marker is the THREAD signature, not a data-RAM
  `std::bad_alloc`; and the de-fused per-molecule compile was measured at ~11.62 GB, far below the
  256 GB node. Fixed: `_compile_smoke_impl` now spawns the probe with
  `env = {**os.environ, **parallel._thread_env(SLURM_CPUS_ON_NODE // 12)}`, reusing the single-source
  thread-env helper so the probe carries the node-scaled BLAS cap AND the XLA trims production uses;
  `preflight.sbatch.tmpl` gains `ulimit -u unlimited` (mirrors the inline train template) for the same
  pthread headroom; preflight's OWN precompute keeps the full thread count (BLAS-bound, and skip-if-
  cached). The full probe stdout+stderr is now persisted to `logs/compile_smoke_probe.out` -- the
  preflight log had retained only a 500-char tail, which is why the root cause could not be read without
  pulling the raw SLURM logs; relatedly the `full` pull profile now includes the `logs/` tree
  (`filters/full.filter`) so a failed run's logs reach a local pull. Tests: the probe runs in the
  production environment and persists the full output
  (`test_compile_smoke_probe_runs_in_production_env_and_persists_output`), the BLAS cap scales with the
  node (96->8, 28->2, 4->1), and the rendered preflight script carries the `ulimit`. **Why:** a
  compile-smoke gate is a faithful proxy only if it reproduces the production compile environment; one
  that probes a harsher thread/codegen environment than the train tasks blocks runs that would have
  compiled and trained. **USER:** redeploy `cluster/_preflight.py` + `cluster/templates/preflight.sbatch.tmpl`,
  confirm on a compute node that a single `_train_one_spec --smoke` on the stranded run's heaviest spec
  (still on scratch) passes under the fixed 8-thread + XLA environment, then FRESH `submit`.

## Phase 28 -- dfs6311 deep_attn compile-OOM: opt-in shape-padding pass collapses the per-molecule kernel count (2026-07-17)

- 2026-07-17 `599d64726` (results-neutral training-throughput fix for the retained-per-signature compile-OOM) -- the corrected-environment smoke probe of the heaviest cell (`spec_0021`, `deep_attn_3x16`, subset_size 26) still died `LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory` (SIGSEGV / exit 139) after ~39 min, at peak RSS 116 GiB / VSZ 148 GiB on a 252 GiB node with ~135 GiB free -- an allocation-COUNT wall (`vm.max_map_count=65530`, `SwapTotal=0`, heuristic overcommit), not a RAM ceiling. **Mechanism:** the de-fused step's four module-scope `@eqx.filter_jit` energy kernels are cached one-per-distinct-JIT-signature in a never-evicted process-global cache; a full attention subset retains ~26 enormous kernels whose accumulated JIT `mmap`s trip the 65530 mapping ceiling at compile time. Shape padding ALONE collapses nothing: two molecules padded to identical shapes still recompile, because the per-molecule signature also keys on molecule-identifying static leaves the energy never reads (`_pyscfad_mol` -- a pyscfad `Mole` whose basis-shaped `ctr_coeff`/`exp` leaves force a per-shape recompile -- plus `name`/`atom_composition`/`mol_metadata` and the scalar PBE energies stored as Python floats) and on the occupation counts (`nocc`/`nocc_a`/`nocc_b`, Python ints baked into the kernel by electron count); stripping the identifying leaves was measured energy-neutral to float64 zero. **Fix:** a standalone opt-in pass `xcquinox/alec/padding.py` (`pad_group_to_common_shape`, default OFF) canonicalizes each molecule -- pads every shape-carrying array to one common `(n_ao, n_grid, naux)` target with results-neutral masks (padded grid weight 0; padded AO block decoupled: overlap = I, Fock diagonal 1e6, so padded orbitals sort last, never occupy, and their density block is exactly zero every SCF cycle), strips the identifying leaves, and traces the scalar energies and the occupation counts -- paired with the ONLY core-solver change, in `solver_manual.py`: the three `int(mol_data["nocc*"])` casts are dropped (the existing `arange(nao) < nocc` occupation masks accept a traced value) and the static empty-channel fast-path `if nocc == 0` is guarded on `isinstance(nocc, int)`, so the default path stays byte-identical while a traced (padded) `nocc=0` falls through to the all-zero mask. Molecules of one spin-type then share ONE kernel -> two total (RKS + UKS, genuinely distinct code paths). The pass is delegated from `defused_grad.defused_value_and_grad(pad_target=...)`; the flag threads `grid_config -> spec_builder -> TrainingSpec -> train._run_per_molecule_loop`, which computes one common target over all molecules so every group shares the signature. **Verified locally on real PySCF molecules through the real differentiable SCF:** energy, per-channel loss, density / V_xc, and the model gradient are equal to float64 round-off padded vs unpadded (closed- and open-shell, meta-GGA, attention); two distinct UKS molecules collapse from two kernels to one; the traced `nocc=0` empty beta channel stays finite in value AND gradient; and -- the acceptance criterion -- a model trained three steps WITH padding is identical (loss trajectory and parameters) to one trained WITHOUT, and yields the same energy under a standard UNPADDED forward-only SCF, so padded-input learning transfers unchanged to plain, non-padded inference. The existing de-fuse, solver, config, and golden-spec suites pass unchanged. **Why:** the DFS-parity basis/grid are fixed and node RAM is sufficient (the full attention-cell footprint fits at ~116 GiB), so the wall is a per-process mapping ceiling and the remedy must cut the NUMBER of simultaneously-retained kernels, not buy RAM; padding is opt-in and results-neutral by construction so it cannot alter the trained functional -- the invariant the generalization test pins. **Detection signal (future OOM triage):** an `LLVM ... Cannot allocate memory` at RSS well below node RAM is a mapping/commit-count limit, not a RAM-OOM; and the earlier local de-fuse verification had certified the compile ceiling only for the NON-attention arch on a single molecule, never the failing combination (attention x ~26 distinct real shapes x 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3), which is what the on-cluster smoke gate exposed. Files: NEW `padding.py`, `defused_grad.py`, `solver_manual.py`, `config.py`, `cluster/grid_config.py`, `cluster/spec_builder.py`, `train.py`, NEW `tests/test_shape_padding.py`. **NEXT (USER):** rsync the changed `alec/` files; re-run the monitored probe (`hpcjobs/dfs6311_smoke_vma.sbatch`) with the flag ON -> expect two kernels, `nVMA` far below 65530, `{"kind": "done"}`; then set `pad_group_to_common_shape: true` in the dfs6311 config and FRESH `submit`.
- 2026-07-17 `599d64726` (on-cluster confirmation of the mapping-count mechanism) -- the unpadded monitored probe of the heaviest cell (`hpcjobs/dfs6311_smoke_vma.sbatch`, `spec_0021`, job 2089722 on dg016, `BLAS_THREADS=8`) climbed the per-process mapping count to `nVMA=64762` of the 65530 `vm.max_map_count` ceiling at a FLAT 109.2 GB RSS (of a ~252 GiB node, ~140 GiB free) over ~38 min, then failed to materialize a matmul kernel (`XlaRuntimeError: Failed to materialize symbols ... dot.<n>_kernel`, cascading `LLVM ... Cannot allocate memory` / `Resource tracker ... became defunct`, worker exit 1). A climbing VMA count at a FLAT, well-under-node RSS is the direct signature that the wall is the per-process mapping ceiling, not host RAM -- the empirical "before" the shape-padding pass is measured against; the paired padded probe (`dfs6311_smoke_vma_padded.sbatch`, flag ON) must hold `nVMA` in the low thousands and print `{"kind": "done"}` (running as of this entry). **Why:** records the exact detection signal (VMA climbing to ~65530 at flat, well-under-node RSS) and the ceiling value for future OOM triage.

## Phase 29 -- de-fused training step: a per-step SCF recompilation ratchet, distinct from the Phase-28 compile wall (2026-07-18)

- 2026-07-18 `0adab6867` (results-neutral throughput/stability fix for a SECOND mapping-count wall) -- the padding-innocence verification job (job 2089923, `hpcjobs/dfs6311_nan_verify.py`: `ae:CO` -> C + O at 6-311++G(3df,2pd), polarized correlation, `full_3`, padded vs unpadded at every step) ran 15+ finite steps and then died SIGSEGV / exit 139 with the Phase-28 signature -- `LLVM compilation error: Cannot allocate memory`, `JITDylib ... is defunct`, `Resource tracker ... became defunct` -- at MaxRSS 90.7 GB / MaxVMSize 127.7 GB on a ~252 GiB node. Phase 28's mechanism does not fit it: this job held THREE molecules (~5 distinct JIT signatures, ~24k mappings by the measured ~1930/signature slope, far below the 65530 ceiling) and died at step ~16 on ALREADY-WARM kernels, not at first compile. **Mechanism:** the de-fused step differentiates the group loss EAGERLY -- removing the group-wide fusion is its purpose (`defused_grad.py:161`, `jax.value_and_grad(assemble, ...)`, no jit) -- and under reverse-mode AD an eager `jax.lax.scan` is rebuilt by `partial_eval`/`transpose` on every call into fresh `ClosedJaxpr` objects. Those hash by object identity, so they never hit the primitive-executable cache: each call compiles and then PERMANENTLY retains two more XLA modules, because the runtime does not release LLVM code mappings once allocated (`jax.clear_caches()` cannot reclaim them). Isolated measurement on jax 0.7.0 over 5 identical calls: eager grad over eager scan gives `currsize` 5, 7, 9, 11, 13 (+2/call, monotone); the same scan inside a jit is flat at 3. The reachable sites are the two self-consistent loss channels, both of which call `run_scf` with `forward_only=False` (`solver_manual.py:277-278`): the density channel `_grid_term` (`losses.py:380`) and the density-matrix channel `_dm_term` (`losses.py:329`). **Fix:** a training-path jit seam in `oneshot.py` -- `grid_density_for_loss` and `dm_prediction_for_loss`, each delegating to a module-scope `@eqx.filter_jit` wrapper, sharing one `_scf_needs_jit` predicate -- so the scan compiles once per molecule shape and is reused, restoring the per-molecule sizing the de-fused step already applies to the energy channel (`defused_grad._energy_*_jit`) without reintroducing the group-wide fusion that exhausted host RAM at codegen time. The seam is applied to the MANUAL backend ONLY: the pyscfad driver requires concrete arrays for libcint integral construction and raises on tracers (`solver_pyscfad.py:531-556`), and that guard sits AHEAD of the ONESHOT short-circuit, so an unconditional jit breaks every pyscfad mode rather than only the self-consistent ones. The public `oneshot_grid_density` / `oneshot_dm_prediction_fast` entry points are unchanged, so evaluation, metric, and notebook callers are unaffected. **Verified locally on a real 2-molecule FULL-SCF group (H2 <-> 2H, differing AO and grid extents):** compiles per step went `318, 2, 2, 2, 2` -> `190, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0` and the process mapping count went `5449, 5963, 6493, 7003, 7518` (+~510/step, linear, no plateau) -> flat at `2059` from step 2, with the loss still decreasing monotonically (1.818e-2 -> 1.254e-2 over 8 steps) and warmup cost also reduced. Both seams were confirmed load-bearing by disabling the predicate, which restores the `+2`/call signature on each channel independently. The de-fuse parity and shape-padding equivalence suites pass unchanged. **Why:** the Phase-28 padding pass cut the NUMBER of distinct retained kernels but not the RATE at which new ones are created, so it postponed this wall rather than removing it -- a run long enough to matter would still have crossed the ceiling, and the exposure grows with run length rather than with subset size. The two fixes are complementary: padding removes the compile-time signature explosion, this removes the per-step ratchet. **Detection signal (future triage):** a mapping-count death at step N on warm kernels, rather than during first compile, is a per-step retention defect and not a per-signature one; the discriminator is compiles-per-step (`jax.log_compiles` counting `"Compiling "` records from `jax._src.interpreters.pxla`), which must be exactly zero once shapes are fixed. **How it shipped:** the existing guard `test_defused_no_rejit_over_repeated_shapes` (`test_defused_grad.py:247`) does assert zero recompiles, but builds its group with `solver_config=None`, which takes the one-shot branch and never reaches `run_scf`; the O(1)-compile invariant was therefore never exercised under `SolverMode.FULL`, the mode production runs. New `tests/test_compile_ratchet.py` closes that gap -- steady-state-quiet under FULL both unpadded and with padding ON over a multi-molecule group, the per-channel seam invariant, the pyscfad no-trace guard, and a self-oracle for the compile counter (`tests/fixtures/compile_counter.py`) so a broken instrument cannot pass by reporting zeros. Files: `oneshot.py`, `losses.py`, NEW `tests/test_compile_ratchet.py`, NEW `tests/fixtures/compile_counter.py`, `tests/test_losses_step7.py`, NEW `hpcjobs/alec_regression.sbatch`. **NEXT (USER):** rsync the changed `alec/` files, `sbatch ~/alec_regression.sbatch` (four independent batches, per-batch exit codes), then re-run `hpcjobs/dfs6311_smoke_vma_padded.sbatch` and confirm `nVMA` holds flat across the 20 s samples instead of climbing.
- 2026-07-18 `0adab6867` (measured departure from bit-for-bit reproducibility, recorded not fixed) -- moving the density channel under jit changes its arithmetic at the 1e-14 level, and `solver_manual.py:387-389` (and the UKS twin at `:635-637`) takes a DISCRETE branch on that value: `is_converged_from_energies` compares `|dE| < conv_tol`, then `jnp.where(already, state.density_matrix, D_mixed)` freezes the density. Per-cycle energies differ eager-vs-jit by 1.4-2.8e-14 Ha, so a `conv_tol` landing inside that gap makes the two paths freeze at DIFFERENT cycles; a contrived `conv_tol` reproduces a 27% change in the density-channel integral. At the production `conv_tol=1e-6` the per-(molecule, step) straddle probability is ~4e-8, i.e. ~1e-3 to 1e-2 over a multi-thousand-step run. Measured MANUAL-path deltas otherwise: forward 7.1e-15 to 1.7e-13 absolute (6.1e-16 relative), worst RELATIVE gradient deviation 4.8e-15 (~22x float64 eps), and bit-identical for H2O/O FULL, DF FULL, and every descriptor architecture. **Why recorded rather than fixed:** the asymmetry is PRE-EXISTING and this change reduces it -- the energy channel was already jitted (`defused_grad.py:72-93`) while the density channel was eager, so the two channels could already freeze at different cycles for the same molecule; both now take the same path. Removing the sensitivity outright means changing SCF convergence semantics, which is a results-affecting change that needs its own controlled experiment rather than riding along with a throughput fix. Related, also unfixed: `SolverConfig.__post_init__` (`solver.py:83`) does not validate that `mixer_kwargs` is hashable, so a hand-written dict surfaces as a large equinox "Non-hashable static arguments" error at the first jit rather than at construction (the cluster path is safe -- `grid_config._parse_mixer_kwargs` normalizes to tuple-of-pairs -- but notebook and script callers are not).
- 2026-07-18 `0adab6867` (result banked from the failed job) -- job 2089923 did answer its Part A question before dying: the shape-padding pass is INNOCENT of the `ae:CO` NaN at the production basis. Over 16+ steps on the exact open-shell species that failed on the cluster, `max|grad_padded - grad_unpadded|` held at 1.819e-12 (float64 round-off) with a matching loss trajectory. The job's Part B (aggressive `(10.0, 2.0)` reference drift plus first-non-finite-op isolation) was never reached, so the `ae:CO` singularity itself remains UNREPRODUCED -- 15+ steps at `(3.0, 1.5)` drift stayed finite (1.16e4 -> 5.78e2 -> 6.04e2). **Why:** it separates the two failures cleanly. Padding is cleared, the mapping-count death is now understood and fixed, and the polarized-correlation NaN is an independent open item. Note for that hunt: `_abort_if_nonfinite` (`train.py:145-176`) checks the loss and its components but NEVER the gradient, so a finite-loss / non-finite-gradient step passes the guard, reaches the optimizer, and corrupts every weight -- the abort then fires one step later on the next group evaluated, which means "step 5, group `ae:CO`" is where corruption surfaced, not necessarily where it originated. Note also that under the new density-channel jit, XLA eliminates work the channel does not consume (`features_used`, `energy_trace`, `total_energy`), so a NaN confined to those quantities is no longer computed there.

## Phase 30 -- gradient-level abort guard, multi-group compile-stability coverage, padding flag in production config (2026-07-19)

- 2026-07-19 `14799bde7` (cluster test-environment fix, no code-path change) -- the first run of the Phase-29 regression job (2090652) failed all four batches in 4 seconds: every `python -m pytest` invocation printed `No module named pytest` and exited 1. The production `xcquinox_j070` env had never carried pytest -- it exists to run training, and no test job had targeted it before -- so the job tested nothing. pytest 9.1.1 was installed into the env (a first attempt hit the group disk quota; ~2 GB of a stale vendored source tree under the group project dir was removed to clear it; the local suite runs pytest 9.0.3, same major). `alec_regression.sbatch` now probes `python -m pytest --version` right after env activation and exits 99 with a distinct message when it fails. **Why:** four sub-second "batch failures" with per-batch exit code 1 are indistinguishable at a glance from four real test failures; the fail-fast probe makes a broken environment unmistakable and cheap.
- 2026-07-19 `14799bde7` (fail-loud guard extension; changes abort behavior and diagnostics, not trained results) -- `_abort_if_nonfinite` (`train.py`) gains an optional `grads=` sweep: every inexact-array leaf of the gradient pytree fed to `optimizer.update` is checked, and the guard raises even when the loss and all components are finite, reporting the first non-finite parameter path (`jax.tree_util.keystr`), its shape and NaN/Inf element counts, and the bad/total leaf count. All six call sites now pass their gradients: the per_molecule, static, and both twophase sites via their step functions (`_step`, `_train_step`) returning the raw gradient pytree alongside the loss, the lossnorm site via `_normed_step` likewise, and the gradnorm site via the `model_grads` already in loop scope. The guard runs before every `tracker.update`, so a corrupted model cannot enter the best-model/validation trackers or a resume checkpoint. Cost is negligible: the parameter trees are small (3x16 networks) and the per-step host sync already existed for the loss. Tests: finite-loss/NaN-grad raises naming the leaf path, counts are reported across multiple bad leaves, `grads=None` preserves the prior loss-only semantics exactly (the four pre-existing guard tests pass unchanged), and two loop-seam tests prove the per_molecule and static loops abort AT the corrupt step naming the group (previously they applied the corrupt update and survived to the next step). **Why:** the loss-level guard has a one-step blind spot recorded in Phase 29 -- a finite-loss/non-finite-gradient step passes the guard, corrupts every weight through `apply_updates`, and aborts one step LATE on the next group's now-NaN loss, so the production abort "step 5, group ae:CO" names where corruption SURFACED, not necessarily where it originated. The sweep converts the next reproduction into isolation evidence: the abort now names the true origin step and the first non-finite parameter path.
- 2026-07-19 `14799bde7` (banked from smoke job 2090653 + the coverage that explains it) -- the padded VMA smoke re-run under the Phase-29 jit seam aborted at step 5, group `ae:CO`, with `loss_AE`/`loss_rho`/`loss_vxc` all non-finite: the production polarized-correlation NaN now reproduces deterministically in a ~25-minute smoke on `spec_0021`, upgrading the open item from "unreproduced" (Phase 29) to "has a reliable reproducer". The mapping count at the abort was 12,994 -- down from the 18,495 recorded for the padding-only probe of the same spec -- but the trace grew ~2,000 mappings/step across steps 1-5 with no group yet repeated, which the single-group compile-stability tests cannot classify: a kernel keyed on group identity would pass them and still compile on every step of a real run. New `test_multi_group_padded_loop_stops_compiling_after_first_epoch` closes that gap by mirroring the production loop exactly -- a NEW group each optimizer step (different species, natural shapes, field-presence pattern, and molecule count) under ONE global `common_pad_target`, three epochs. Result: epoch 1 compiles (first encounters), epochs 2 and 3 compile exactly ZERO and the mapping count holds flat. The observed cluster growth is therefore bounded first-encounter compilation -- one compile per (spin-type x field-presence) variant, closed after every variant has been seen, expected to plateau within the first epoch -- not a residual per-call ratchet. On-cluster confirmation past step 5 is blocked by the NaN itself; the re-run with the gradient guard live provides it together with the NaN's origin. `pad_group_to_common_shape: true` is now set in `hpcjobs/configs/dfs_step7.dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml` (the flag had existed since Phase 28 but no production config carried it; results-neutral by the Phase-28 equivalence tests). **Why:** the mapping-ceiling fixes are only trustworthy if per-step growth is understood; classifying it as bounded-by-variant-count converts the remaining risk into an arithmetic bound (variants x mappings-per-kernel, far below the 65,530 ceiling) and leaves the polarized-correlation NaN as the single open item gating the production resubmit.

## Phase 31 -- cluster verdicts for Phases 28-30, pytest teardown false-red fix, ae:CO exonerated by the gradient sweep, NaN-isolation instrument (2026-07-19)

- 2026-07-19 `d6e0825fa` (test-infrastructure fix; results-gating, not results-changing) -- the Phase-30 regression re-run (job 2091615) was CONTENT-GREEN: batch 1 `25 passed, 1 deselected` (compile-ratchet + de-fuse parity + shape-padding suites -- the on-cluster confirmation Phases 28/29/30 were waiting for), batch 2 `67 passed, 2 xfailed`, batch 3 `91 passed`, batch 4 `28 passed`; 211 passed, zero failures. The job still reported FAILED because batch 2's interpreter, AFTER printing its green summary, died in glibc heap corruption during JAX's atexit backend cleanup -- log lines in sequence: the summary, `corrupted size vs. prev_size while consolidating`, the SLURM `Aborted (core dumped)` line, `batch 2 exit code: 134` -- the long-known local teardown core dump now flipping a production exit-code consumer. With the signature confirmed, the held Phase 28-30 lineage was pushed (`2b8990d24..e1e309822`, seven commits). **Fix:** the tests conftest records the session status in `pytest_sessionfinish` and exits via `os._exit(status)` in a `trylast` `pytest_unconfigure` (stdout/stderr flushed first -- `os._exit` skips buffer flushing; `XCQ_NO_HARD_EXIT=1` restores stock teardown), so the interpreter never reaches the corrupting atexit and the true pytest status survives. Verified by subprocess sessions in `tests/test_conftest_hard_exit.py` plus an exit-code battery: all eight reachable pytest status classes (pass 0, fail 1, collection error 2, KeyboardInterrupt 2, INTERNALERROR 3, usage error 4, no-tests 5, collect-only 0) bit-identical to stock teardown; the atexit-probe discriminator (a registered `os._exit(77)` handler) exits 0 with the hook and 77 against the pre-change conftest -- the exact green-summary/hijacked-exit false-red class; pytest-cov 7.1.0 XML and the CI two-step `--cov-append` handoff survive (cov writes its reports in its `pytest_runtestloop` hookwrapper, before `pytest_unconfigure`); the `sys.exit(pytest.main([__file__]))` self-run guards keep their exit codes. Regression batch 2 now also runs the new test file. **Why:** a batch harness that reads `rc=$?` cannot distinguish a teardown core dump from a real failure, so one benign dump invalidates a 37-minute green job; exiting before interpreter teardown removes the entire class while preserving every consumer's semantics. **Detection signal (recurring-artifact convention):** a green `N passed` summary immediately followed by a glibc abort (`corrupted size vs. prev_size` / `free(): ...`) and exit 134 is the teardown dump, not a test failure; the content verdict is the summary line, and the remedy is this hook.
- 2026-07-19 `804e5b672` (isolation evidence banked + instrument; no trained-results change) -- the padded smoke re-run with the Phase-30 gradient sweep live (job 2091616) aborted at per-molecule step 4, group `bh76:OH+N2_to_H+N2O`, with a FINITE loss (1.0856e-2, every component finite) and ALL 36/36 gradient leaves NaN (first leaf in tree-flatten order `.xnet.net.layers[0].weight`, n_nan=16, n_inf=0 -- flatten order is not causal; every leaf of both networks is poisoned, so the NaN enters on a path shared by all parameters, i.e. inside the SCF/solver or a loss channel). This EXONERATES `ae:CO`: run 2090653's step-5 abort was the one-step-late echo of this step's silently applied NaN gradient (exactly the blind spot the Phase-30 sweep closed), and it explains why the CO/C/O reproduction attempts stayed finite -- they targeted the wrong group. Species now in play: HO (X2Pi, orbitally degenerate), H (fully spin-polarized zeta=+-1; the zeta path is guarded by the +-(1-1e-6) clip + double-where), N2, N2O (closed shell). Both leading mechanisms are basis-linked, consistent with the NaN appearing only at 6-311++G(3df,2pd): the diffuse functions populate the rho in (1e-18, 1e-10) band where `ec_base` (PW92) still differentiates unmasked (`_NN_TAIL_THRESHOLD` guards only Fx/Fc; `d2rs/drho2 ~ rho^(-7/3)`), and they push the overlap/Fock spectrum toward near-degeneracy where the plain `jnp.linalg.eigh` backward's `1/(lambda_i-lambda_j)` factors are bounded only by the 1e-8 forward symmetry-break (OH's 2Pi pair by the 3e-5 orientation lock) -- a bare eigh-degeneracy NaN alone is demoted because def2-SVP trained these same degenerate species without incident. NEW `hpcjobs/dfs6311_nan_isolate.{py,sbatch}` (supersedes the CO/C/O-targeted `dfs6311_nan_verify` pair): decomposes `defused_value_and_grad`'s three stages -- per-molecule forward trajectories, the assembly gradient with true channel EXCISION (a zero channel weight still traces the channel's backward and `0*NaN=NaN`, so channels are removed from the assembled total instead), and per-molecule energy VJPs with real and unit seeds -- to name the molecule and the channel; `--spec` replays a real spec's epoch 0 exactly (pretrained weights, seeded shuffle, real optimizer updates), with `--pad` supplying the padding the July-15 spec files predate (job 2091616 was padded only via the `_train_one_spec --pad-group` CLI override -- a stored-spec replay without the flag would silently run the wrong configuration) and `--horizon-epochs 1` mirroring the smoke's schedule; `--local` rebuilds the single group standalone at the production configuration (pool geometries from `g2_97.traj`, DF, orientation lock, polarized correlation, aux-only reaction species, per-electron density loss) as a fresh-init structural probe; `--ablate` applies one seam toggle per process (`SYM_BREAK_SHIFT` 1e-8->1e-6, `DEGENERACY_REG` 1e-10->1e-8, an `ec_base` tail double-where `stop_gradient` freeze below `_NN_TAIL_THRESHOLD` -- the Phase-17 meta-GGA alpha pattern applied to correlation -- or the zeta boundary eps 1e-6->1e-4), with every patch target verified against the actual import bindings in `solver`/`solver_manual`/`oneshot`. **Why:** the finite-loss/all-leaf-NaN signature localizes the defect to the backward pass but not to a species or seam; the decomposition plus the ablation matrix converts the next cluster run into a mechanism verdict, after which the fix lands once, in one shared helper across energy/potential/baseline (PW92: PRB 45, 13244 (1992); DFS: PRB 104, L161109 (2021)). **NEXT (USER):** rsync `xcquinox/alec/tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_hard_exit.py`, `hpcjobs/alec_regression.sbatch`, `hpcjobs/dfs6311_nan_isolate.{py,sbatch}`; resubmit `alec_regression.sbatch` (expect `ALL BATCHES PASSED` -- validates the hard-exit on the exact workload that cored); `sbatch dfs6311_nan_isolate.sbatch` (padded smoke replay; exit 3 with a VERDICT TABLE is the informative outcome) and optionally the fresh-init structural variant via `ISOLATE_ARGS="--local --padded"`; ablation submissions follow the verdict.

## Phase 32 -- cluster verdicts for the isolation round: hard-exit validated, the NaN localized to the padded computation path (2026-07-20)

- 2026-07-20 `132ad2bfc` (verdicts banked from jobs 2091732/2091733/2091734; comment-only instrument corrections, no code-path change) -- the three 2026-07-19 submissions returned:
  - **Regression 2091733: green on content AND exit code.** sacct `COMPLETED 0:0`; batch summaries `25 passed, 1 deselected` / `71 passed, 1 skipped, 2 xfailed` (batch 2 grew by `test_conftest_hard_exit.py`) / `91 passed` / `28 passed`; `[regression] ALL BATCHES PASSED`. The conftest hard-exit (`d6e0825fa`) is thereby validated on the exact workload whose teardown dump produced the Phase-31 false red -- the same batch 2 that previously cored after its green summary now delivers exit 0. The held lineage (`d6e0825fa`, `804e5b672`, `1593d34c5`) was pushed on this verdict.
  - **Padded spec replay 2091734 (primary): exit 3, and the decomposition result IS the localization.** The replay aborted at step 4, group `bh76:OH+N2_to_H+N2O`, loss 1.085617e-2 with all 36 gradient leaves NaN -- matching smoke 2091616 to every printed digit across independent processes, so the padded training path is deterministic, not a threading coin-flip. The decomposition then reported ALL stages finite: per-molecule forward trajectories (HO -75.76644702, N2 -109.57957167, H -0.50290016, N2O -184.77372524), the assembly gradient under every channel excision, and all eight per-molecule energy VJPs -- at a forward loss of 1.0879948e-2. The two numbers are the finding: the decomposition intentionally re-runs the group WITHOUT `pad_target` (its neutrality justification was proven only on ae:CO), so at identical parameters, in the same process, the padded gradient is NaN while the unpadded gradient is finite and the forward loss shifts by 0.219% -- orders beyond round-off unless amplified. The defect therefore lives in the padded computation path for this group, and the "all ok" verdict table exonerates nothing; the padding-neutrality proof does not transfer from ae:CO to a group containing an empty-beta-spin H, aux-only reaction members, and the 3e-5-split OH 2Pi pair. The tail (`ec_base` rho^(-7/3) band) and eigh-backward (1/dlambda) candidates remain live as the amplifier the padded path's perturbation crosses. Instrument comments corrected accordingly: the sbatch ablation example now restates `--pad --horizon-epochs 1` (ISOLATE_ARGS replaces the whole argument list; the abbreviated example would have silently submitted unpadded ablations -- the same configuration-drift class that left the July-15 spec files unpadded), and the decompose() comment records the measured divergence instead of asserting global neutrality.
  - **Structural probe 2091732 (`--local --padded`): inconclusive, OOM.** Fresh-init decomposition finite, 18 drift steps finite (loss 12.600 declining ~1e-4/step), then SIGKILL at 227.4 GB MaxRSS with linear RSS growth of ~10 GB per drift step (cause undiagnosed; known instrument limitation, parked). Weight-independence of the singularity is unresolved; the ablation round proceeds on the replay, which reproduces in ~10 minutes at 99 GB.
  **Why:** without the padded/unpadded reading, exit 3 plus an all-ok verdict table reads as a null result and the group as exonerated; recording the differential inverts that -- it is the sharpest localization to date (deterministic, same-state, single-variable) and redirects the ablation matrix onto the padded path. **NEXT (USER):** five ablation submissions on the padded replay, one seam per job (`ec_tail`, `both_eigh`, `sym_break`, `degeneracy_reg`, `zeta_eps`); a cured epoch (exit 0) names the mechanism and gates the Stage-3 shared-helper fix (PW92: PRB 45, 13244; DFS: PRB 104, L161109); five persisting exit-3 verdicts make the padding pass itself the prime suspect, in which case decompose() gains `pad_target` so the failing graph is decomposed directly and the padded-vs-unpadded parity suite gains a bh76 group containing OH and H.

## Phase 33 -- ablation verdict names the mechanism: SYM_BREAK_SHIFT raised 1e-8 -> 1e-6 (2026-07-20)

- 2026-07-20 `4059768eb` (results-affecting numerical fix; closes the polarized-correlation NaN that has gated the dfs6311 production resubmit since Phase 29) -- the five single-seam ablation replays of the padded spec_0021 epoch (one toggle per job, submitted per the Phase-32 plan) returned a clean split: `SYM_BREAK_SHIFT` 1e-8->1e-6 alone CURED the step-4 `bh76:OH+N2_to_H+N2O` gradient NaN (job 2092200: exit 0, full epoch, step-4 loss 1.085617e-2 unchanged to all seven printed digits), the combined eigh toggle containing it also cured (2092199, exit 0), and the three remaining seams all left the NaN in place -- the `ec_base` tail stop-gradient freeze (2092198, exit 3; the leading tail candidate from Phase 31 is thereby refuted as the driver), `DEGENERACY_REG` 1e-10->1e-8 (2092201, exit 3; consistent, since a uniform overlap shift commutes through eigh and opens no gaps), and the zeta boundary eps (2092202, exit 3). **Mechanism:** the eigh reverse-mode rule carries 1/(lambda_i-lambda_j) factors, and the eigenvector backward amplifies matrix-level round-off eps by eps/gap^2. The graded symmetry-breaking diagonal (`_sym_break_diag`, magnitude `SYM_BREAK_SHIFT`) floors the gaps of symmetry-exact degeneracies, and at 1e-8 that floor gives eps/gap^2 = 2.2 -- order unity -- so the graph-order round-off differences introduced by the shape-padding pass turned the backward into non-finite garbage on the padded graph while the unpadded evaluation of the identical state stayed finite (the Phase-32 differential); at 1e-6 the ratio is 2.2e-4. The prior in-code justification for 1e-8 ("comfortably above float64 accumulation noise ~1e-13") compared against forward accumulation error and ignored the backward amplification. **Fix:** `solver.py` `SYM_BREAK_SHIFT = 1e-6`, centered in the admissible window [4.7e-7, 3e-6] whose lower wall is eps/gap^2 <= 1e-3 and whose upper walls are the 3e-5 orientation-lock splitting (production `inputs.orientation_lock_strength`; the quasi-random diagonal must not rival the deliberate degenerate-pair selection) and the Weyl bound (each eigenvalue moves by at most the diagonal's max element, measured 9.99e-7). New `tests/test_sym_break_shift.py` (regression batch 3) enforces the window, the value, the degeneracy-splitting floor, the Weyl bound, and both gradient regimes; against the prior constant the value pin, the amplification bound (2.2 > 1e-3), and the bounded-gradient ceiling (measured 1.6e8 > 1e8, the marginal regime itself) fail, and the unshifted-pathology and invariant-bound tests pass at either value by construction. Callers were checked by measurement: the oneshot fixed-density path does not route through the perturbed eigh (energy delta exactly 0, so the 1e-12 golden tests are structurally immune), a converged closed-shell full SCF moves by 2.4e-13 Ha, no test compares a converged manual/pyscfad SCF energy to an external reference at a tolerance the shift could reach, and no spec/checkpoint serializes the constant. The stale `O(1e-8)`/`~2e-10` bias figures in `solver_manual.py`'s two Roothaan-step comments were refreshed to the Weyl statement. Reference caches produced under the 1e-8 solver carry at most a ~1e-6 Ha inconsistency against the new one -- three orders below the orientation lock and six below the trained loss scale -- so regeneration is optional and existing checkpoints remain valid. **Why:** the constant was the single knob the on-cluster A/B isolated, the replacement value is fixed by a two-sided sizing argument rather than by the incident alone, and the cure was demonstrated on the exact failing production replay before the constant was changed in source. **NEXT (USER):** rsync `xcquinox/alec/solver.py`, `xcquinox/alec/solver_manual.py`, `xcquinox/alec/tests/test_sym_break_shift.py`, `hpcjobs/alec_regression.sbatch` (+ the Phase-32 comment-only `hpcjobs/dfs6311_nan_isolate.{py,sbatch}`); resubmit `alec_regression.sbatch` and the default no-ablation `dfs6311_nan_isolate.sbatch` replay -- the latter ran exit 3 at 1e-8 and must now run exit 0, the cure-at-source confirmation; with both green the production dfs6311 resubmit is unblocked (it was gated on this NaN only).
- 2026-07-20 (confirmation banked from jobs 2092238/2092239) -- both confirmation runs returned green on the synced tree: the regression (2092238, `COMPLETED 0:0`) reported `25 passed, 1 deselected` / `71 passed, 1 skipped, 2 xfailed` / `98 passed` (batch 3 grew by exactly the seven new sym-break tests) / `28 passed`, `ALL BATCHES PASSED`; the no-ablation padded replay (2092239, `COMPLETED 0:0`, `ablate=none`) passed the previously fatal step -- `step 4 bh76:OH+N2_to_H+N2O: loss=1.085617e-02 grads finite`, the same group and the same loss to all seven printed digits that produced the all-leaf NaN under 1e-8 earlier the same day (2091734, exit 3) -- and finished `epoch 0 completed with no non-finite gradient`. Identical spec, identical padding, identical schedule; the only change between the exit-3 and exit-0 runs is commit `4059768eb`. The polarized-correlation NaN open since Phase 29 is closed; the production dfs6311 resubmit is unblocked.

## Phase 34 -- dfs6311 OOM wall closed: gc cadence and glibc arena cap productionized from the three-probe evidence chain (2026-07-23)

- 2026-07-23 `8d158816b` (memory-management fix for the production OOM epidemic; results-neutral -- collection and allocator policy, not arithmetic) -- the padded 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3 production sweep (`run_20260720T181059Z`) hit an OOM wall: 7 of 11 deep_3x16 subset sizes SIGKILLed at 212-220 GB MaxRSS on 251.6 GB nodes (2 completed, 2 cancelled in flight), and even survivors peaked at 173-187 GB; deaths correlated with the number of reaction groups (one optimizer update per group per epoch) and with the padded bh76 groups' large per-update footprint, consistent with uniform per-update retention rather than any group-specific defect. A three-probe chain on the fastest-dying cell (`spec_0003`, deep_3x16, subset_size 4, 10 updates/epoch) isolated two stacked mechanisms:
  - **v1 (job 2093006, instrumented rerun):** the recompile-ratchet hypothesis was refuted -- 939 compile records, all in the epoch-1 warm-up, zero after -- yet RSS climbed ~1.6 GB/update (27 -> 184 GB over 100 updates) on warm kernels to SIGKILL at epoch ~17 (221 GB): retention of buffers, not kernels. The Phase-32 parking of the structural probe's ~10 GB/step growth as a possible instrument artifact is REVERSED -- that growth was the same real retention, not an instrument effect.
  - **v2 (job 2093139, + gc.collect per update + live-array census):** the cadence ARRESTS the growth -- RSS stationary near 200 GB over the final ~30 epochs, no new high-water mark (219.4 GB) after epoch 34, lifetime 5.4x v1 -- at ~zero cost (2.56 vs 2.70 min/epoch). The eagerly differentiated per-update graph strands dead-but-cyclic reference islands holding GB-scale device buffers, and the generational collector, triggered by object counts rather than bytes, rarely fires in the array-dominated loop. Still SIGKILLed at epoch ~98 on a FLAT curve: with the live-buffer census PINNED at 23.49 GB across all 900 updates, ~88% of the ~200 GB resident set was C-level retention that no Python-side collection can return, and the ~200 GB floor plus a >= 19 GB per-update transient left no margin on the node.
  - **v3 (job 2093577, + MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2):** the C-level floor was glibc arena caching. Capping malloc arenas at 2 (default 8 x 96 cores = 768; freed heap parks on per-arena free lists instead of returning to the OS under the ~100-thread XLA/BLAS runtime) cut the floor from ~200 GB to ~86 GB (HWM 219.4 -> 108.0 GB; sacct MaxRSS 102 GB), the census stayed pinned at 23.49 GB (discriminating arena caching from XLA-internal retention), and the probe COMPLETED all 200 epochs at 2.40 min/epoch -- no contention tax from two arenas, loss converged to ~1e-10.
  **Fix:** both levers become production defaults. `train.py` `_GC_EVERY_DEFAULT = 1` -- the per_molecule loop collects after every optimizer update unless `XCQUINOX_GC_EVERY` overrides (explicit non-positive disables; unset or unparseable values take the default); `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` is exported in the three train-worker sbatch templates (`train_array_cpu`, `train_eval_inline_cpu`, and `preflight` -- the last so the compile-smoke gate measures a production-identical allocator), each with a startup echo so the lever's presence is provable from any job log. The export must precede interpreter start (glibc reads it at first malloc, so an in-process setenv is too late); the worker subprocess inherits it through `_train_task`'s full-environment spawn, and the compile-smoke probe through `os.environ` in `_preflight._compile_smoke_impl`. Stages that never run the train worker (datagen, pretrain, eval, benchmark_refs) and the unused GPU template deliberately keep glibc defaults pending their own measurements; the scoping is pinned by a render test. Tests: the parser default and override semantics, a default-path collection count over a real two-step per_molecule run, and template-render assertions (cap present on the train-worker path, absent elsewhere) -- the first two fail against the prior code and the third against the prior templates. `alec_regression.sbatch` gains a fifth batch (`test_train.py` + `test_cluster_submit.py`) so the cluster regression executes the training-loop and rendering suites.
  **Why:** the node ceiling is fixed at 251.6 GB and the baseline working sets are 100-190 GB, so the sweep only fits if the growth is arrested (gc cadence) AND the allocator floor is cut (arena cap); v3 measures the pair at ~86 GB floor / ~108 GB peak on the probe cell with zero throughput cost, converting the OOM epidemic into ~145 GB of headroom on the probe cell and restoring margin for the larger cells.
  **Side finding (recorded, deliberately not shipped here):** the unpadded validation path re-compiles ~10.3k tiny eager ops at EVERY validation boundary (10328/10322/10322/... across all eight validations of the v3 run; a bounded eager-dispatch cache evicts the validation kernels between boundaries), costing ~8 min per validation but memory-neutral under the gc cadence (RSS brackets -13.1 to +0.2 GB, never floor-raising). Padding the validation data with the training `pad_target` is the named throughput follow-up; it is NOT results-neutral a priori -- the Phase-32 differential measured a 0.219% padded-vs-unpadded forward-loss shift at identical parameters, so moving validation onto the padded path changes validation-loss values and with them early-stop / validation-best decisions, and it therefore requires its own controlled experiment rather than riding this change.
  **NEXT (USER):** rsync the changed files; resubmit `alec_regression.sbatch` (now five batches); FRESH production submit of `dfs_step7.dfs6311_grid3_v3.yaml` (`--partition long-96core`; datagen/pretrain/CCSD reference caches reuse by content; the two completed specs 0000/0006 remain valid for figures; the new-run train logs must open with `[malloc] MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`).

## Phase 35 -- c2 benchmark-reference grid drift: escalation-path grids made deterministic, DF identity stamped into the density-refs cache (2026-07-26)

- 2026-07-26 (physical/numerical artifact + reproducibility fix; results-contained -- the affected datapoint sits in the excluded validation slice, so no reported TEST metric changes) -- every completed task of the dfs6311 production sweep (`run_20260723T161502Z`) drops exactly one species from the held-out eval: `[precompute 1/1] c2: FAILED (external rho_ref_grid shape (26840,) does not match rho_grid shape (26568,))`, three times per task (final / train-best / val-best eval passes), 13/13 finished tasks, and already present in the 2 tasks of the reboot-killed `run_20260720T181059Z` that reached eval. Root cause (established by local reproduction, then confirmed against the cluster cache):
  - pyscf density-prunes the DFT grid ONCE, at the first SCF cycle, on the density held at that moment (`small_rho_cutoff` = 1e-7). The eval-side consumer (`data.py` precompute, plain `kernel()`) therefore always stores/compares on the minao-guess-pruned grid -- 26568 points for c2 at 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3, reproduced exactly under pyscf 2.9.0 (the parity pin) and 2.11.0 (raw unpruned count 28240; version and geometry vectors eliminated -- the c2 W4-11 coordinates are byte-identical since their introduction).
  - the reference generator (`external_refs.run_scf_with_cache`) also opens with a plain `kernel()`, but on non-convergence `_converge_scf_tiered` rebuilds a FRESH mean-field seeded `dm0=make_rdm1()`, and THAT object's grid is pruned on the non-converged 50-cycle DIIS density. The stored `grid_coords`/`grid_weights` came from the escalated object, and stage 2 (`run_ccsd_with_cache`) evaluates the CCSD density on the stored grid verbatim -- so the final npz inherits the drifted grid. Pruning c2 on non-converged densities lands at 26840/26848/26856 points depending on DF/lock configuration (basin-sensitive: the c2 PBE DIIS oscillates between two SCF solutions ~0.1 Ha apart, newton E=-75.8168 vs shifted-DIIS E=-75.7158), versus 26568 on the guess -- the stored 26840 is one draw from that neighborhood.
  - c2 is the ONLY pool species (of 214) whose plain PBE kernel fails to converge, hence the only drifted reference. Controls: n2 and f2 converge on the plain kernel and their cached grids (26616 and 26568) match their eval-side counts exactly.
  - cluster probe (`hpcjobs/dfs6311_c2_ref_probe.py`, read-only): `c2.npz` carries `rho_ref_grid (26840,)`, stamps grid_level 3 / basis 6-311++G(3df,2pd) / lock 3e-5, DF-named intermediates (`c2_g3_..._df_ol3e-05_{scf,ccsd}.npz`), all generated in the same 2026-07-06 wave as its siblings (c2 06:41, co 08:29, f2 08:36, n2 09:35) -- a code-path asymmetry within one wave, not a stale-era survivor. `_benchmark_npz_is_complete` cannot detect the drift (it checks basis/grid-level/lock identity, and the loader's `grid_level_used` gate passes at 3==3), so every subsequent wave SKIPped the file as complete; deletion alone would NOT fix it, since regeneration under the same code re-enters the escalation and stores another drifted grid.
  **Fix:** `run_scf_with_cache` captures `payload_grids = mf.grids` immediately after the first kernel attempt and stores its `n_grid`/`grid_coords`/`grid_weights` regardless of which convergence tier ultimately succeeds -- generation and consumption are now grid-identical by construction (converged dm/MOs/e_tot still come from the converged tier; they are basis-space objects, independent of the stored grid). Detection signal for any recurrence stays fail-loud: the loader's shape check. Hardening from the same investigation: the density-refs cache identity now includes density fitting -- `generate_one` stamps `density_fit_used` into the final npz and `_benchmark_npz_is_complete` rejects a stamped mismatch (absent stamp = tolerant, so the 213 valid existing refs are not invalidated); `data._ALLOWED_EXTERNAL_KEYS` admits the stamp. Tests (each fails against the prior code): a forced-escalation seam test pinning the stored grid to the first attempt's size; DF-identity accept/reject/legacy-tolerant predicate cases; a generate_one stamp + DF-flip regeneration case; loader acceptance of the stamp. Verified: 36 passed (`test_benchmark_refs.py` + `test_orientation_lock.py`), 8 passed (`test_external_refs.py -k "escalation or run_scf"`), 19 passed (`test_data.py -k external`).
  **Why:** a reference cache whose grid depends on the generation-time SCF trajectory is irreproducible by construction -- the same species regenerated on a different day (different escalation outcome, different DF setting) yields a different grid and silently invalidates or re-breaks downstream density metrics; pinning the stored grid to the guess-pruned one makes the cache deterministic and consumer-matched, and the DF stamp closes the remaining silent-reuse channel. The c2 datapoint itself is expendable for the current sweep (it lives in the 49-reaction validation slice, excluded from every reported TEST CSV; 0 NaN-drops in all reported sets), which is why the remedy can wait for the sweep.
  **NEXT (USER), gated on sweep completion (in-flight-safety -- the running array reads this cache at eval time):** rsync `xcquinox/alec/external_refs.py`, `xcquinox/alec/benchmark_refs.py`, `xcquinox/alec/data.py` (+ tests, + `hpcjobs/dfs6311_c2_ref_probe.py`); delete `c2.npz` and the two `_intermediates/c2_g3_*_df_ol3e-05_{scf,ccsd}.npz` files under `external_refs_bench_6311ppg3df2pd_g3`; let the pending benchmark_refs job (2094568, or a resubmit) regenerate c2 under the fixed grid semantics; the regenerated file must show `rho_ref_grid (26568,)` via the probe. Re-running the three eval passes for the 13 finished specs afterwards is optional (TEST metrics unaffected); the val-slice per-reaction records gain the c2 atomization datapoint from then on.
- 2026-07-26 (deployment record + operational hardening) -- the c2 remedy was deployed ahead of a full harness restart: the fixed modules were rsynced; a dedicated single-species job (2099698, `hpcjobs/dfs6311_c2_ref_regen.sbatch`, `--species-slice` targeting c2 at index 62 of the sorted 214-species pool) regenerated c2 into a staging directory in 10.2 s (COMPLETED 0:0; an initial failure report was a squeue-absence misread -- the job had simply finished between two queue samples; sacct + the job log are the only valid outcome oracles); the staged file was swapped into the production cache with a single atomic rename after probe verification, the drifted final npz and both drifted intermediates retired to `_retired_20260726/`. Post-swap probe: c2 `rho_ref_grid (26568,)` with `density_fit_used` stamped, n2 `(26616,)` / f2 `(26568,)` untouched. The regen sbatch was subsequently aligned with the proven in-repo job-script conventions (no `set -e` -- conda activation may return non-zero on success, so activation is verified by the resolved interpreter path instead; `${SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK:-24}` fallback; `--exclusive --mem=0`; an explicit rc gate with a named `[c2-regen] FATAL` line after every stage so any death names its own stage in the log; out-dir as an optional first argument for staging-vs-in-place use), exercised across an environment-permutation harness (activation returning non-zero on success, unset/garbage SLURM variables, missing interpreter, per-stage failures, argument quoting), and the probe's footer now states both reference states (drifted vs healthy) instead of only the drifted one. **Why:** the first version of the job script bypassed the review discipline applied to library code and deviated from the working scripts' shell conventions; the conventions exist because environment seams (activation return codes, unset scheduler variables) are exactly where job scripts die undiagnosably.
