- 2026-07-26 `4eff6be78` (figure tooling, scaffolding) -- the cross-basis comparison figure gained an architecture filter (`--comparison-archs`, `basis_comparison_focus*` outputs; full-union files preserved): the full union spans ~102 arch x subset columns (12854 px, unreadable), the focused deep/deep_attn pair renders at 3604 px. Empty or all-unknown filters fail loud; partially-unknown names are reported and skipped. 117 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).

## Phase 36 -- DFS Eq. 21 combined energy-density figure for the ablation suite (2026-07-28)

- 2026-07-28 (figure tooling; results-additive -- a new derived-metric figure/CSV, no existing metric changed) -- the per-basis ablation suite gains the DFS combined energy-density metric: `ablation_combined_energy_density.png` + `.csv` in every `figures_dfs_step7_<alias>[_val_best]/` dir, computed from the pulled held-out eval JSON (the per-molecule `density_rmse`/`density_rmse_pbe` columns and the per-reaction energy errors; no new cluster output needed). ED follows Dick & Fernandez-Serra (PRB 104, L161109 (2021)) Eq. 21 -- the harmonic mean of an energy error and a density error rescaled to kcal/mol, ED = 2/(1/E + 1/(gamma*D)) -- with three documented deviations, each stamped on the figure: the headline energy leg is the suite's 2-subset WTMAD-2 (BH76+W4-11 labeled reweighting, not the Letter's diet-GMTKN55-150), with the plain combined reaction MAE as a second leg whose Spearman rank agreement with the headline is printed on the figure (leg-independence check); the density leg is the eval pipeline's grid-weight-averaged RMSE vs CCSD (not the Letter's per-electron L1, Eq. 20 -- SI Sec. VI reports the ranking largely independent of the density-metric choice, and its L2 variant, SI Eq. 8, correlates best with WTMAD-2 at R^2 = 0.98); and gamma is self-calibrated per leg from the pooled PBE anchors (gamma = E_PBE/D_PBE, so ED_PBE == E_PBE by construction) because the Letter's gamma = 1084.87 kcal/mol is the regression slope against its per-electron L1 error and is dimensionally wrong against a grid-weighted RMSE. Semantics match the dfs_selfconsistent_density notebook's `combined_energy_density` (reimplemented in the figure script; the notebook module's import chain is too heavy for a plotting-only script). Cells lacking either leg -- or keyed but non-finite in one -- are excluded and named on the figure; the PBE density anchor deduplicates molecules across specs, uses finite rows only, and a divergence between the anchor's molecule set and the NN density union (possible under a run-level `pbe_density_errors.json` wider than the NN eval) is stamped as a warning rather than silently averaged; the middle panel decomposes each cell into (E, gamma*D) with iso-ED harmonic contours, so energy-limited and density-limited cells separate visually. On the current pulls (val-best): dfs6311_grid3_v3 10/13 cells beat PBE on the WTMAD-2-leg ED (leg agreement rho = 0.73 over 13 cells), svp_grid2_v3 27/73 (rho = 0.88 over 73); both anchor sets verified identical to the NN unions (198/198), so the warnings are silent on all current data. Tests: hand-computed gamma arithmetic and the ED_PBE == E_PBE identity, harmonic-mean guards, molecule-dedup, exclusion and anchor-coverage rules, CSV schema, render + suite-gating canaries; 131 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`), each new test failing against the prior code. Correctness was established against three independent oracles: the notebook implementation on identical inputs (exact agreement, including 500 randomized cases), the two PDFs (every quoted equation, constant, and R^2), and a from-scratch recomputation of all 26 dfs6311 CSV cells from the raw pulled JSONs (agreement at 1e-9 on every field). The pull-and-figures runbook gains the dfs6311_grid3_v3 category row, the `_grid3` alias note, and the ED outputs.
  **Why:** the DFS paper's central claim is that a functional must be judged jointly on energies and densities; the suite carried both legs as separate figures but never combined them, so beats-PBE statements could not be made in the paper's own metric. Validation-time density persistence was confirmed unnecessary for this: every eval pass (in-sample, final, train-best, val-best held-out) already stores the per-molecule density-error scalars vs the CCSD benchmark references, which is exactly what Eq. 21 consumes.

- 2026-07-29 (figure tooling + docs; results-additive -- new composite views + a reader's guide, no metric changed) -- two overview composites join every per-basis figure set: `ablation_density_energy_overview.png` (held-out: per-pool WTMAD-2 bars whose single-pool form is stamped as the one-bucket reduction 56.84*MAD_pool/mean|ref|_pool -- a scaled relative error, not a reweighting -- plus the genuine 2-subset WTMAD-2, the held-out density panels, and the Eq. 21 ED headline with an "ED unavailable" placeholder when the anchors are missing; each energy panel carries its own pool-filtered PBE baseline) and `ablation_insample_overview.png` (in-sample AE MAE bars + per-molecule |AE| strip + the density panels, with three stamped disclosures: final checkpoint only, no in-sample PBE AE baseline, no in-sample ED -- eval/ has no val-best variant, per_molecule.json no PBE AE column, and gamma no PBE energy anchor to self-calibrate against). The shared panels were extracted to module-level ax-taking helpers (the make_cluster_pulls_figure plot_composite pattern) -- `_grouped_arch_bars`, `_ed_lines_panel`, the in-sample/held-out density panel bodies, and a new `_insample_ae_strip_panel` -- with output identity verified by pixel-diff on the regenerated svp_grid2_v3 val-best set (four refactored figures, zero differing pixels); plot_holdout_density_ccsd's inline PBE map was unified onto `_pbe_density_map` (duplicate logic). `README_density_figures.md` decodes every panel, marker, footer band, and all 14 ED CSV columns with file:line anchors, states the metric definitions and the three documented deviations from the Letter, and fixes the reading order (beats-PBE claims belong to the ED cells and WTMAD-2 panels; in-sample figures support training-fit statements only). The runbook outputs list names both composites and points at the guide. Tests: single-pool WTMAD-2 reduction arithmetic (hand-computed against the two-pool form), the pbe_line=None bar-panel guard, AE-strip data handling, both overview render canaries incl. the ED placeholder, and the builder-contract updates (writes-three -> writes-four; holdout-emission set now 7 names); 136 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).

Every held-out figure now carries a dataset footer line (`_holdout_eval_note` + an opt-in `dataset` slot in the footer stamper, default None so all other figures are pixel-unchanged -- verified) stating what the held-out eval is with live counts; on the current pulls that line reads "BH76 52 + W4-11 113 reactions" -- the per_reaction files carry the run's TEST slice, not the full 216-reaction canonical pool, a fact previously visible only in the code. RESULTS observation recorded from the same session's raw-JSON check: at 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3 the two architectures evaluated so far do NOT beat PBE on held-out densities (paired per-species win rates 13% deep_3x16 / 6% deep_attn_3x16 against the parity-panel pairing, i.e. the per-molecule-pooled PBE baseline; best-cell median NN/PBE = 1.040; in-sample per-arch pooled medians 1.13 / 1.20, per-cell medians 1.06-1.34) while beating it clearly on both energy pools, so the Eq. 21 ED still favors the NN at 10/13 cells -- an energy-for-density trade, not a plotting artifact. PBE's own density error at this basis (pool mean 2.298e-4) is half its def2-svp value (4.978e-4), so the density bar is higher; on svp the density-oriented architectures do beat PBE (deep_combined_3x16: ~59% per-species win rate; best-cell median NN/PBE 0.92 under the parity-panel pairing, 0.94 under within-row pairing -- the spread is real, not a computation defect: that run predates the orientation lock, and 12/198 species' model-free PBE density references vary across spec evals by up to an order of magnitude (mean-relative spread), led by the BH76 transition state RKT14 and the degenerate-state species bn3pi/clo/no/of/oh/ch, whereas the locked dfs6311 run shows zero cross-spec spread). Exactly the density-oriented architectures (cusp/combined/rung35 families) are the dfs6311 specs still training in run_20260727T014146Z.

- 2026-07-29 (figure tooling + docs; results-additive -- self-description completed across the figure suite) -- the dataset footer line now covers the ENERGY figures too: the stamper-based ones (rung summary, combined-MAE/WTMAD-2, the five parity variants, the size-consistency diagnostic) carry it as a dedicated line, and the five bespoke-footer figures (parity, heatmap, MAE-by-arch, MAE-vs-subset, AE parity) carry the reactions clause appended to their grey provenance line; energy figures state the reactions clause only (their panels show no density). The full-pool PBE/SCAN baselines in `provenance_footer` are now labeled "(full pool)" -- those MAEs are computed on the full 216-entry canonical pool while the NN cells are scored on the test slice, and the label closes the last spot where full-pool numbers read as slice numbers. `HOLDOUT_SET.md` enumerates the held-out set's exact constituents, extracted from the canonical pool builder and the pulled run: 216 reaction entries = 212 unique names (4 BH76 forward/reverse-barrier name pairs listed explicitly), split into the 165-name test slice (52 BH76 + 113 W4-11; 167 entries) and the 47-name validation slice (20 + 27; 49 entries) with zero overlap; 214 pool species = 198 density species + 15 atoms skipped by design + c2 (absent from this run's evals per the Phase 35 reference-grid drift, restored for future evals); full per-reaction tables with reference energies and the complete density-species inventory; sources quoted from the pool entries (BH76: Goerigk et al., PCCP 19, 32184 (2017); W4-11: Karton, Daon, Martin, CPL 510, 165 (2011)). Tests: full-pool label pinned, energy-figure dataset acceptance/render, reactions-only eval-note branch; 141 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).
  **Why:** after the density figures gained the dataset line, the energy family was the remaining place where "held-out" was undefined on the figure itself and where full-pool PBE baselines could be misread as test-slice values; and the dataset line's counts ("BH76 52 + W4-11 113") beg the question of WHICH reactions -- HOLDOUT_SET.md is the durable name-by-name answer, so the split never has to be reverse-engineered from the harness again.
  **Follow-up analysis of the density shortfall (recorded here; every claim source-quoted):** the held-out density deficit vs PBE traces to three verified training deviations from the Letter, compounded by coverage and distribution. (1) Loss weighting: the Letter hard-codes "lambda_RE = 1, lambda_n = 20, and lambda_E = 0.01" (main text, after Eq. 18) -- an explicit 20x thumb on the density scale -- whereas the dfs_step7 loss (`L5_gradnorm_vxc_step7`) runs five GradNorm-balanced task channels with a density pre-scale of 0.1 and, per its own docstring, "lets GradNorm ... discover task weights adaptively rather than hard-coding any fixed scaling"; GradNorm equalizes training rates and has no mechanism to reproduce the Letter's deliberate density prioritization. (2) Model selection: the Letter's validation loss is "identical to the training loss presented in the main text except for lambda_E = 0 and w_j = delta_j,25" (SI) -- density included at weight 20 in every checkpoint decision -- while the harness's validation-best selects on reaction-energy MAE alone (the validation species deliberately carry no density references); measured cost on dfs6311: median-of-medians NN/PBE density ratio 1.066 (final-step) -> 1.112 (val-best), worse in 4/13 cells, better in 3, unchanged in 6 (the val-best checkpoint coincides with the final model in the tied cells). (3) SCF depth: the Letter trains at 25 cycles with w_j = ((j-10)/15)^2 and validates at the 25th step; the v3 sweeps run max_cycles 3 (eval rows: cycles_run 3 on 97% of rows, scf_converged false on 89%); the svp full25 control improves deep_3x16 from 1.084 to 1.047 median-of-medians -- real but not sufficient alone. Compounding: only the GGA-family architectures have evaluated on dfs6311 (the Letter's functional is a meta-GGA; on svp the richer deep_combined already beats PBE densities), and the evaluation distributions differ -- the Letter's density errors (Fig. 2 center panel, Table I eps_n) are computed "across the G2/97 dataset" from a 21-molecule training set (SI Sec. II: 10 linear closed-shell + 3 linear open-shell + 8 non-linear molecules), so its density improvement IS a genuine out-of-training-set result (~86% of the evaluated species were untrained) but stays within the equilibrium-molecule family, whereas this suite's density panel is the BH76+W4-11 species set (transition states and radical-heavy atomization species, a harder shift) against a PBE baseline that is twice as tight at 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3 as at def2-svp. (The Letter itself scaled lambda_n by 0.01 for CH and OH -- two of the degenerate-state radicals behind the pre-lock PBE-reference spread above.) Named follow-up experiment, post-run and in-flight-safe: an A/B raising the density channel toward the Letter's weighting (fixed lambda_n rather than GradNorm for the rho channel, or a rho-inclusive validation metric), swept as a new YAML, never the running array.

Tests: species-pool mapping incl. overlap, per-channel gamma independence and None-degradation, both render canaries, collector species keys, and the builder-emission update (10 names with density; the 3x3 CSV, like the ED CSV, is written alongside and never returned); 146 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).

- 2026-07-29 (figure tooling; results-additive -- a count-column fix and gamma made self-explanatory on the figures) -- the per-channel 3x3 CSV's `n_reactions`/`n_density_species` columns were blank: `write_combined_ed_csv` computed the per-leg count override into locals but the row writer still read the flat maps (which the 3x3 path passes empty). Fixed (the writer now consumes the override; the writer fix and its biting counts-value test shipped in `f58d783d6`), and the six on-disk 3x3 CSVs rewritten through the wiring path -- all 663 rows, including counts (dfs6311 val-best: BH76 68 species/cell with 558 reaction rows, W4-11 113/1398, combined 198/1956), reproduced from the raw JSONs independently. Separately, gamma is now defined where it is used, not just valued: the ED line panels' and the enriched decomposition's stamps read "gamma = E_PBE/D_PBE = <value> (self-calibrated)" (the overview's compact decomposition panel carries no gamma value and defers to the caveat), the 3x3 and held-out-overview caveats carry the full ED = 2/(1/E + 1/(gamma*D)) form with the per-channel/pooled gamma definition, the ED caveat names gamma as "the Letter's regression slope, calibrated here on PBE alone", and the reader's guide spells out the construction: the E and D inside each cell's ED are the network's own reference errors; gamma is the Letter's cross-functional zero-intercept regression slope (six nonempirical functionals, 1084.87 kcal/mol in its per-electron-L1 units), degenerate here to a one-point PBE calibration because the stored density scalar is a grid-weighted RMSE in different units; gamma = E_NN/D_NN is impossible (it collapses ED to the energy error for every network). 146 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).
  **Why:** the panel titles said "(own gamma)" and the stamps printed a bare number -- a reader could not tell from the figure what kind of object gamma is or why it is PBE-anchored, and the count columns of a paper-facing CSV must not ship blank.

- 2026-07-29 (eval pipeline + calibration path; results-additive -- new emitted columns and conditional CSV legs, every existing column and figure byte-identical) -- the eval now emits the Letter's Eq. 20 density error exactly: `density_eps_l1 = sum_i(w_i |rho - rho_ref|_i) / N_e` with `N_e = sum_i(w_i rho_ref_i)` (quadrature electron count of the REFERENCE density, charge-correct by construction; `density_eps_terms` / `pbe_density_eps` in `evaluation.py`), its model-free PBE twin `density_eps_l1_pbe`, and the `n_electrons` / `grid_weight_sum` bookkeeping that makes any offline renormalization reconstructible -- alongside, never replacing, the grid-weighted RMSE (kept per the SI Sec. VI metric-independence note and for continuity with every prior pull). The columns flow through `DensityRMSEMetric` (in-sample `eval/per_molecule.json`), the held-out record path (`_DENSITY_RECORD_KEYS` / `density_errors_for_record` / `make_per_molecule_record`), and the run-level PBE table (`reeval_holdout_fixed.run_pbe_density_table`, both the stored-`rho_pbe_grid` fast path and the SCF fallback); atoms and refs-free species carry the same all-None schema as before. Backfill machinery: `reeval_holdout_fixed.py` gains `--checkpoint {model,model_best,model_val_best}` (weights file -> the matching `eval_holdout[_best|_val_best]/` subdir, each with an independent idempotency stamp -- so the val-best artifacts the figures plot can be re-evaled without touching the final-checkpoint ones), and the density stamp bumped to `+density_refs_v4` so v3-stamped specs re-process to gain the eps columns. Calibration data path (`precompute_nonempirical_pool.py` + `hpcjobs/nonempirical_pool.sbatch`, submitted after the live sweep completes): converged KS-SCF total energies and Eq. 20 eps for the Letter's six nonempirical calibration functionals (PW91, PBE, TPSS, revTPSS, SCAN, PBE0) over the full held-out pool at the run's basis/grid/DF settings against the SAME CCSD reference densities -- reusing `run_scf_with_cache` (xc-tagged cache identities; the out-dir must be fresh, since legacy pre-e_tot PBE intermediates load with `e_tot=None` and fail loudly), the SCFs carrying the reference generation's orientation-lock strength (3e-05 for the dfs6311 refs; an unlocked SCF against a locked reference would enter the degenerate-state radicals' orientation artifact into eps as if it were functional error), each reference's identity stamps (basis / grid / DF / lock) verified per species via the generator's own `_benchmark_npz_is_complete` predicate, densities evaluated on the stored first-attempt grid with a shape gate against the reference grid (a mismatch is a per-species failure, never a silent skip -- the Phase 35 c2 lesson), both cache layers resumable with atomic JSON writes. Figure side: `gamma_zero_intercept` (the Letter's Fig. 3 zero-intercept regression, sum(eps*W)/sum(eps^2)), `_wtmad2_from_energies` (per-functional 2-subset WTMAD-2 over the canonical pool via the validated `per_reaction_errors`), `nonempirical_gamma` (cache -> per-functional (eps, WTMAD-2) pairs -> fitted slope; eps averaged over the COMMON species intersection and energies restricted to the common energy-species set, so a partially-filled cache cannot bias the slope by coverage -- the intersection size and the dropped-species count are returned and printed), and `combined_ed_fixed_gamma` (Eq. 21 with an EXTERNAL gamma, so ED_PBE != E_PBE and PBE lands off the y=x locus by its displacement from the calibration trend; summaries carry `gamma_mode`, and the ED panels branch their stamps and PBE labels on it so a fixed-gamma summary never renders the self-calibration claims). When a pull carries the eps columns, `ablation_combined_energy_density.csv` gains the `wtmad2_eps_gamma_dfs` leg (gamma = the published 1084.87 kcal/mol, dimensionally valid now that D is in the Letter's per-electron L1 units) and, when the calibration cache sits in the run dir, `wtmad2_eps_gamma_fit` (gamma refit by the Letter's procedure on our axes); the eps legs run the same coverage disclosures as the RMSE legs (missing-cell listing against the RMSE channel and the eps-channel PBE-anchor-vs-NN-union check, so a partial backfill is named rather than shipped silently); pulls without the columns produce byte-identical CSVs, and the self-calibrated relative-to-PBE legs remain the headline. Correctness fixes landed by the pre-commit review passes, all in this change: the calibration script's density recipe passed an ase.Atoms object where pyscf expects the geometry string (every non-PBE functional leg would have failed after its SCF completed; fixed to the MoleculeSpec pyscf-format geometry and pinned by a no-stubs test running the real H2 SCAN chain end-to-end, density integrating to N_e = 2), `needs_reeval` no longer lets a refs-free re-run churn -- and schema-downgrade -- a density-stamped spec (a `<version>+density_refs_*` stamp satisfies the base version), and the `n_electrons`/`grid_weight_sum` bookkeeping is excluded from `aggregate.json` and the step-5 long form (they are quadrature bookkeeping, not error metrics). The runbook gains the deployment-gate section with the exact rsync/sbatch/backfill commands: `evaluation.py`/`eval_holdout.py` are live-imported by the running sweep's eval chain, so cluster deployment waits for run completion (else later specs' eval schema differs from earlier ones within one run). Tests (each new test failing against the prior code): closed-form eps oracles distinct from the weight-mean L1 (w = [3,1], rho_ref = [2,1] gives eps = 2/7 vs l1 = 1/2), a real-PBE-density N_e integral for H2O in [9.5, 10.5], skip-path schema carriage, holdout record passthrough, checkpoint-subdir isolation and stamp independence, v3-restamp and no-churn behavior, the real-seam H2 chain and the reference identity gate, hand-slope gamma regression, fixed-gamma ED hand values and stamp truthfulness, cache-driven gamma fits (equal and unequal support, malformed caches), the partial-backfill disclosure, and the conditional CSV-leg gate; 43 (`test_evaluation.py`) + 29 (`test_eval_holdout.py`) + 22 (`test_reeval_holdout_fixed.py`) + 8 (`test_precompute_nonempirical_pool.py`) + 156 (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`) passed.
  **Why:** the ED metric could previously be computed only in a self-calibrated gamma = E_PBE/D_PBE form, because the stored density scalar (grid-weighted RMSE, ~2.3e-4 at 6-311++G(3df,2pd)/grid3, ~5e-4 at def2-svp) is dimensionally incompatible with the Letter's published slope -- transplanting 1084.87 onto RMSE units would misplace PBE ~35x (dfs6311) or ~25x (svp) off its own calibration trend. Emitting eps in the Letter's exact Eq. 20 units, plus the six-functional own-axes refit, makes the METRIC identical to the Letter's and both gamma choices defensible; the numbers still live on this suite's held-out set (BH76+W4-11 test slice, CCSD references), not the Letter's G2/97 / diet-GMTKN55 axes, so same-footing means same construction, not literal Table-I comparability.

- 2026-07-29 (guards; results-neutral -- disclosure completeness + reference-cache protection) -- two closes from the deployment-safety review of the mid-run eval-module swap. (1) The within-cell density-species homogeneity check (`_density_cell_coverage_warning`) gained a channel selector and now runs on the Eq. 20 eps channel inside the DFS-units ED block -- the RMSE legs run three coverage disclosures (cell exclusion, cell-species homogeneity, PBE-anchor-vs-NN-union) but the eps legs previously ran only two, so the prior entry's "same coverage disclosures as the RMSE legs" held for two of three; the missing one is exactly the mode that breaks cell-to-cell comparability (a per-species partial backfill inflates one cell's mean eps while its neighbors stay put -- measured +39% on a constructed case -- where the other two modes shift all cells uniformly). Disclosure surface is the console line plus the CSV's per-leg `n_density_species` column; the eps legs render no figure, so there is no panel to stamp. Pinned by a dead-parameter-killing unit test and by build-site wiring tests for BOTH eps guards, each verified to fail when its block is deleted (the suite was previously green under block deletion). (2) The calibration sbatch's out-dir guard widened from path equality to normalized containment (`case "$OUT_REAL/" in "$REFS_REAL"/*`): an out-dir anywhere inside the refs dir would write the job's `_intermediates/` SCF-cache tree into the production reference cache. Verified over 70 path shapes: subdirectories, `./` segments, relative spellings, and symlinks into the refs tree are blocked; sibling dirs sharing the refs name as a string prefix remain allowed (the pattern anchors on the path separator) and glob metacharacters in the refs path match literally (quoted expansion). The queued calibration job runs the submit-time copy with the equality-only guard -- harmless, its out-dir is the disjoint default. 159 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`).
  **Why:** the deployment-safety review demonstrated the suite stayed green with either eps-guard block deleted and that a subdirectory out-dir silently evaded the refs-dir guard; a coverage disclosure that can vanish without a test failing is not a guarantee, and the reference cache's integrity was already the subject of the Phase 35 drift incident.

The eps coverage disclosures -- previously console-only because the eps legs reached no canvas -- are stamped into both figures' note band (a partially-covered pull names its missing cells on the figure), and the no-eps skip path now prints the suite's standard stale-file warning (the RMSE-channel ED skip already did; the eps gate previously skipped silently). `plot_combined_energy_density` gained panel-title/placeholder/figure-title overrides and `plot_ed_decomposition` a title override, defaults reproducing the historical figures byte-identically (12 shared artifacts byte-compared across old-schema and eps-present scenarios against the prior revision, stdout identical, after a same-revision determinism control); docstring absolutes that became conditionally false under a fixed gamma (PBE-on-y=x, ED_PBE == E_PBE) were qualified in the three panel bodies and the ED figure builder. README_density_figures.md (inventory + eps section) and the runbook (outputs list + Sec. 4) describe the figures and the stale-file behavior. Tests: build-site wiring (fixed-gamma summaries + on-figure note content + no-cache placeholder + fit-panel-with-cache), the old-pull no-op guard with the skip disclosure (gate mutants killed), real-render canaries for both variants, and the builder-emission update (12 names with density); 163 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`), the new tests failing against the prior code (the no-eps guard alone passes there by design -- it asserts absences the prior code also satisfies; its teeth were proven by gate mutants instead).
  **Why:** the DFS-units legs are the like-for-like ED comparison (the Letter's own gamma on the Letter's own density units), but a CSV column is not a reviewable claim -- the parity figure puts the published-gamma and own-axes views beside the self-calibrated headline with the coverage caveats stamped where the numbers are. That matters on the current partial pull: 14 of the 18 completed dfs6311 cells carry eps (the mid-run eval deployment's schema boundary landed at spec 4), backfill of the four pre-deployment specs is deliberately deferred to run completion, and the on-figure disclosure is the record of that gap in the meantime.

- 2026-08-02 (figure tooling; presentation-only on the 3x3 family + parity panels, no metric changed) -- three readability items from figure review of the DFS-units 3x3. (2) The DFS-units 3x3's ED row renders as the same grouped per-(arch, subset_size) bars as the WTMAD-2 row (`ed_as_bars`, default off -- the original 3x3 keeps the line panels shared with the dedicated ED figure), PBE dashed + beats-PBE marks included, with the gamma stated in each panel title ("gamma = 1084.87 kcal/mol, DFS published") and the fixed-external stamp in-panel; the stamp text moved to a shared `_gamma_stamp` helper so the lines and bars panels cannot fork their truthfulness contract (ED line panels output-identical, byte-checked). (3) Both 3x3 caveats are two-line, the first line defining the single-pool form explicitly: WTMAD-2 restricted to one pool collapses to 56.84*MAD_pool/mean|dE_ref|_pool -- the pool's mean abs deviation rescaled by its mean abs reference energy, a scaled relative error, not a cross-pool reweighting; the split also narrows the bbox-tight canvas (the widest single line shrank). Tests: square-limit pin on an outlier fixture, gamma-stamp branch pin, caveat-content pin (formula + published-gamma statement on the twin only), bars wiring + render canaries; 169 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`), the new tests failing against the prior code.

- 2026-08-02 (figure tooling; corrections from external review of the rendered twin -- one presentation defect, one gamma-selection defect) -- (1) The previous entry's parity centering was PER-PANEL: each 3x3 parity panel spanned its own channel's envelope, so the three frames differed and outlier-stretched channels (a few W4-11 species reach eps ~1.1e-1) still rendered with the bulk in a corner, defeating cross-channel comparison. `_density_parity_panel` now accepts an external square `limits` pair and `plot_density_energy_3x3` (original and twin) passes ONE row-wide envelope pooled over all species -- the three channel frames are identical and directly comparable; single-parity figures keep their own-data envelope. (2) The composite DFS-units twins (decomposition, overview E/F, 3x3 row 3) plotted ONLY the Letter's published gamma = 1084.87 while the own-axes six-functional calibration on this run's data -- gamma = 1158.34 kcal/mol, cache present -- appeared solely in the dedicated figure's fit panel and one CSV leg. The own-axes value was first re-derived independently (fresh implementation of the reaction energies, per-pool MAD/mean|ref|, 2-subset reweighting, eps means, and the zero-intercept slope, from the calibration JSON and the canonical reaction list alone): 1158.336985911894 against the pipeline's 1158.3369859118943, agreement to 1 ulp, all 216 reactions computable for all six functionals. The OPERATIVE gamma of every single-gamma DFS-units view is now the own-axes fit whenever the calibration cache resolves (the calibration performed on this data's axes), the published slope only as fallback; `combined_ed_fixed_gamma`/`channel_ed_summaries` carry an additive `gamma_source`, the in-panel stamp -- moved to the top-right of the ED panels -- names value AND source ("fixed: own-axes fit" / "fixed: DFS published"), the twin row-3 titles drop the value (the stamp is the label), and the 3x3 twin CSV carries both families (`<ch>_wtmad2_eps_gamma_dfs` and `_fit`). The dedicated parity figure keeps both gammas side by side. Under the operative fit the combined channel beats PBE in 13/14 covered cells (ED_PBE = 9.635) vs 12/14 under the published slope. Caveats/provenance/README/runbook defer the plotted value to the stamps and name both sources. Tests: no-cache fallback (operative = published, sourced), cache case (operative = fit on all four twin surfaces + both CSV families at their own gammas), stamp source branch and top-right placement, external-limits pin, row-share spy (three identical envelopes = the pooled envelope); 171 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`), the new and updated tests failing against the prior code.
  **Why:** the published slope was calibrated on the Letter's reaction set, basis, and reference level; with a full six-functional calibration on THIS data's axes in hand, plotting the transplanted constant as the single operative gamma understated the information available and quietly ignored the better-founded value -- the two differ by 6.8%, enough to move one combined-channel cell across the PBE line. Per-panel parity envelopes looked like a fix but broke the one property a channel-decomposed row must have: a shared frame.

- 2026-08-03 (figure tooling; the 3x3 family goes all-bars, the parity row becomes its own figure, figure notation follows the Letter) -- both 3x3 figures (RMSE original and DFS-units twin) now render NINE bar panels: row 2 replaces the per-species parity scatter with per-channel DENSITY-ERROR bars -- the cell-mean error on the figure's density channel (grid-weighted RMSE / the Eq. 20 per-electron L1) restricted to the channel's species, PBE dashed at the channel's deduplicated per-molecule anchor, beats-PBE marks meaning lower error than PBE -- so the density story reads in the same categorical form as the energy and combined rows it sits between, and the bar heights equal the ED legs' D column in the companion CSVs (machine-readable already). Row 3 is bars unconditionally (the flag is gone; the lines panel remains for the dedicated/overview figures). The parity view is promoted to standalone figures, `ablation_density_parity_by_channel[_dfs_units].png`: three channel panels in ONE shared square frame (the row-envelope machinery moved there; a bh76-only-outlier fixture keeps pooled != per-channel so the share stays test-distinguishable). Figure notation now matches the Letter, verified against the PDF before any label was written: the combined metric renders as the CALLIGRAPHIC ED -- generic on the self-calibrated figures, ED_{|n|} on the eps-leg figures, exactly the symbol of Eq. 21, Table I, and Fig. 2's ylabel -- and the density error as varepsilon_{|n|} with its defining integral (Eq. 20, E[(1/N_e) int|n - n_ref|]) spelled out in the eps caveats alongside the quadrature form implemented (stated per species; Eq. 20's dataset expectation is carried by the cell-mean clauses); equation NUMBERS remain citations -- every rendered axis, title, and provenance line now uses the symbols, with "Eq. 20"/"Eq. 21" appearing only as parenthetical citations; CSV column names keep the ASCII schema. Tests: density-bar values pinned per channel against the fixture (maps + anchors, nine bar panels counted), the standalone's shared-frame pin plus a zero-parity-calls-from-the-3x3 pin, notation-symbol pins (glyphs, caveat equations, ylabel), emission set 16; 173 passed (`test_make_ablation_arch_figure.py`), the new and updated tests failing against the prior code.
  **Why:** a scatter row between two bar rows made the 3x3 read as three different figures stacked; with the density errors as categorical bars the three questions the figure answers -- who wins on energy, who wins on density, who wins jointly -- have the same visual grammar, and the parity detail keeps its own full-size canvas instead of a squeezed row. The notation change removes a translation step between the figures and the Letter: a reader holding both sees the same symbols denote the same quantities.

- 2026-08-03 (documentation + ERRATUM + observability; results-neutral -- no training behavior changed) -- `notebooks/analysis/LOSS_PRIMER.md` is now the canonical description of the training objective and of every weighting decision between the sweep YAML and the optimizer step, written after a line-by-line re-derivation of the weight flow and an executed check on production artifacts. Its headline corrects this file's own record: **ERRATUM to the 2026-07-29 follow-up analysis, leg (1)** -- that entry stated the dfs_step7 loss "runs five GradNorm-balanced task channels", quoting the `L5_gradnorm_vxc_step7` docstring; in fact GradNorm is DORMANT on every dfs_step7 production run. The sweep YAML sets no `update_scheme`, the cluster default is `per_molecule` (grid_config.py; and `validate_every: 25` REQUIRES that scheme -- the config layer rejects any other), and `run_training` dispatches to the per-molecule loop before consulting `spec.balancing`; that loop forces the nominal `vxc_weight`/`density_weight` pre-scales to 1.0 and applies the FIXED density-dominant channel weights {AE 1, BH76 1, IP13 1, vxc 1, rho 20} -- i.e. the Letter's lambda_RE = 1 / lambda_n = 20 structure, exactly the "deliberate density prioritization" the erratum'd entry said the pipeline lacked. (The 2026-06-05 methods-box entry had this right; the July entry regressed against it.) Executed proof: over all 1400 optimizer updates of a completed production spec (run_20260728T140018Z, spec_0012), the recorded loss equals 1*(AE+BH76+IP13+vxc) + 20*rho to a maximum relative deviation of 2.2e-16, every entry carries update_scheme=per_molecule, and loss_vxc is nonzero in 1000/1400 updates (the OEP V_xc channel is live in production). Legs (2) selection-without-density and (3) SCF depth of the July analysis stand; the named A/B follow-up is re-aimed accordingly (the training lambda already matches the Letter -- the deviations that remain are density-blind checkpoint SELECTION and SCF depth). The primer also adjudicates the trajectory-weight discrepancy between the Letter and its own code: the paper states w_j = ((j-10)/15)^2 over j = 10..25 while the vendored dpyscf implements linspace(0,1,N)^2 weights with skip = max(5, N-10) (last 10 steps at ~0.39..1.0) -- `scf_tail_window`'s "reproduces DFS exactly" refers to the code, not the paper formula; at the production `full_3` the profile is [0, 0.25, 1.0] squared to [0, 0.0625, 1] on the energy channels only. Two mis-statements fixed at their sources: the `losses.py` docstrings now scope the GradNorm sentence to `update_scheme="batched"` and state the per-molecule fixed weights, and `train_metadata.json` now records the runtime truth -- `update_scheme`, `balancing_active` (False on every per-molecule run; the configured GradNorm block is written but never consulted there), and `effective_channel_weights` -- so the artifact no longer reports a dormant balancer as if it ran (the base metadata key-set pin updated accordingly; runtime-truth keys RED-verified against the prior code).
  **Why:** the weight story had been mis-stated twice in this repo's own documentation, both times seeded by the same two artifacts: a loss CLASS named after a balancer its production loop never runs, and a metadata file recording the configured-but-dormant balancer while omitting the weights actually applied. A primer pinned to executed production arithmetic, plus metadata that reports the runtime truth, removes the trap; the erratum keeps the canonical history honest rather than silently overwriting it.

- 2026-08-03 (RESULTS-AFFECTING, deliberate mid-sweep intervention; tooling only in this commit) -- the CH and NO training CCSD references were confirmed to predate the orientation-lock stamp and were relocked mid-run. PROBE RESULT (`hpcjobs/dfs6311_lock_stamp_probe.py`, read-only, run against the production cache): CH carries NO `orientation_lock_strength` key, mtime 2026-07-03 20:21:56; NO likewise, mtime 2026-07-04 01:49:05; HO carries `3e-05`, mtime 2026-07-15 20:10:57; N2 (closed-shell control) unstamped. The lock-blind reference-cache fix landed 2026-07-14 (`599d64726`), so HO was regenerated after it and CH/NO never were: the training SCF has been running locked at 3e-05 against two UNLOCKED references for the whole sweep, and `data.py`'s consumer guard could not see it because it raises on a MISMATCHED stamp but is blind to a MISSING one. This is the confirmed mechanism behind the frozen density channel of the diagnosis entry below. TOOLING: `hpcjobs/dfs6311_lockfix_chno_regen.sbatch` regenerates CH and NO into a STAGING directory with the production identity (basis 6-311++G(3df,2pd), grid 3, DF on, auxbasis auto, lock 3e-05, mirroring `cluster/inputs.py`'s DFS-domain `precompute_all` call), never touching the live cache, and gates on both staged files carrying the lock; `hpcjobs/dfs6311_lockfix_swap.py` performs the atomic swap on the login node, preserving the displaced files as `<name>.npz.prelock`. PROPAGATION (verified in code, not assumed): references are read at TRAIN time (`train._build_batch` -> `data.precompute_fixed_density_data`, train.py:358 via run_training), the precompute cache key includes the reference's `(mtime_ns, size)` (data.py:280-283), and each array task is a fresh process, so every task the harness has not yet fired picks up the relocked references with no spec re-materialization and no disturbance to running tasks. The species selection is self-limiting rather than hard-coded: `run_oep_cascade`'s skip predicate reads a missing stamp as 0.0 and re-runs only on a lock/basis mismatch. PROVENANCE: the swap creates a deliberate boundary inside one run -- specs started before it trained against unlocked CH/NO, specs after against locked -- so the swap script writes `lockfix_swap_manifest.json` into the run directory recording the swap time, the per-species before/after stamps, and the exact spec partition (complete / in flight / not yet started) at that instant. EXECUTED 2026-08-03 15:53:39 EDT (staging job 2111686 on long-40core, 1572 s; both species regenerated with oep_converged True and electron counts 7.000/15.000, staged-gate verified before any live write; displaced files preserved as `CH.npz.prelock` / `NO.npz.prelock`). BOUNDARY AS EXECUTED: specs 0000-0023 are the OLD side (21 complete plus 0020/0021/0023 in flight at the swap) and specs 0024-0087 the NEW side -- the partition read off the checkpoints agreed independently with the scheduler's running/pending sets. The figure suite now reads that manifest and stamps the boundary on every figure the density builder renders (`lockfix_boundary` / `lockfix_note` in make_ablation_arch_figure.py, folded into the shared note band and echoed to the console), counting only the cells actually plotted and naming the spec range on each side; runs without a manifest are unchanged. In-flight tasks are counted on the OLD side because they loaded their references at task start -- but a task that TIMES OUT and resumes after the swap re-enters `_build_batch` and would switch references mid-training, so any of 0020/0021/0023 that requeues belongs to neither side and must be re-run.
  **Why:** the sweep's density channel is inert (diagnosis below), so the remaining ~76% of cells would otherwise spend weeks training against a reference no functional can match. Swapping mid-run trades within-run homogeneity for a recorded before/after contrast on exactly the degenerate species the lock exists to fix -- accepted deliberately, with the affected early cells to be re-run later. The alternative of finishing the sweep unchanged was rejected because it cannot test the Letter's density claim at all; restarting outright was rejected as more expensive than re-running the 18 completed cells.

- 2026-08-03 (PHYSICAL ARTIFACT + diagnosis; no code behavior changed) -- **the held-out densities do not improve because the density channel exerts no effective optimization pressure: it is flat across training, and where the open-shell radicals CH and NO are in the training set they own 68-98% of it with an error the functional cannot close.** Diagnosis in `notebooks/analysis/DENSITY_DIAGNOSIS.md`; every number regenerated by `notebooks/analysis/density_diagnosis_evidence.py` from pulled artifacts alone. THE ARTIFACT AND ITS MECHANISM: CH carries an Eq. 20 per-electron density error of 1.55e-01 against its stored CCSD reference (NO 7.78e-02) where the population median is 8.4e-03, and model-free PBE reproduces CH's error to within 0.4% (NN/PBE = 1.004), so no functional change closes it. The cause is a DEGENERATE-COMPONENT MISMATCH, established from the pulled data rather than assumed: `density_rmse_pbe` is model-free -- a fixed functional against a fixed reference file -- so it cannot vary between specs unless the SCF settles elsewhere, and pooling every pulled def2-svp evaluation shows all 20 closed-shell species bit-identical across every spec and run (max/min 1.000) while EXACTLY THREE vary -- HO 23.0x, CH 20.1x, NO 8.0x, the three open-shell 2-Pi radicals. They vary at constant energy (CH's PBE total energy spans 8.2e-08 Ha, HO 1.1e-06, NO 4.4e-08, against ~1e-13 float noise for the closed-shell set) -- same energy, different density is a degenerate manifold. Decisively, 4 of 114 CH draws land at or BELOW the closed-shell population median (smallest 0.61x), which a genuine functional-vs-reference gap cannot produce (fixed functional + fixed reference = one number) and component mismatch does. The stored references are themselves well-formed (SVP `CH.npz`: oep_converged True, n_electrons exactly 7.0000, oep_density_error 9.5e-03 against controls HO 9.3e-03 / CN 8.5e-03 / C 9.0e-03), so this is not a corrupted cache. The run's own YAML states the orientation lock exists to pin OH/CH/NO reference and training densities to the same component; under it the 6311 run has ZERO cross-spec scatter, but only HO landed on the component its reference used (1.36e-04, ordinary) while CH (1.58e-03) and NO (2.57e-03) did not -- **the lock delivered reproducibility, not agreement**. Direct confirmation at the production basis is OPEN (those references were not pulled); probe commands in the diagnosis Sec. 6. DETECTION SIGNAL worth remembering: a channel frozen across 200 epochs while it grows to dominate the loss budget, plus a per-species NN/PBE error ratio of ~1.0 at an order of magnitude above the population -- and, for degeneracy specifically, a MODEL-FREE error that varies across otherwise identical evaluations at constant energy. CONSEQUENCE: `loss_rho` moves by a median factor of 0.998 over 18 specs (rising in 9) while `loss_AE` falls 15.9x-198398x and `loss_BH76` to 0.271-0.784x; the flatness is not a window artifact (median ratio 0.998/1.000/1.004/1.004 at 5/10/20/50-epoch windows; OLS over all epochs gives +0.48% across 200). The channel occupies 15-94% of the loss purely because everything else collapsed around it. Net: these runs are energy-only training with a decorative density term, so they do not test the Letter's central claim that density supervision improves densities. SCOPE, recorded because the obvious stronger inference does NOT survive its own test: CH is in every cell with subset_size >= 6 and none below, yet held-out density does NOT track CH presence -- the two best density cells in the run (spec_0017 0.968, spec_0016 0.999) are CH-containing, and the outcome separates by ARCHITECTURE instead (deep_attn_3x16 median 1.027 vs deep_3x16 1.087, permutation p = 0.0118). The artifact establishes that the channel is inert; it does NOT establish that repairing it would improve held-out densities. That is the open experimental question. Cleared by measurement: the training weights (least-squares recovery from the logs gives (1,1,1,1,20) to 4e-16 over all 42950 updates, and perturbing the density weight to 19 or 21 raises the residual to 5e-02, so the test has power); held-out contamination (no held-out species at artifact scale; PBE eps median 9.1e-03, max 2.2e-02); checkpoint selection (final 1.0503 / train-best 1.0520 / val-best 1.0488, -0.14%, with a paired test agreeing on magnitude but favouring FINAL on 1072/1998 species rows -- so not a driver, and not evidence that val-best is better; this supersedes the 1.066 -> 1.112 figure of 2026-07-29, which was the RMSE channel on run_20260723T161502Z); the 3-cycle SCF (91.3% of held-out rows nominally unconverged, but median last-step energy residual 1.285e-05 Ha = 0.008 kcal/mol); and an energy-density trade-off NOT DETECTED though with little power (Pearson r = +0.18 is t = 0.62 on 12 df, p ~ 0.55). REMEDY, ranked, none applied to the running array: (1) per-species density weighting, the Letter's own `lambda_n` x 0.01, applied to **CH and NO** rather than the Letter's CH and OH -- in this run OH is the trio member that landed correctly and behaves normally, while NO carries 26% of the channel in the largest cells and would be missed by a CH-only fix; (2) a channel-homogeneity guard at spec-build/first-epoch naming any species exceeding the channel population by an order of magnitude; (3) make the lock's component agreement provable per species (reference generation and training/eval SCF on the same component, verified not assumed) -- narrower than it sounds, since reproducibility is already achieved. The A/B answering the open question must run a subset_size >= 6 cell in BOTH architectures, since the density outcome tracks architecture and a single-arch test could not separate the effects.
  **Why:** the pipeline reproduces the Letter's density weighting exactly, which made "densities do not improve" look like a modelling limit; it is instead a degenerate-state handling gap on two radicals that silently consumed the channel in every cell large enough to learn from. Recording the artifact with its detection signal matters more than the fix: a species whose SCF and reference sit on different members of a degenerate manifold produces an error no functional can close, it dominates an averaged channel, and it is invisible in the aggregate loss curve -- the Letter hit the same wall on the same class and papered over it with a per-species weight. The model-free-error-varies-at-constant-energy test is the cheap general detector and belongs in the reference pipeline, not just in this diagnosis.

- 2026-08-05 (figure tooling; results-additive -- four newly completed cells plotted, plus a coverage mark that stops a partial-sweep gap being read as a ladder result) -- the dfs6311 production sweep (`run_20260728T140018Z`) reached 32/88 held-out cells: `deep_cusp_3x16` at subset_size 7/12/15 (specs 0028-0030) and the FIRST meta-GGA cell of the run, `deep_mgga_3x16` at subset_size 1 (spec 0033). That first meta-GGA cell puts a second Jacob's-ladder rung on the figures, and doing so exposed a defect in the two figures that aggregate OVER subset_size. `plot_rung_summary` takes each arch at its LARGEST subset and means within the rung, so it would have reported GGA = mean(deep@ss26 7.97, deep_attn@ss26 6.36, deep_cusp@ss15 9.29) ~ 7.9 kcal/mol against meta-GGA = deep_mgga@ss1 = 33.3 -- a 4.2x gap on the headline "does climbing the ladder help?" figure. At the matched subset_size 1 the GGA archs sit at 13.28 / 14.36 / 17.96 against the same 33.32, so the reported gap is a training-set-size difference, not a rung difference. `plot_mae_by_arch` carried the same confound from the other direction, averaging 11 subset cells for `deep_3x16` against 1 for `deep_mgga_3x16` with rung bands drawn behind the bars to invite the comparison. FIX (`make_ablation_arch_figure.py`): the mismatch is marked on the existing axes rather than restricting or reweighting the data. Shared helpers `_subset_coverage` / `_shallow_archs` / `_shallow_rungs` / `_rung_coverage` / `_coverage_caveat` define an arch as SHALLOW when its largest subset_size falls below the deepest any arch reached, and a RUNG as shallow only when the rung's own deepest arch is shallower -- so the GGA rung, which holds a full-depth arch alongside a stopped one, is correctly not marked. `plot_mae_by_arch` hatches every bar of a shallow arch (hatch is free there -- its series are distinguished by color and its rungs by background band) and states each arch's subset span in the tick label; `plot_rung_summary` can spend neither hatch (already the BH76/W4-11 split) nor face color (rung identity), so a shallow rung's face is faded 55% toward white, keeping its hue, and takes a dotted warning edge and a warning-colored tick label carrying `n` and the ss span. Both stamp a footer line naming the shallow archs/rungs and the depth each reached. The parity legends (`plot_parity`, `plot_ae_parity`), which draw each arch's deepest spec into one cloud, now state that depth per arch. On a level grid nothing is hatched or faded and no footer line is added, so a complete sweep renders as it did before. Reference provenance needed no change: specs 0028-0030 train on CH (0030 on NO as well) and fall on the post-swap side of `lockfix_swap_manifest.json`, taking the relocked-reference cells from 1 to 4 and picking up the star glyph from the existing data-driven path; spec 0033 trains on {H, H2O, Li} only and is correctly unmarked.
  **Why:** a sweep fills the arch x subset_size grid column by column, so an architecture that enters late is compared against fully-trained siblings for as long as it takes to catch up -- and the two figures that answer "does this architecture help?" and "does climbing the ladder help?" are precisely the ones that average that gap away. Marking it keeps every measured cell plotted, where restricting to a matched subset would discard the deepest cells and reweighting would invent a number; the one comparison the data cannot yet support is instead made visibly untrustworthy. The mark is derived from coverage rather than hard-coded, so it disappears on its own as the remaining columns fill in.

- 2026-08-05 (figure tooling + a new offline reference job; results-additive -- adds the SCAN comparator, changes no existing number) -- **the meta-GGA architectures are now training, so the figures gained the meta-GGA reference they are actually measured against.** The `_mgga` archs pretrain to SCAN; with PBE (a GGA) as the only reference line, the question those archs exist to answer -- does a net trained on CCSD densities beat the meta-GGA it clones? -- could not be read off any figure. The SCAN reference lines had in fact been CODED since the rung figures were written and had simply never rendered: `scan_pool_baseline` degrades to all-NaN when its cache is absent, the figures then omit the line, and nothing on stdout ever mentioned SCAN, so no SCAN cache was ever generated at any basis (confirmed: no `scan_pool_energies_*.json` anywhere on disk). GENERATION (`notebooks/analysis/precompute_scan_pool.py`, `hpcjobs/dfs6311_scan_pool.sbatch`): one SCAN KS-SCF per species over the 214-species held-out BH76+W4-11 pool at the production identity (6-311++G(3df,2pd), grid 3, DF, auxbasis auto), on long-40core so it does not compete with the production training array for nodes; resumable at both the per-species SCF and the JSON layers. The DENSITY leg costs no second SCF -- `run_scf_with_cache` already persists the AO-basis density matrix beside `e_tot`, so `--with-density` contracts that stored dm onto the reference grid and scores it with the SAME functions the PBE columns use (`evaluation.pbe_density_errors`, `evaluation.density_eps_terms`), emitting `scan_pool_density_<basis>.json` beside the energy cache. ORIENTATION LOCK: `--orientation-lock` is threaded into the SCF and must match the references' lock (production 3e-05); the density leg reads the reference's own stamp and REFUSES to score a species whose stamp is missing or mismatched, rather than treating a missing stamp as 0.0 -- the same blindness that let the CH/NO references drift (entry above). A grid guard rejects any species whose SCAN density fails to integrate to pyscf's electron count, which is what proves the recomputed AO grid is the grid the reference weights were written for. COVERAGE MATCHING (`make_ablation_arch_figure.py`): `reaction_mae_kcalmol` silently drops a reaction whose species energy is missing, and a meta-GGA SCF on a diffuse basis does not always converge, so a partial SCAN cache would have averaged fewer reactions than the PBE line beside it while being drawn as a like-for-like pair. Every SCAN reference now carries its coverage measured against what PBE achieved on the same list (`scan_coverage` / `scan_line_value` / `scan_density_mean`): drawn unqualified at full coverage, labeled with the covered count below it, and withdrawn entirely under 90%. PBE's own numbers are deliberately NOT restricted to SCAN's coverage -- the PBE reference must not move because a SCAN species failed. NEW SURFACES: SCAN's WTMAD-2 (`wtmad2_scan_baseline`, reduced over the reactions the PBE leg reduced, via the existing `_wtmad2_over_pools`), its per-reaction errors (`scan_reaction_errors`), its density anchors on the held-out/in-sample density panels and the 3x3 D/E/F rows, and `ed_scan` on the Eq. 21 summaries and the G/H/I rows. `ed_scan` is a genuine second point, not a relabeled energy leg: gamma is calibrated on PBE, so `ed_pbe == e_pbe` by construction while `ed_scan` is moved by SCAN's density leg. Absent either cache every figure renders byte-identically to before.
  **Why:** a reference line is only a reference if it answers the question the figure poses. Once meta-GGA archs enter the sweep, "beats PBE" stops being the interesting claim -- a meta-GGA net that beats a GGA but loses to the meta-GGA it was warm-started from has demonstrated nothing, and that comparison was invisible. Doing the density leg in the same pass matters because the Letter's claim is specifically about densities, and the demo already measured SCAN's held-out density as WORSE than PBE's at def2-svp (5.60e-4 vs 2.32e-4); a SCAN density line better than PBE's at the production basis would therefore be evidence of a lock/component mismatch rather than a result, which is why the lock stamp is enforced rather than assumed. The coverage matching exists because the cheapest way to get a wrong answer here is a SCAN line averaged over a quietly smaller benchmark than the PBE line drawn beside it.

- 2026-08-06 (RESULTS-AFFECTING for every architecture carrying a density-matrix-dependent descriptor) -- **the SCF's exchange-correlation potential was not the functional derivative of the exchange-correlation energy it integrates.** `oneshot._compute_vxc_nn_core` assembles V_xc from two per-point forward-mode JVPs that pin the FEATURE tangent to zero, so `v_rho` and `v_sigma` are partial derivatives at FIXED descriptor features rather than total derivatives. Whenever a descriptor responds to the density matrix, the chain-rule term `sum_g w_g (de_xc/dfeatures)_g . dfeatures_g/dP` is absent from the Fock matrix and the SCF iterates on a gradient that does not belong to its own energy. `compute_vc_polarized_per_spin` carries an INDEPENDENT copy of the same defect (its three JVPs likewise pass a zero feature tangent), and since every spec of the production sweep is built with `use_polarized_correlation: true` (`cluster/spec_builder.py` overrides the registry default from the sweep YAML; verified by loading all 88 spec files and all 8 pretrain manifests), that second site is the correlation potential for every open-shell species -- the radicals and free atoms atomization energies are built from. BISECTION: with features held FROZEN the shipped V_xc is the exact derivative for every architecture (4.4e-10 to 2.7e-11); with features recomputed from the perturbed density matrix it is wrong by 1.9e-03 to 1.0e-02, and the discrepancy equals the missing term. (Absolute residuals depend on the probe -- perturbation direction, step size, grid level -- so every figure in this entry is quoted as the committed test measures it, at its own eps of 1e-6, and is reproducible by running that file.) Affected paths are MANUAL/FULL (`full_3`, the sweep's only solver, and `full_25`) and the pyscfad backend; FIXED_J is NOT affected because `SolverConfig.effective_feature_policy` auto-resolves it to FROZEN, where the term vanishes identically. The defect is pre-existing library code, so every historical SCF result for a `_mgga` / `_rung35` / `_dm` / `_combined` architecture is affected -- in this sweep 5 of 8 architectures, 55 of 88 cells. WHY THE TESTS MISSED IT: `test_fd_energy_potential_consistency` computed the features once and held them fixed inside the perturbed energy -- the identical assumption the implementation makes, so both shared one blind spot -- and built its model from `get_architecture("deep")`, which has no descriptors, so the density-matrix-dependent path was never exercised at all. FIX: the missing term is assembled ADDITIVELY (`oneshot.feature_energy_derivative` + `oneshot.feature_response_vxc`) rather than by replacing the assembly with a whole-energy `jax.grad`. `de/dfeatures` is taken at the same sanitized inputs and under the same tail mask as the `v_rho` JVP, frozen with `stop_gradient`, and contracted against the derivative of the SAME `P -> features` closure the energy uses, so the potential cannot drift from the functional. The additive form was chosen over the whole-energy gradient deliberately: the existing assembly carries ~40 lines of double-`where` tail sanitization (`_V_RHO_THRESHOLD`, `_V_SIGMA_THRESHOLD`) that keeps `0*inf` off the tape, a raw `jax.grad` of the energy has no counterpart for it, and one was measured returning a non-finite V_xc for `deep_dm_3x16` on H2/sto-3g while staying finite on H2O -- an intermittent failure a single smoke test would miss. A second defect of the same class was uncovered by the fix and closed with it: `features.compute_dm_features`'s `off_diag_norm` took `||X||_F` of a block that is identically zero whenever the basis has one function (`nao == 1`, e.g. H or He in a minimal basis), whose derivative is `0/0`. That NaN propagated through the feature array into the sibling columns, so a single degenerate system produced a NaN Fock matrix -- latent until this change made the feature map differentiable, and not reachable at production bases, where `nao > 1`. Guarded by a masked square root that leaves every value bit-identical, rather than by squaring, since this feature is O(0.1-1) on real densities and rescaling it would invalidate checkpoints that consume it. Routing is by architecture (`has_dm_dependent_descriptor`, keyed on the presence of `compute_from_dm`), so `deep` / `deep_attn` / `deep_cusp` keep the analytic path unchanged; `cusp` is the only geometry-only descriptor, which is exactly why those three behaved. The added term must NOT be wrapped in `stop_gradient`: doing so leaves the returned V_xc bit-identical -- `de/dfeatures` does not depend on the density-matrix argument being differentiated, so the inner derivative cannot see it -- while silently cutting every OUTER derivative, and the training loop differentiates this Fock with respect to the network parameters at every step. Measured with the freeze in place, `d V_xc / d(parameters)` was wrong by 1 to 7 percent, and the gradient of the SCF-PREDICTED density matrix (the quantity the density channel is built on) by 5.4e-02 relative against a finite difference; without it, 1.0e-08 to 1.9e-08 and 6.2e-10 respectively. On the UKS side `de/dfeatures` is ACCUMULATED across all three terms of the split energy (spin-scaled exchange twice, correlation once) before a single contraction, since the features are shared. The zeta floor/clip/tail-freeze, previously duplicated in the energy and in the per-spin correlation potential with a comment asserting they must match, now come from one shared `oneshot.uks_zeta`, with a test that fails if either function re-derives them. VERIFIED against a finite-difference oracle with features recomputed from the perturbed density matrix, production configuration (polarized correlation, non-degenerate initialization), H2O/def2-svp (RKS) and Li/def2-svp (polarized UKS), pre-fix meaning the routing predicate forced False (exactly the old code path): `deep_rung35_3x16` goes from 1.87e-03 to **2.12e-10** (RKS) and from 8.69e-04 to **3.54e-08** (UKS), `deep_rung35only_3x16` from 2.21e-03 to **5.19e-10** and from 1.61e-03 to **3.55e-08**; the meta-GGA architectures improve by five orders on the RKS side (5.33e-03 to 3.13e-08) but remain bounded by the separate `stop_gradient` below. Controls are unchanged to the digit, pre-fix and post-fix alike: 1.93e-10 / 9.18e-11 / 2.15e-10 (RKS) and 3.33e-08 / 3.35e-08 / 3.57e-08 (UKS). The open-shell threshold is 5e-7, not the 1e-9 the RKS side reaches, because the polarized probe has a genuine truncation/round-off floor: sweeping eps on the descriptor-free controls gives 2.64e-06 / 1.74e-06 / **3.33e-08** / 1.65e-06 at 1e-4 / 1e-5 / 1e-6 / 1e-7 for `deep_3x16` and 8.79e-06 / 6.34e-06 / **1.79e-07** / 1.64e-06 for `deep_notransform`, i.e. the residual falls as eps^2 and then rises with round-off, minimising at 1e-6. The untransformed-input architectures sit 5.4x above the transformed ones, so the bound must clear 1.79e-07. A genuine energy/potential inconsistency would not fall with eps at all, which is what distinguishes this floor from a defect. The open-shell test additionally EXCLUDES grid points whose guard status differs between the two evaluations: `clip`/`maximum` on zeta and the network tail mask redefine the functional, autodiff returns the true derivative of the redefined function, but a central difference straddling one of those boundaries averages two different slopes and inflates every architecture's residual, including the descriptor-free control's. ALSO IN THIS CHANGE: `features.compute_dm_features`'s `idempotency_error` now uses the SQUARED Frobenius norm -- `||X||_F` is not differentiable at X = 0 and `X = D S D - D` is identically zero at every converged density, so autodiff returned -2.08e-03 against a finite difference of +4.50e-09; the squared form is a polynomial with the same zero set and restores agreement (autodiff ~0 at the stationary point, where 0 is correct because it is a minimum, and the central difference vanishes as eps^2 there, 4.35e-08 / 4.35e-10 / 4.35e-12 at eps 1e-4 / 1e-5 / 1e-6; away from the manifold autodiff matches the finite difference to 1.7e-11). The RKS `D/2` and UKS per-spin normalizations stay exactly self-consistent under squaring. This is a NONLINEAR rescaling, not a constant factor (`new = old^2 * n`), so it is negligible only where the feature is already ~0: at a converged density both forms give ~1e-16 or below, but the linear mixer produces a genuinely non-idempotent `D_mixed` every cycle -- precisely the REASSEMBLE regime this change concerns -- and there the value moves by roughly an order of magnitude (H2O, perturbed density matrix: 1.63e-02 to 1.39e-03). Checkpoints trained on the old scale exist under `notebooks/checkpoints_v3b/` for the `deep_dm` / `deep_combined` families; no such architecture is in the dfs6311 sweep, and their gradient was wrong before the change in any case, so the rescaling is accepted rather than deferred. NOT extended to the loss path, deliberately: `losses._vxc_term` and `oneshot._uks_spin_resolved_vxc` still assemble V_xc from `compute_vxc_nn` alone, but they operate on frozen precompute features taken at `dm_pbe`, so each remains the exact derivative of its own frozen-feature energy. The consequence is that `loss_vxc` now regresses a different potential from the one the SCF builds, against an OEP reference that is a full functional derivative -- not a regression, but a second reason the affected cells need retraining rather than re-evaluation. The pyscfad backend now RAISES for density-matrix-dependent descriptors outside FROZEN policy instead of silently returning a potential that is not the energy's derivative: `eval_xc_alec_gga` is a libxc-compatible per-point callback returning `(exc, vrho, vsigma)` and there is no channel through it for a global matrix term; the `get_veff` override that would carry it is specified in the raising function's docstring but not built. NOT CHANGED, each for a measured reason: the zeta `clip`/`maximum` guards stay (replacing them is not a correctness fix -- excluding straddled points alone drops the residual 6.95e-05 -> 6.15e-07 -- and a smooth surrogate is worse physics: `(1-d)tanh(z/(1-d))` compresses zeta = 0.5 to 0.462 and shifts the Li directional derivative by 2.5%, while an independent check measured 0.68 kcal/mol of change on the descriptor-free `deep_3x16` from a different bounded map, and dropping the `maximum` floor returns NaN at rho_tot = +-1e-300); the `metagga` `stop_gradient` stays pending a production-basis check (removing it takes the meta-GGA architectures from 2.5e-08 to ~1e-10 and is NaN-free at 3 cycles on def2-svp, but it is NOT redundant with the alpha clip as previously argued -- removing it inflates `max|dalpha/dsigma|` from 1.15e14 to 2.20e31 on Li at 6-311++G(3df,2pd), and the documented 2026-07-04 blow-up needed a pretrained network at 25 cycles, which exceeds this workstation's memory); and `dm_entropy` stays, with its defect and trigger condition recorded at the source -- its gradient is ill-defined at ANY idempotent density matrix (22 of 23 eigenvalue gaps below 1e-10, and eigenvector derivatives carry `1/(lam_i - lam_j)`), the clip makes it exactly 0.0 against a finite difference of +5.97e-02, and the participation ratio `(Tr[DS])^2/Tr[(DS)^2]` was evaluated as an eigh-free replacement and rejected because it equals N_occ exactly on the idempotent manifold, so every normalization that would make it size-intensive is identically constant there. WHAT THIS DOES NOT EXPLAIN, recorded because the obvious inference fails its own test: correcting the potential moves the converged `full_3` energy by 6.3e-02 (`deep_mgga_3x16`), 3.3e-02 (`deep_rung35_3x16`) and 2.6e-01 (`deep_rung35_mgga_3x16`) kcal/mol on H2O with the sweep's own pretrained checkpoints -- 40 to 144 times the shift measured with freshly initialized networks, since a random network has almost no alpha sensitivity and that is precisely the quantity the missing term multiplies, but still about two orders of magnitude short of the ~22 kcal/mol gap between `deep_mgga_3x16` (26.62) and the SCAN baseline it clones (4.89). Three damped mixer steps from a PBE seed cannot move the density far however wrong the Fock gradient is; the earlier framing, that the SCF "converges a density that does not minimise its own energy", describes a converged solve and not a 3-cycle one. This does NOT bound the effect on TRAINING, where the wrong Fock is differentiated through 3 unrolled cycles at each of 200 steps and `loss_vxc` (weight 0.01) regresses directly against the wrong potential -- so the benefit requires retraining rather than re-evaluation, and the meta-GGA gap still needs a second explanation.
  **Why:** a potential that is not the derivative of its own energy is a correctness defect independent of how much it happens to move any particular number, and it had been silently present for every meta-GGA and rung-3.5 result the project has produced. Fixing it additively rather than by replacing the assembly preserves the tail sanitization that a whole-energy gradient demonstrably loses, and keeps the three sound architectures on their existing code path, so 33 of 88 cells should need no re-run. That last claim is bounded by what the hardware can show: running the same SCF twice on this machine differs by up to 1.3e-07 Ha on a 2-Pi radical (multithreaded CPU reductions are not bit-reproducible, and a degenerate radical amplifies last-bit differences -- the same mechanism documented in the density-channel entry above), which is at or above the old-versus-new difference measured for those three architectures. The defensible statement is therefore "no systematic change detected, at or below run-to-run reproducibility", not "byte-identical"; the DM-dependent architectures shift far above that noise, as intended. The measured-but-insufficient energy shift is recorded rather than quietly dropped because the fix was undertaken on the hypothesis that it explained the meta-GGA gap, and it does not; keeping that in the record prevents the next investigation from re-deriving a closed question, and keeps the open one -- why the meta-GGA cells do not improve with subset size -- visibly open. The guards left in place are each documented with the measurement that argued against touching them, so the next person to notice a `stop_gradient` in an energy functional finds the reason it survived rather than repeating the experiment.

- 2026-08-06 (RESULTS-AFFECTING for every meta-GGA architecture) -- **the iso-orbital alpha carried a `stop_gradient` on the energy path, and removing it takes the meta-GGA architectures from a bounded-but-wrong potential to the descriptor-free control level.** `metagga.compute_alpha` ended with `jnp.where(rho < _RHO_GRAD_CUTOFF, jax.lax.stop_gradient(alpha), alpha)`, freezing the residual `~n^{-8/3}` tail gradient. alpha is a descriptor feature consumed by `E_xc`, so wherever that freeze was live autodiff returned something that is not the derivative of the function being differentiated, and the inconsistency propagated straight into `V_xc = dE_xc/dP`. With the freeze in place the meta-GGA architectures sat at 3.1e-08 (RKS) and 1.7e-05 (polarized UKS) on the energy/potential finite-difference test, against ~1e-10 / ~3e-08 for every sound architecture. With it removed they land AT the control: `deep_mgga_3x16` 2.09e-10 / 3.86e-08 against the descriptor-free `deep_3x16`'s 1.93e-10 / 3.34e-08, `deep_mgga_attn_3x16` 1.12e-10 / 3.89e-08, `deep_rung35_mgga_3x16` 7.03e-10 / 3.85e-08. (Those RKS residuals are deterministic within a process but move by up to 4.8x with evaluation ORDER, so the figures quoted are the ones pytest's own parametrization produces, not the more flattering fresh-process values; `_TOL_RKS` was set to 2e-9 to clear the worst by ~3x rather than the 1.4x that 1e-9 would have left.) Combined with the feature-derivative fix of the entry above, every architecture except the `dm_statistics` family is now at the finite-difference floor on both the RKS and the polarized-UKS path. THE COUNTER-EVIDENCE, recorded because it is real and was nearly decisive: the `[0, _ALPHA_MAX]` clip bounds alpha's VALUE and zeroes the derivative of points pinned at the ceiling, but it does NOT bound the derivative of points below it -- removing the freeze takes `max|d alpha/d sigma|` from 1.15e14 to 2.20e31 on Li at `6-311++G(3df,2pd)`, so the freeze was load-bearing for the magnitude even though it was wrong for the derivative. What settles it is that the full-SCF training gradient stays finite anyway, INCLUDING at the exact configuration the freeze was introduced for. The Phase-17 `bh76:HLi` failure needed pretrained weights at 25 cycles on the diffuse basis; run here with `scf_grad_checkpoint=True` that costs 1.6-5.6 GB, not the workstation-exceeding footprint previously assumed, so it was measured rather than deferred: `deep_mgga_3x16` and `deep_rung35_mgga_3x16`, pretrained checkpoints, `6-311++G(3df,2pd)` grid 2, 25 cycles, polarized, on free H (`rho_min` 6.4e-10), free Li (5.4e-13) and LiH (2.2e-13) -- all six gradients finite and nonzero. A separate 29-configuration sweep over molecule, basis, grid, cycle count, checkpointing, architecture and seed found 0 non-finite. NOTE for anyone re-running these: `hpcjobs/dfs6311_nan_verify` does NOT exercise this path -- it builds an architecture with no descriptors and no `meta_gga`, so `compute_alpha` is never called; an earlier draft of this entry cited it as the safety net, which was wrong. TESTS: the test that asserted the freeze (`gs == 0.0 and gr == 0.0` below the cutoff) pinned the defect itself and is replaced by a finiteness assertion across the same eight decades of density, plus `test_compute_alpha_has_no_stop_gradient_on_the_energy_path`, which checks the source carries no freeze (comments stripped, since the removal is documented by quoting the old line) and that autodiff matches a finite difference in the formerly-frozen regime. Its probe is constructed rather than guessed: `tau = tau_W + tau_unif` at `rho = 1e-7` puts alpha at exactly 1, BELOW the ceiling and BELOW the old cutoff -- an arbitrary tau at that density pins alpha at `_ALPHA_MAX`, where the clip legitimately zeroes the derivative and the test would have passed vacuously for an unrelated reason. It discriminates: rel 8.4e-12 after, rel 1.00 before (autodiff 0.0 against a finite difference of 1.6166e+11). The `_TOL_BLOCKED_MGGA` tolerance branch was DELETED in the same change, since the architectures it covered now measure at the control level and a loose bound would have made the assertion pass vacuously.
MEASUREMENT ARTIFACT found while checking the above, recorded because it invalidates single-run evidence on this class of system and is NOT caused by this change: **the diffuse-basis H atom does not converge in 25 SCF cycles (`converged=False` in every run), and under multithreaded BLAS its unconverged energy and training-gradient magnitude WANDER between otherwise identical runs.** Four early repeats of one configuration gave total energies spanning 60 mHa (38 kcal/mol) and `|g|max` spanning eight orders (5.7e-02 to 1.5e+07). Pinned down by a controlled comparison, three repeats per cell: with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` the result is BIT-IDENTICAL across runs both with the freeze (E = -0.4984442925, `|g|max` = 1.380186e-01) and without it (E = -0.5001520809, `|g|max` = 1.088971e-01); with default threading BOTH wander (freeze off, E spread 13.7 mHa; freeze on, 3.3 mHa). So the nondeterminism is float-summation order in a still-moving SCF trajectory, not the gradient freeze -- the frozen code wanders just as much. Consequences worth carrying: a single run on an unconverged open-shell diffuse-basis system is not evidence of anything, any such measurement must be repeated or pinned to one BLAS thread, and the honest reading of "0 non-finite in 29 configurations" above is that it sampled a wandering quantity many times without hitting a NaN, not that the quantity is stable. The single-threaded pair also isolates this change's real effect on that system: 1.7 mHa, an SCF that has not converged being pushed to a different point by a different (and now correct) potential.
  **Why:** the freeze was introduced to stop a NaN, and it did -- but it stopped the symptom by misreporting a derivative, which is the same class of defect as the missing feature-derivative term fixed in the entry above and had the same consequence: an SCF minimising an energy whose gradient it does not have. Recording the 2.20e31 magnitude alongside the fix matters more than the fix itself, because the next person to see that number will be tempted to reinstate the freeze; the note says what to do instead, and names the one configuration that local hardware cannot settle. A tolerance branch that no longer reflects a real defect is worse than no branch at all, since it silently absorbs the next one.

- 2026-08-06 (RESULTS-AFFECTING for the `dm_statistics` architectures; ADDITIVE elsewhere) -- **`dm_entropy` was removed (descriptor width 3 -> 2) and a multi-width rung-3.5 descriptor added.** `dm_entropy` had no usable gradient at any converged density and could not be repaired: the physical-bounds clip put every natural occupation on a boundary for a single determinant (H2O/def2-svp: every natural occupation sits ON a clip boundary -- at 2.0 or at/below 1e-12 to within round-off (the exact count at 2.0 varies between 2 and 5 with summation order)), so autodiff returned exactly 0.0 against a finite difference of +5.97e-02; removing the clip made it worse, since 22 of 23 eigenvalue gaps are below 1e-10 and `eigh` eigenvector derivatives carry `1/(lam_i - lam_j)`, leaving the gradient ill-defined at ANY idempotent density matrix -- which is every converged SCF density. AN ENTIRE CLASS IS RULED OUT, not just this feature: for a single determinant the eigenvalues of `DS` are exactly {2,...,2,0,...,0}, so any function of the SPECTRUM alone depends only on `N_occ` and is CONSTANT on the idempotent manifold. Measured, `Tr[(DS)^n]/N` returns `2^(n-1)` for H2, N2 and CO alike, and the participation ratio `(Tr[DS])^2/Tr[(DS)^2]` returns `N_occ`; N2 versus CO is the sharp test, same 14 electrons and different bonding giving an identical value. A useful replacement must probe the EIGENVECTORS -- the spatial and bonding structure -- not the spectrum. Screening and sources are in `notebooks/analysis/DM_DESCRIPTOR_SPEC.md`.
