- 2026-08-20 -- **Held-out NaN records traced: a per-species exception swallowed in the energy pass, never re-queued, with its message discarded.** The 2026-08-20 pull carried 176 null-`E_total_nn` rows on the v4gga run (spec_0043: 1; spec_0050: 28; specs 0055-0061: 147; per-architecture rate deep_3x16 / deep_attn / deep_cusp 0, rung35 0.01%, rung35_attn 1.5%, rung35ms 3.3%), every one with the same signature: `E_pbe` finite, NN-vs-CCSD density fields finite, `cycles_run` 0, `scf_converged` True, no `scf_total_energy`/`scf_energy_step_*` keys. That signature is the untouched default of `make_per_molecule_record`, written only when `scf_info` has no entry for the species, which happens on one path: a raise inside `evaluate_holdout`'s per-species `try` (the energy-pass `run_scf`, the `float()` of its energy, or the tail-weighted mean) before `scf_info_out[name]` is assigned, caught by the bare `except Exception` and turned into `nan`. The density fields survive because `density_errors_for_record` runs a second, independent `run_scf` per species, later and unguarded; a deterministic failure would have killed that call and the whole shard. The failures are transient: specs 0056, 0059 and 0060 carry byte-identical final and validation-best checkpoints, so the cluster evaluated the same model on the same 214 species twice -- energies agree to <6e-10 Ha and densities to <6e-12 where both passes finished, yet 40 of the 42 NaN outcomes in those pairs occurred in only one pass, and every NaN row's density equals the finite pass's density to the run-to-run floor. Cross-channel NaN sets within a spec are disjoint (all-three-channel overlap 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 0 over the eight specs), single atoms (al, p) failed too, and 220 of 224 NaN-carrying shards hold exactly one NaN. All 176 came from tier-1 shards that completed and wrote their JSON; `_holdout_parallel` marked every name in a completed shard as done by key, so no NaN species was ever re-queued to the lower-parallelism tiers (0 of 176 appear in any tier-2 shard). The rate tracks the architectures' memory footprint rather than any physics: dead tier-1 shards (killed workers, no JSON -- the severe form of the same pressure) number 153 for rung35ms, 68 for rung35_attn, 5 for rung35 and 0-4 elsewhere; the rung35ms projector stack is `(3, N_grid, nao)` float64, three times the rung35 stack, with 5-6 species resident per worker and 40 workers per node. The exception class itself is unrecoverable from the pulled data because the worker's `eval[name] FAILED` line went to a stdout stream that `run_workers` discards and no per-shard log existed. The 2026-08-18 wording ("per-species evaluations killed transiently inside cluster shard processes") is corrected accordingly: the process survived and wrote its shard; a per-species exception was raised and swallowed. A distinct population -- genuine three-cycle SCF divergences scored verbatim (|scf_energy_residual_0| > 0.1 Ha on 6.4% of rung35ms rows, 3.5% rung35_attn, 0.4-0.9% elsewhere; median |residual_0| 2.6e-3 Ha for rung35ms, ten times the others) -- explains the arm's catastrophic bars and is not touched by any repair.
  **Why:** a null energy whose density columns are populated reads, on the figures, as an incomplete evaluation of a finite model; establishing that it is an execution artefact of a specific code path (and not a property of the model or the species) is what licenses repairing it by recomputation, and the same trace names the three defects that let the artefact persist silently: the done-by-key bookkeeping, the swallowed message, and the missing per-shard log.

- 2026-08-20 -- **Held-out eval scheduler repaired: non-finite species re-queued through the tier ladder, failure records made explicit, worker diagnostics persisted, the eval watchdog's spurious stall warnings silenced, and a stdout-pipe deadlock removed.** `cluster/_holdout_parallel.py` now retires a species only when its shard energy is finite; a null or NaN energy re-enters the remaining set and flows to the next tier and, after the last worker tier, to the in-process serial sweep, after which a species still non-finite is accepted with its last payload and named in one log line (`N species failed in every tier: ...`). `eval_holdout.merge_holdout_shards` resolves name collisions by precedence (finite beats non-finite; among equals the later tier wins) and de-duplicates `mol_records` by molecule. `evaluate_holdout` prints every per-species failure to stderr (previously only the first, to a stdout stream that was discarded) and records `eval_error: "<Type>: <msg>"` in the SCF info; `make_per_molecule_record` writes `cycles_run: None, scf_converged: None` (plus `eval_error` when known) for a failed species, keeps the `(0, True)` sentinel for finite rows of solver modes that run no SCF (the `SCFConvergenceMetric` convention), and leaves finite SCF rows byte-identical (pinned by test), so the historical signature -- null energy with `cycles_run 0, scf_converged True` -- can no longer be produced. `parallel.run_workers` gains an opt-in per-job `log_file` (both streams, written beside the shard JSON as `worker_t{tier}_s{idx}.log`), `_holdout_parallel` forwards every `FAILED:` line to the task log, and the stall watchdog is skipped for jobs that declare no progress file (the eval worker never wrote one; the training jobs keep theirs). While the log was added, the stdout pipe was found to be read only after process exit, so a worker writing more than the 64 kB pipe capacity blocked forever (a 1 MB-stdout worker was still blocked after 45 s); both pipes are now drained by threads; at retirement each drainer is joined on a bounded budget (`STREAM_JOIN_SEC`), the captured lines are snapshotted before they are joined into a string, and a stream whose drainer is still parked on a pipe held open by a grandchild is left for that thread to release (closing it from the parent blocks on the reader's buffer lock, measured at 7 s until the grandchild exited), so a leaked pipe costs a truncated capture rather than a stalled poll loop (20.0 s -> 0.45 s in the grandchild-holds-pipe scenario). The local repair driver strips and re-copies `eval_error` with the other SCF scalars, so a repaired row cannot keep stale failure text. Every consumer of the record's `cycles_run`/`scf_converged` (`plot_scf_convergence`, `mgga_diagnosis_evidence`, `verify_overfitting_report`, `make_cluster_pulls_figure`, the repair driver, `refinalize_verbatim`) was checked and reads a failed species as not converged. Tests: 36 + 43 + 57 in the holdout and parallel files, 26 in the driver's, 151 in the holdout/parallel selection, all green. Deployment: `xcquinox/alec/eval_holdout.py`, `xcquinox/alec/parallel.py` and `xcquinox/alec/cluster/_holdout_parallel.py` on the cluster, in ONE transfer (the new scheduler passes `log_file`/`progress_file=None` to `WorkerJob`; against an old `parallel.py` that raises `TypeError`, which `_eval_one_spec` swallows into the full-serial fallback -- correct results, hours slower, and only one "parallel path failed" line in the task log); the shard worker, its CLI and the shard JSON keys are unchanged and old and new shard and record files are mutually readable, so the transfer itself is safe while an array is running. A species that fails deterministically now also reaches the in-process serial sweep, whose density pass is unguarded -- a raise there lands in the same serial fallback; no such raise occurred in the 2026-08-20 record. The tier-1 worker count for the DM-projector architectures stays a configuration lever (`cluster.eval_workers`, DEFERRED #24).
  **Why:** the tier ladder already existed for whole-shard deaths but had no per-species counterpart, so exactly the failures it was built for slipped through as finite-looking records; a failure record that reads `cycles_run 0, scf_converged True` is indistinguishable from a converged zero-cycle SCF; and a diagnostic that never reaches a log cannot be acted on.

- 2026-08-20 -- **Log-y siblings of the grouped-bar figures.** The six figures whose bars come from the shared per-(arch, subset_size) helper (`ablation_energy_wtmad_mae`, `ablation_insample_overview`, `ablation_density_energy_overview` and its DFS-units twin, `ablation_density_energy_3x3` and its DFS-units twin) are now rendered twice by `build_density_energy_figures`: the linear file unchanged, and a `_logy` sibling on a logarithmic y axis (`ablation_rung_summary` keeps its single linear file and `ablation_mae_by_arch` its single log file). The shared panel helper `_grouped_arch_bars` takes `yscale`; under `"log"` the limits are set from the quantities drawn (floor at the smallest bar or comparator over 1.6, ceiling at 1.5 times the largest, so the incomplete-eval star and the row-3 gamma stamp keep their headroom), the minor grid is drawn and the y label carries `(log)`; a panel with nothing positive drawn is framed on one bare decade, because an autoscaled log axis with no positive data raises at draw time and would abort the whole composite. Every plotted quantity is strictly positive by construction (absolute-error reductions, RMSE means, ED filtered above zero), so no data path needed masking, and the linear renders are unchanged (the SCAN headroom block is scale-aware, the grid call equals its former default). Each pair of write sites expands one kwargs dict, so the two renders cannot drift apart. CSV outputs are unchanged. Tests: four helper tests (log limits span everything drawn; the default stays linear with a zero bottom; an empty log panel still saves; an unknown scale is refused), a 3x3 test asserting the real axes state at save time, the filename pins raised from 4 to 6 and 16 to 22 files with the six `_logy` names, the DFS-units negative guard extended, the frame formula pinned exactly with span-driven floor and ceiling cases, `yscale` validated in every plotter, a RuntimeWarning naming any finite non-positive bar under the log axis, and a builder-level spy pinning each of the six `_logy` write sites to the log scaling (a linear duplicate under a `_logy` name cannot ship undetected); the figure test file passes 294 of 294 (3 deselected). `README_density_figures.md` and `RUNBOOK_pull_and_figures.md` list the siblings.
  **Why:** on the merged view the rung35ms bars reach 397 kcal/mol and flatten every other architecture's differences to the pixel; a log axis keeps both the outliers and the sub-kcal/mol structure legible, and keeping the linear file unchanged preserves the figures already quoted.

- 2026-08-20 -- **The SCAN-seeded meta-GGA cells read against SCAN.** `notebooks/analysis/NOTES_v5_mgga_vs_scan.md` records the 2026-08-20 analysis: at subset sizes 2-5 the `deep_mgga_3x16` cells reproduce SCAN's held-out accuracy on energy and density (E/E_SCAN 0.94-1.01, D/D_SCAN 1.01-1.08 on the merged slice) and are worse than SCAN at 1, 6 and 7; one of 21 (leg, subset) cells beats SCAN. The signed-error decomposition shows why: both parents' BH76 error is ~90% bias (PBE -7.5, SCAN -6.0 kcal/mol of MAE 7.7 and 6.4); fine-tuning removes the bias in both arms and injects reaction-level scatter nearly uncorrelated with the parent's residual (sd 5.7 -> 7.9-8.7 for the meta-GGA; corr 0.2-0.3 with SCAN's errors; NN-minus-SCAN 8.6-11 kcal/mol RMS), so the exchange is a net gain over PBE and a wash over SCAN; on W4-11 the meta-GGA is 2-5 times worse than SCAN (sd 13-26 vs 4.8) with cell-dependent biases of -15.5 to +21.1 kcal/mol, the training molecules are fit far below the target accuracy (final losses 1.6e-8 to 1.1e-4, minima 5.8e-14 to 7.3e-11; the loss has no parent-anchor channel), and the density channel holds at SCAN level. The pretrain-fidelity probe (pretrained networks evaluated on frozen PBE densities at the production identity against SCAN, and the GGA networks against PBE as the control; numbers re-derived by an independent script to 0.013 mHa; one density, bit-identical weights, production spin states and loaded weights confirmed) shows the meta-GGA cells do not start at SCAN: the pretrained meta-GGA over-binds H2O / N2 / CH4 by 30.5 / 55.9 / 20.8 kcal/mol relative to SCAN, against 2.5 / 4.2 / 2.4 for the GGA control relative to PBE, with the sign flipping between open-shell atoms (+7 to +34 mHa in E_xc) and closed-shell molecules (-12 to -22 mHa). The dominant cause is in the production evaluation of open-shell exchange, not in the network: `split_exc_energy_uks` (`oneshot.py:493-495`) applies the Oliver-Perdew spin scaling (Phys. Rev. A 20, 397 (1979)) by doubling rho and quadrupling sigma per spin channel but passes the total-density meta-GGA alpha feature unchanged into both channels; alpha has an exact doubled-spin transform (alpha_sigma from 2 rho_sigma, 4 sigma_sigma, 2 tau_sigma -- libxc's spin-polarized SCAN exchange equals two unpolarized evaluations at those ingredients to <1e-12 Ha), and transforming only that term moves the atomization offsets to -7.6 / -7.9 / -7.6 kcal/mol (75-86% of the effect) and the atomic exchange offsets to the molecules' sign. The same frozen-feature scaling sits in the SCF potential (`_uks_spin_resolved_vxc`, `oneshot.py:764`) used by every solver backend and by the vxc loss, so it acts on the training and the evaluation of every open-shell species of the meta-GGA architectures. The pretraining rows add a secondary contribution (open-shell atoms stored with spin-resolved SCAN targets against total-density inputs, `pretrain_data_gen._atom_columns:94-96,129-153`: +1.0 / +7.3 / -2.0 kcal/mol of the offsets). The GGA control is exempt by construction (no descriptor feature), which is why it sits at 2-4 kcal/mol. DEFERRED #25 records the correction as a campaign decision: it changes the energy and potential of every open-shell species for the meta-GGA architectures and invalidates the v5 meta-GGA cells.
  **Why:** the campaign question was whether SCAN seeding alone lets the meta-GGA arm improve on SCAN; the data say the limiting factor is the fine-tuning regime's scatter injection relative to the parent's removable bias, which is a property of the recipe, not of the seeding.

- 2026-08-20 -- **Pretraining does not deliver the parent functional for any descriptor-carrying architecture.** The probe that exposed the meta-GGA alpha defect was run on the v4gga pretrains against PBE (frozen PBE densities, production footing; every value re-derived independently to 0.001 kcal/mol): atomization-energy offsets (H2O / N2 / CH4) of -2.5 / -4.2 / -2.4 (deep_3x16) and -2.3 / -4.1 / -3.1 (deep_attn_3x16) for the descriptor-free networks, against -13.2 / -4.2 / -25.7 (deep_cusp), -13.5 / -3.5 / -29.1 (deep_rung35), -29.5 / -20.4 / -56.1 (deep_rung35_attn) and -22.0 / -30.9 / -42.8 (deep_rung35ms). These are not spin-scaling defects: the cusp feature is pure geometry and exact under the doubled-spin substitution, and the rung-3.5 occupancy has no in-domain doubled-spin evaluation (values to 1.9 against its [0, 1] bound, energies 40-80 times worse), so the frozen-feature convention stands for them. The offsets come from the H-atom pretraining error shared by every cusp-carrying network (+13.7 mHa against +0.8) multiplied by the hydrogen count, and from molecular extrapolation of density-matrix features never constrained by the atoms-plus-mesh pretraining set (molecular offsets flip sign for rung35_attn and rung35ms). The pretraining loss does not see it (deep_rung35_attn_3x16: lowest exchange residual of the six, largest offset). DEFERRED #26 records the protocol gap; no campaign check ever compared a pretrained network with its parent on atoms or atomization energies.
  **Why:** every descriptor-carrying cell of both arms began its 200-epoch fine-tune from a functional 13-56 kcal/mol from the parent on the reaction type it was trained on, which the held-out biases and scatter of Phase 38-39 then reflect; the safeguard that follows is a preflight that measures exactly this table per architecture and refuses a campaign that fails it.

- 2026-08-20 -- **Local backfill of the 176 NaN entries, stopped after 8 channels once the record was retired.** The repair driver ran on specs 0043, 0050, 0055 and 0056 under the measured identity gates (spec_0059/final controls reproduced at dE_nn <= 1.2e-12 Ha, dE_pbe <= 3.0e-13 Ha): 47 species patched, 1 refused by the PBE-solution gate (spec_0050/val-best), 0 diverged, 0 unresolved, in 61 minutes; the remaining channels were left as pulled when every in-flight training was cancelled and the v4gga descriptor-architecture record was retired (below). The end-to-end check of the repaired scheduler against the same run (six species through the real shard worker with the spec_0043 model) reproduced the cluster record to 2.6e-13 Ha.
  **Why:** the recomputation was sound -- the failures are transient execution artefacts -- but a record whose functional definition is superseded is not worth completing; the ledger files allow the remaining channels to be re-applied if the pre-fix figures are ever wanted.

## Phase 40 -- Pretraining-fidelity certificate: every architecture measured against its parent before a campaign stage runs (2026-08-23 to 2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-21 to 2026-08-23 `d84328798`..`5f07fb1b8` -- **Per-architecture pretraining-fidelity certificate, enforced from the pretrain array task through to the figure suite.** A pretrained exchange/correlation network pair is accepted only when it reproduces its parent functional in ENERGY units, and the statement that it does is a machine-checked file beside the checkpoint. `xcquinox/alec/cluster/fidelity.py` evaluates `dE_xc = E_xc^NN[rho_parent] - E_xc^parent[rho_parent]` through the production energy path, on the parent's own self-consistent density, at the run's SCF identity, and folds the molecular differences against the free atoms into atomization offsets `dAE(mol) = dE_xc(mol) - sum_atoms n_atom dE_xc(atom)`; PASS requires max |dE_xc| over the free atoms <= `tol_atom` AND max |dAE| <= `tol_AE`. The parent follows the architecture's RUNG (`rungs.seed_xc_for_arch`), not the run's SCF seed, which is a different thing: PBE for a GGA-rung architecture, SCAN for a meta-GGA one. The oracle set is every free atom of the BH76 / W4-11 pools at the pools' own charges and spins (14 species), Li and Na from a ground-state table for the DFS molecules that dissociate into them, the DFS pretraining set at the level the architecture was pretrained on (30 systems at the GGA level; 28 at the meta-GGA level, whose protocol drops H2 and N2), and H2O / N2 / CH4 at the POOL geometries for every rung, so that the three offsets tabulated in `SPEC_pretrain_fidelity_program.md` Section 2 are one physical quantity across architectures -- 39 systems for a GGA rung, 38 for a meta-GGA one. The verdict, every per-system number, the five-field run identity (basis, grid level, density fitting, auxiliary basis, orientation-lock strength), the SHA-256 digests of the two network files measured and the installed `xcquinox_version` (a versioningit string that carries the git revision) are written to `<run_dir>/pretrain/<arch>/fidelity_certificate.json`, which every gate reads through one reader so the sites cannot drift apart.
  - **Three independent routes to the parent's E_xc.** Per system the parent energy is taken point-wise with libxc on the record's own stored (SCF-pruned) grid, with pyscf's `nr_rks` / `nr_uks` on a freshly built grid of the same level, and as the XC energy the reference SCF itself accumulated; a pairwise disagreement above `PARENT_GRID_TOL_HA = 1e-6` Ha fails the certificate naming the system. Measured worst spread over the complete oracle sets at def2-svp / grid level 1: 1.6e-9 Ha (PBE, 39 systems) and 1.9e-9 Ha (SCAN, 38 systems), in both cases on the diffuse Be atom and in both cases the fresh-grid route's pruning difference; the stored-grid and reference-SCF routes agree to 2.8e-14 Ha or better. With the parent functional itself presented behind the model interface the certificate is an identity -- 3.6e-15 Ha (PBE) and 2.0e-10 Ha (SCAN) on the O atom -- and an imposed +0.5 mHa per electron offset is reported as +4.000000005 mHa on the eight-electron O atom against the same predicted value and fails `tol_atom` by name; that offset cancels to -2.0e-5 kcal/mol in the atomization fold, which is why the verdict carries a free-atom tolerance beside the atomization one.
  - **Degenerate free atoms are measured on an orientation-locked record.** An open p shell holding 1, 2, 4 or 5 electrons is a P term whose hole can converge to any orientation; the exact functional is orientation-invariant, a quadrature on a fixed real-space grid is not, and the meta-GGA parent feels it: the SCAN E_xc of the free O atom spreads by of order 0.1 mHa between independent unconstrained SCFs at def2-svp / grid level 3 (0.26 mHa over one triple of runs, 0.084 mHa over another; 0.21 mHa for F at sto-3g / grid level 1) against 1.6e-3 mHa for PBE -- a meaningful fraction of the 1.0 mHa free-atom tolerance, decided by which orientation the SCF happened to reach. The certificate therefore builds the reference density of such atoms under `orientation_lock.DEFAULT_STRENGTH` (3e-5, the value the production configurations carry) whenever the run's own `inputs.orientation_lock_strength` is zero, records the applied lock per system and names the locked systems in the payload; two independent locked runs then agree to 3.4e-11 mHa at that identity. Spherical atoms (half-filled, closed and s-shell) are excluded, and an element beyond argon is refused rather than guessed.
  - **A non-finite measurement is a named failure, never a silent pass.** The verdict is a `max(...) > tol` comparison and `nan > tol` is False, so a diverged pretraining checkpoint could be certified PASS and a NaN could mask a 500 mHa atom depending on the order `max` met them. Every per-system value is now tested with `math.isfinite` before any comparison; a non-finite one is nulled in the payload and recorded as a failure naming the system and the key, a nulled `dE_xc` cannot enter the atomization fold, and the file is serialized with `allow_nan=False`, so a bare `NaN` or `Infinity` token cannot reach disk.
  - **The reference density became a parameter of the one precompute.** `data.precompute_fixed_density_data` gained `reference_xc` (default `"pbe"`, leaving training and evaluation unchanged), which selects the functional of the reference SCF and therefore the density behind every field of the record -- the density matrix, the grid quantities, the total-density and per-spin descriptor blocks, and `E_pbe` / `E_xc_pbe` / `E_non_xc` -- instead of the certificate rebuilding eighteen fields it would then have to keep identical to `data.py` by hand (a source-level guard refuses any such rebuild). The name is canonicalized before the cache key, the SCF and the record -- stripped, lower-cased and resolved to `"pbe"` or `"scan"` by libxc parse identity, so `"PBE"`, `"pbe,pbe"` and `"gga_x_pbe,gga_c_pbe"` are one SCF, one cache entry and one recorded name. An unknown functional is refused up front; hybrids and non-local-correlation functionals are refused, because pyscf books their exact-exchange or VV10 energy where no point-wise consumer of the record can evaluate it. The closed-shell XC energy dispatches on the functional's rung (`libxc.xc_type`), so a meta-GGA reference reads the XC energy pyscf accumulated on the grid rather than a GGA row set that cannot carry the kinetic-energy density. A SCAN record reproduces a standalone pyscf SCAN calculation of the same molecule on the same grid in both total energy and density matrix, and it is a genuinely different record: on the OH radical the per-spin meta-GGA alpha differs by 5.88 between a SCAN record and a PBE one, against 5.98e-6 between two independent PBE runs of the same radical.
  - **Reference-SCF convergence is stamped, and non-convergence refuses the record.** A reference SCF that ran out of cycles used to be written silently. DIIS now runs from the minao guess at a 100-cycle cap (the slowest converging runs measured took 48 and 53 cycles, the latter past pyscf's default of 50, while a run that has stalled does not recover with more cycles), a stall is handed to pyscf's second-order solver started from the DIIS end point under the same |g| < sqrt(conv_tol) criterion, and what is still unconverged raises `ReferenceSCFNotConverged` instead of producing a record; `mol_metadata` carries `reference_xc`, `reference_scf_converged`, `reference_scf_cycles` and `reference_scf_solver`. The stalls are real: the orientation-locked PBE O atom at def2-SVP / grid level 1 exhausted pyscf's default cap in 2 of 3 attempts, and the unlocked SCAN O atom stalled in 2 of 12 processes from the minao guess (one at a final gradient of 8.98e-5 against a 3.2e-5 criterion), rescued by the second stage in 106 total cycles. A second-order stage that cannot run -- an unrestricted system whose orbital-rotation space is empty in both spin channels, where pyscf packs a scalar in place of the rotation vector -- is reported as the same refusal carrying the DIIS cycle count, never as a bare pyscf exception. Seeding the reference SCF from a converged PBE density of the same system was measured and rejected: under the orientation lock it carried the PBE orientation into the SCAN run and converged to a stationary point 1.7e-4 Ha above the lock's minimum in 2 of 3 processes (the minao start reached the minimum in 9 of 9), unlocked it stalled in 1 of 3 processes itself, and it moves the pruned grid unless the grid is pre-initialized with the minao guess.
  - **Configuration.** A `fidelity` block on the grid config carries `tol_AE` (1.0 kcal/mol) and `tol_atom` (1.0 mHa) -- the program's binding decision -- beside `override_reason` and `enforce`. Either tolerance above 2.0 requires a non-empty `override_reason`, which is copied into every certificate; a non-positive or non-finite tolerance is refused outright (a NaN passes both `<= 0` and `> 2.0` as False, so it would otherwise load unremarked); a non-string `override_reason` or a non-boolean `enforce` is refused rather than coerced, since `bool("false")` is True and `bool(None)` is False and either coercion silently disables a gate. The check lives in `validate_grid_semantics`, and all five commands that load a resolved config and can reach `sbatch` re-validate it -- `prepare`, `submit`, `resubmit`, `resubmit-preflight` and the deferred-eval submitter -- so a hand-edited `resolved_config.yaml` has no supported route back into a submission.
  - **Enforcement has two layers.** The ON-NODE gates -- the pretrain array task's exit code, the train task, the preflight sweep and `train._build_model` in process (`XCQUINOX_ALLOW_UNCERTIFIED=1` opts a local probe out) -- call `gate_certificate`, which honours a recorded waiver: `fidelity.enforce: false` WITH a non-empty `override_reason` computes and writes the true verdict and lets the stage proceed, which is what lets the workflow-verification matrix exercise the train and eval wiring on a deliberately short pretraining run with the physics on record. The RECORD layers -- `validate_run`, `merge_v4_arms` and the figure suite -- call `certificate_status` and require PASS unconditionally, so such a run can never become a quantitative result. The pretrain task reads its verdict back off the WRITTEN certificate through the same predicate rather than off the payload it returned, so its exit code and the downstream decisions come from one statement; the train task refuses with `fidelity_certificate_missing` / `fidelity_certificate_failed` at exit 3, neither of which is in the resubmit path's retryable set; the preflight sweeps `sorted(set(cfg.sweep.arch))` with no exemption and reports the count. `validate_run` reads every certificate through the certificate module's own reader, so a document that parses but states no certificate (`[]`, `null`, `"nope"`, `0`) is a named failure rather than the silent pass it was, and an unreadable file no longer raises out of a module whose contract is to report; it additionally compares the certificate's architecture, parent, code version and network digests against the run's, and its identity field by field over the UNION of the two key sets, since a field the certificate states and the run does not is as much a disagreement as the reverse. `cluster status` prints the certified count beside the checkpoint-pair count, taken from the record predicate rather than the gate, so a waived FAIL is visible as what it is.
  - **Cross-arm merge and figures.** `merge_v4_arms` refuses an arm whose swept architectures lack a PASS certificate, re-reads each pretrain directory's certificate at the moment it is linked into the merged view (an unchecked or FAILED certificate must not occupy the slot the figure layer reads), refuses two arms whose certificates for one architecture disagree in verdict or identity, and builds the view into a staging directory renamed into place only on success, so a refusal leaves the previous view bit-identical. The figure suite reports uncertified architectures, refuses to build for one, renders the worst per-architecture numbers (largest free-atom |dE_xc| in mHa, largest |dAE| in kcal/mol) in the provenance footer, and stamps a disclosure on runs started before the gate date, whose networks were never checked against their parent.
  - **The DFS pretraining set is committed package data.** `xcquinox/alec/data/dfs_pretrain_set.json`, behind `xcquinox/alec/dfs_pretrain_set.py`, holds the eight free atoms and 22 G2/97 molecules of the DFS pretraining protocol with their geometries, replacing a read from an ASE trajectory outside the repository: the compute nodes carry only this package, and the certificate and the pretraining data generator must resolve byte-identical geometries. A name-to-composition check refuses the index-swap class of error at export and at test time, and a SHA-256 pin on the committed bytes catches a coordinate drift with no trajectory present.
  - **No new job kind and no template change.** The certificate runs inside `cluster._pretrain.main`, which already holds the checkpoint just written, the run identity and this job's exit code, and the train array is already gated `afterok` on the pretrain array, so a failed certificate blocks the campaign with no extra dependency edge. A second `python -m` line in `pretrain.sbatch.tmpl` would pay the jax and pyscf import a second time and would need failure semantics of its own; a separate SLURM job kind would add a dependency edge, a submission record and a log family for one function call. The template's single invocation is pinned by a test. The cost is wall clock -- about forty systems, each a reference SCF at the run's identity plus the network and parent XC evaluations (39 systems in 32 s at sto-3g / grid level 1, against a measured per-species precompute wall of 0.6 to 5.2 s at 6-311++G(3df,2pd) / grid level 3 for the small species and several times that for the largest molecules of the set) -- which `cluster.pretrain_time` must now cover; the example configuration says so above `pretrain_time`.
  **Why:** no stage of the pipeline had ever compared a pretrained network with its parent functional. On the pretrained checkpoints the running campaigns were training from, the atomization-energy offsets from the parent on H2O / N2 / CH4 (frozen parent densities, production footing; `SPEC_pretrain_fidelity_program.md` Section 2) were 2.3 to 4.2 kcal/mol for the descriptor-free architectures and 13.2 to 56.1 kcal/mol for every descriptor-carrying one -- larger than the architecture differences the campaigns exist to resolve, so an arch-to-arch comparison was in part a comparison of pretraining errors, and this was true while the campaigns ran. A tolerance without a gate is a preference; the certificate is the gate, and it is enforced at every layer that could otherwise let an unchecked number reach a figure. Recording the installed `xcquinox_version` is what makes the certificate a statement about the code that actually ran rather than about code in principle.

- 2026-08-24 `73ce49ab4`, `2a977c501`, `4101dcb78` -- **Every certificate decision and every certificate record derives from ONE parsed document.** The gate's waiver path parsed the certificate file up to three times and the readers beside it parsed it again for their own fields, so under a concurrent rewrite a decision could be assembled from documents that never existed together: a served sequence of a bare failure, a reasonless waiver and an unrelated reason released a checkpoint that no single document releases. Every site now parses once and derives everything -- classification, status, reason, excerpt, identity and release -- from that one parse.
  - `73ce49ab4`: the atomization fold's non-finite guard and the writer's refusal of a non-finite payload are pinned by tests; `validate_run` reads each certificate once, so a certificate rewritten between reads can no longer pair a stale reason with a fresh document, and an identity key the certificate omits is reported as absent rather than passing unexamined; the pretrain worker's summary line is formatted from the certificate that reached disk, the document its gate decided on; the figure layer counts only certificates that state their numbers, reports the rest separately, marks the provenance line when a contributing certificate did not pass, and names each uncertified architecture's status in the suite refusal; `XCQUINOX_ALLOW_UNCERTIFIED` refuses any set value other than `1`, naming the accepted spelling, so `true`, `yes` or `0` can no longer disable the in-process gate.
  - `2a977c501`: all six serving orders of the three-parse waiver path now refuse, each consuming one document; the worker's gate and its summary line read the same document, so a mid-run rewrite cannot report a recorded failure as a released pass; the figure layer counts unreadable or absent certificates instead of silently shrinking the disclosed pool and names the number a half-numbered certificate is missing; the merged view labels a waived failure as one and validates the same document it records. The release truth table over twenty single-document states is unchanged.
  - `4101dcb78`: the last two multi-document records -- the train task's failure record and the workflow matrix's certificate record -- are folded onto one parse, where a rewritten certificate could otherwise label a refusal PASS and a served sequence could record a complete waiver beside a refusing gate; both reproduce the first document's record under every serving order and are identical to the shipped record over 22 single documents. The figure footer discloses a run whose certificates are all unreadable instead of dropping the clause, keeps the pre-gate footer byte-identical, omits the worst-of-zero clause grammatically, keeps a one-axis bound when the other axis states none, and scopes its pool to the registry, so an unregistered directory's certificate cannot replace the run's own bound.
  **Why:** a gate whose decision and whose record can derive from different documents can misreport or misrelease under concurrent writes -- exactly the condition a cluster run creates, where the pretrain worker writes the certificate while the record layers read it. Every decision now names exactly one document, and the single-document behaviour is unchanged, so the fold alters only what happens when the file moves underneath a reader.

## Phase 41 -- Per-architecture workflow matrix: every registered architecture driven end to end from one command, and its cluster job (2026-08-21 to 2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-21 to 2026-08-23 `74ed36161`..`cacecae3b` -- **The matrix: one command drives datagen, pretraining, certificate, preflight, train, evaluation, oracles and run validation for one architecture at a rendered identity, locally and in process, with nothing queued.**
  - **Rendered configuration** (`cd58ffa51`, `a834e9e2f`). `write_matrix_yaml` renders a one-architecture def2-svp / grid-level-1 grid config per registered architecture from a shipped template that `validate_grid_semantics` refuses until an architecture is substituted; all 31 registered architectures enumerate. `stage_cached_inputs` stages the cached reference and subset-ledger inputs out of the repository; a supplied reference-cache directory is an INPUT -- it must exist and hold a staged cache (manifest or species npz) and is never created -- and directory staging copies with `shutil.copy2` explicitly so the partial-directory behaviour is testable.
  - **Oracle selection** (`f8562331e`). `oracle_selector` builds the `pytest -k` expression that selects exactly one architecture's parametrized oracle cases from the spin-scaling oracle module, excluding every registered name that CONTAINS the target name so `deep` does not collect `deep_attn`; the expressions were checked against pytest's own expression parser for all 31 architectures over 3 node ids in both parametrize id orders (31/31 exact) and against a collect-only run on a stand-in module. The job collects exactly 18 cases for `deep_3x16`.
  - **Stage runner** (`c386571a3`, `847cbbf7c`). `run_arch` drives the stage sequence -- submit dry-run, datagen, pretrain, certificate, preflight, train, eval, oracles, `validate_run` -- through a single runner seam, each stage a subprocess with its own log and a bounded timeout; the submit stage is asserted dry-run, the evaluation channel must carry both slice marks and close exactly three reactions, and an absent oracle module is a named stage failure. The rendered template carries the waiver block (`enforce: false` with a written reason) because a 50-step pretraining cannot certify, so the expected certificate outcome is a waived FAIL with the physics on record; `validate_run` itself stays strict, and its expected outcome under that waiver is a non-zero exit whose failure report holds exactly one line, the certificate-verdict refusal naming the architecture under test -- a zero exit, an extra failure line, or a refusal naming another architecture is itself a matrix failure. `run_matrix` continues past a failed architecture, and the certificate stage runs at the rendered identity, pinned by a test.
  - **Command line and findings** (`531ee1b79`, `0b534f226`, `cacecae3b`). `python -m xcquinox.alec.cluster.workflow_matrix` takes `--archs` by exact registry name, a per-stage timeout and a work root required outside the repository, and returns `matrix_exit_code(results)` rather than re-deriving the predicate. Usage errors -- an unknown, empty or repeated architecture list, an in-repository work root, report path or reference directory, a non-positive stage cap -- exit 2 with the reason on stderr, so exit 1 keeps its meaning of a defect found. The findings block names the first unexpected non-zero stage with its return code and log path (an early datagen failure had been misattributed to the certificate, and a preflight failure produced no findings section at all), gives an enforced FAIL certificate and a failing oracle run their own findings, scores a short stage list with a finding naming the missing stages, and never calls clean a record whose certificate did not release the gate or states no release.
  **Why:** the 2026-08-20 finding (Phase 39) was that no stage of the pipeline had ever exercised any architecture's full workflow, let alone every architecture's -- the descriptor-carrying networks reached production 13.2 to 56.1 kcal/mol from their parent without one end-to-end run touching them. The matrix is the standing proof that each registered architecture's workflow runs to completion at a real identity, and its exit code is the public predicate.

- 2026-08-21 `7d345ae0b`, `409e05540` -- **Cluster walltimes validated as strings.** An unquoted sexagesimal walltime (`time: 8:00:00`) was resolved by the YAML parser to the integer 28800 and rendered as `#SBATCH --time=28800`, which SLURM reads in MINUTES: a 20-day request in place of eight hours. The literal is now recovered from the cluster block when its base-60 value reproduces the loaded integer, and every walltime field (`time`, `preflight_time`, `eval_time`, `pretrain_time`, `datagen_time`, `timeout_retry_time`, `benchmark_refs_time`) must be `H:MM:SS` or `D-HH:MM:SS` -- bare minutes, `MM:SS`, `D-HH`, floats and free text are refused naming the key and the value -- with all 22 shipped configurations loading unchanged. The `--time`-style command-line overrides normalize through the same helper before `dataclasses.replace`, naming the offending flag and refusing before any run directory or `sbatch`. **Why:** the several forms SLURM accepts are all legal and mean different things, so a mistyped walltime is not a parse error but a different request; a request that silently becomes 20 days is discovered only by the scheduler, after the job has booked the node.

- 2026-08-24 `c0ac175d4`, `969ea662b`, `42cb379fc` -- **The matrix as a single-node cluster job.** `hpcjobs/workflow_matrix.sbatch` runs the matrix on one node from a work root outside the repository, taking the architecture list from `MATRIX_ARCHS` (one name for the smoke; all 31 in `MATRIX_BATCHES` batches of 16 and 15 otherwise) and the per-stage wall from the template's rendered stage wall; the 24 h request is bounded by 11 timed subprocesses per architecture at the 1800 s cap over 16 architectures (22 h). The matrix's exit code reaches SLURM through the tee'd log by `PIPESTATUS`, and script-level refusals (inactive environment, work root inside the repository, missing ledger, empty reference directory) exit 3 so that 1 keeps its meaning. Every `MATRIX_ARCHS` name is validated against the registry listing before anything runs and the matrix's usage-error exit is mapped to the job's own refusal class, so a typo is never reported as a completed run with findings; the wall bound is recomputed from the batch and shard settings AT RUN TIME and refused when it exceeds the request (44 h and 88 h configurations against a 24 h request both refuse; the defaults run at 22 h). A five-field contract probe -- the stage list, the shard cap, the stage-cap bound, the oracle module's presence and the parser's own exit -- replaces the matrix entry point with a raiser before anything runs and treats any outcome but the parser's exit as a probe failure, so a checkout missing the stage-cap refusal is reported as a partial sync with the push line rather than as an environment failure (the earlier probe fell through the parser and died on an unwritable work root), and each field is read with a default so an older package reports the field wrong instead of crashing. The job stages a private reference copy per run, refuses an in-repository reference directory, matches the staging predicate on hidden files, carriage returns and dangling symlinks, reads its stage thread budget from the allocation, gives a single-architecture run the whole node, points the oracle stage at the module file so an unrelated broken test module cannot abort it, judges the work root before creating it so a refusal leaves nothing behind, and carries the mail directives and the house `sbatch` idiom. **Why:** the matrix is a standing proof only if it runs at cluster scale on the code the cluster actually has: a probe that falls through on a partial sync reports the sync defect as an environment failure, and a wall bound that is not recomputed from the knobs in force turns a batch-size edit into a truncated run. The per-architecture baseline table is recorded here once the cluster run completes.

## Phase 42 -- Pretraining protocol: the parent's energies enter the objective, held-out validation selects the network, and each data file states one identity (2026-08-21 to 2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-21 `08126b7c3`, `7a63ba140`, `0592e8882` -- **The pretraining set, its parent-density columns, and float64 enforced at the data entry point.** `PretrainSystem` records carry a normalized identity (name, geometry key, spin, charge, composition); `pool_atom_systems` collects the 14 distinct BH76 / W4-11 free atoms by (element, charge, spin) rather than by case-sensitive name; `resolve_pretrain_systems` merges the DFS set at the rung's level (30 GGA / 28 meta-GGA records) with the pool atoms into 37 / 35 systems, de-duplicated by geometry key rather than by name. The rung is read from the meta-GGA ingredient exactly as `rungs.arch_ingredients` reads it and pinned against `rungs.seed_xc_for_arch` on all 31 architectures, and the LDA exchange constant is shared with `spin_channel_exchange_rows` (bitwise identical rows) and pinned directly to libxc's value, where a 1e-14 perturbation is caught. `_system_columns` builds each system's rows on `precompute_fixed_density_data`'s reference density -- PBE or SCAN by rung -- replaying PySCF's grid pruning and refusing a density that fails the electron-count check (1e-2 per electron, floor 1.26e-3 measured on H2 at grid level 0) or the SCF-gradient check; the reconstructed exchange and correlation energies equal PySCF's `nr_rks` / `nr_uks` on the same density to 1.8e-13 Ha (PBE) and 1.5e-12 Ha (SCAN, with tau carried). `cluster/_datagen.py` now enables x64 before JAX is imported and refuses to run without it: the production pretraining data had been float64 only through pyscfad's import side effect, and with x64 off the descriptor columns are silently float32 -- on the CPU backend the datagen stage runs on, the iso-orbital indicator moves by up to 2.3e-3 (O) and 0.46 (CH4) and E_x^SCAN by -3.8e-8 Ha (O) and +3.1e-9 Ha (CH4). **Why:** the set must hold every free atom that anchors an atomization energy in the training pools, at the pools' own charges and spins, beside the DFS molecules at the rung's level, so that the objective and the fidelity certificate speak about the same systems; and a float32 descriptor column is a different functional input from the one training and evaluation see, which is not a defensible difference to carry by accident.

- 2026-08-23 `dbbc37622` -- **Per-system parent energies, the two-block schema, and the data identity.** Each system carries its parent's exchange and correlation energies on the reference density, taken as the row quadrature of the stored enhancement factors so the energy term vanishes exactly when a network reproduces those factors; against PySCF on the same density the targets close to 4.9e-13 to 4.5e-11 Ha over O, CH4 and H2O for both parents at def2-SVP / grid level 3. The `.npz` carries every column both exchange footings produce -- the historical total-density block and, on the `spin_channel` footing, a per-channel doubled-density exchange block with its OWN LDA baseline column, since an open shell's total-density LDA column is the spin-polarized one -- with int32 per-row system indices, the per-system energy table and the stored mesh share, 41 keys in all, and the reader refuses a missing block, an unknown key or a float32 column by name (the previous writer silently dropped the nested rows). The data identity is a manifest keyed on every `PretrainSystem` (name, geometry, spin, charge, composition), the parent, the exchange footing, the basis and grid, the orientation lock and the mesh: nine single-ingredient changes each invalidate it, and an identical regeneration is bit-stable. The pretraining precompute now applies the training orientation lock (3e-5); unlocked O rows differed by up to 0.94 in rho between processes, while locked rows reproduce to 1e-10 relative at the production grid. **Why:** a loss can hold a network to the parent's energies only if those energies are stored per system on the same rows the loss integrates; and a manifest that records an identity the file does not have is worse than no manifest -- the pretraining rows were being generated unlocked while training, references and evaluation ran at 3e-5.

- 2026-08-23 `39cb25d04`, with its provenance derived in `dfd229956` -- **The per-system energy term in the pretraining loss.** The network's enhancement factor is integrated over each system's rows with the stored weights and LDA prefactors and compared with the parent total recorded for that system, so a pretrained network is held to the parent's ENERGIES and not only to its point-wise enhancement factor. The term is assembled from the two-block data at the run's footing, with the per-block weights, the exchange footing and the mesh share read back off the data rather than assumed (a manifest declaring the spin-channel footing beside a file without the per-channel block is refused, as is a system table whose length disagrees with the energy table). With libxc's own enhancement factors in place of the network the reconstruction closes on the stored targets to 1.8e-14 Ha (PBE exchange), 1.1e-16 Ha (PBE correlation on spin-polarized open shells), 6.7e-15 Ha (SCAN exchange on the exact per-channel tau) and 5.8e-16 Ha (SCAN correlation), and to 4.5e-15 / 2.5e-17 Ha per system on the derived-provenance path; a +1 mHa target shift moves the term by the predicted amount to 1e-12 relative, the gradient agrees with central differences to 9e-8 relative, and at weight zero the loss value and every gradient leaf are bitwise those of the previous loss, with four pretraining configurations reproducing their trajectories bitwise. Seven tests refuse a decorative term. **Why:** no architecture pretrained to its parent -- on frozen parent densities the descriptor-carrying networks sat 13.2 to 56.1 kcal/mol from it in atomization energy (Phase 39) -- and no stage of the pipeline ever compared the two. A point-wise objective does not control the integral, so the protocol makes the parent's energies part of what pretraining minimizes and the Phase-40 certificate the gate that says whether it worked.

- 2026-08-23 to 2026-08-24 `dfd229956`, `572caf345`, `34263b694` -- **Held-out validation, a refusal on divergence, and one compiled training loop.** A seeded fraction of the pretraining MOLECULES is held out -- never an atom, since every pool atom is bounded by the certificate and anchors an atomization energy -- the validation loss at the run's own weight (energy term included) is monitored every `validate_every` steps with a patience, and the best-validation network is the one returned and written, with the step of the best value recorded; the stop arithmetic (best step plus patience times the validation interval) and the table-length guard are pinned, an empty held-out side is refused, and a fraction of zero reproduces the previous trajectories and artifacts bitwise in all four configurations (losses compared as hexadecimal floats, checkpoint digests equal). A run whose validation values are all non-finite now raises `PretrainDiverged` and writes no final checkpoint instead of returning the untrained initial network, on the validated and the unvalidated path alike. The energy term is measured on the SAVED network after training regardless of its weight and recorded with the per-system maximum |dE_xc| and RMS in mHa -- at weight zero the metadata used to read a fictitious 0.0 where a real run measures 4.9e-3 Ha^2 and 11.1 mHa -- the metadata is strict JSON, and `final_loss_x` / `final_loss_c` now describe the saved network with the last training step's values kept as `last_step_loss_x` / `last_step_loss_c`. The no-validation path runs the same in-module loop as the validated one: the historical trainer compiled its step only on the iterations that also cleared the compilation caches, costing 235 ms per optimizer step against 2-7 ms of work, and the loop now carries that trainer's periodic snapshot cadence transcribed and cross-checked against it at two rates, so checkpoint directories are equal by name and digest and the loss trajectories are bitwise those of the previous path in six configurations, at 54 to 139 times the speed (251.9 -> 1.816 ms per step at 4000 rows; 261.4 -> 4.812 ms at 16350 rows). The meta-GGA rung is carried by ONE predicate -- the presence of the metagga descriptor, with the architecture's flag and descriptor list required to agree at construction -- read by the parent-density resolution, the rung ingredients, the harness data derivation and the pretraining run. **Why:** validation-best selection is the anti-overfit machinery the fine-tuning stage already uses and pretraining had none of it; a silently returned untrained network is indistinguishable downstream from a converged one; a per-step recompilation made the unvalidated path 50 to 150 times more expensive than the work it does, which a sweep over weights and architectures pays on every cell; and one rung predicate closes the case where an architecture pretrains on one rung's targets while its self-consistent field seeds the other.

- 2026-08-23 to 2026-08-24 `39750677b`, `a501f41c0`, `4b0234d9e`, `d8482b9f7`, `8912b50b5`, `b68d0dd4d` -- **The protocol in the harness: knobs threaded, data keyed on the run's own identity, degenerate atoms refused, the configuration schema closed.** The pretrain worker passes the ten YAML knobs into its `PretrainSpec` and states them in the run-record log line (a configuration setting any of them had loaded and been ignored), the datagen stage generates one file per distinct (polarization, parent) pair of the sweep's architectures through the shared naming function, and the preflight ensure call loops the same required specs with the same keywords. Both cluster pretraining-data entry points and the datagen main now pass the run's orientation lock to the generator, so the currency check is asked at the RUN's lock and a file built at another lock is regenerated rather than served -- before, a run at any lock found the 3e-5 file current -- and the run-level lock defaults to `orientation_lock.DEFAULT_STRENGTH` through one call-time definition (the library `SolverConfig` keeps 0.0 as an opt-in primitive), parsed as a finite non-negative number, with the constant living in a module that imports nothing so the loader's closure carries no numpy (122 modules against 223). The generator refuses a spatially degenerate open-shell atom below grid level 3 or at lock 0.0 unless the configuration states `inputs.allow_irreproducible_degenerate` together with `inputs.irreproducible_degenerate_reason` in prose, threaded from the configuration through the datagen entry point and the preflight loop into the generator and recorded in the manifest: at the generator's default level 1 four locked draws of the O atom spread 3e-3 in rho, 0.64 in the iso-orbital indicator and 3.7e-6 Ha in the stored exchange energy while agreeing to 9e-10 Ha in the total energy (grid 3 reproduces to 3e-11), so two files could carry one manifest identity. A waiver stated where the identity does not need it (grid level 3 or above at a non-zero lock) is refused. The campaigns written before the lock state `inputs.orientation_lock_strength: 0.0` explicitly, the value their training, references and pretraining data were in fact built at -- no pretraining manifest on disk carries a lock key and none of the 110 cached reference intermediates carries a lock tag -- with the waiver their grid level 1-2 identity needs; the nine tracked ones are pinned by name and the count no longer depends on untracked working copies, while every grid-3 campaign states 3e-5. The loader now refuses unknown keys in the sweep, solver, hyperparameter, inputs, pretrain, fidelity, cluster and root blocks (the accepted keys are the dataclass fields; an `x-` prefix is the extension namespace), the protocol numbers carry their bounds at parse so the worker paths that skip the semantic check refuse them too, and whole numbers parse as integers, so 2**53+1 survives the load intact. **Why:** a knob that loads and is ignored is a silent default, and a data file served against an identity it does not have is a silent Hamiltonian change; both defeat the manifest the protocol relies on. Restating the pre-lock campaigns at the calibrated lock was measured and rejected: it would have re-identified each of them -- regenerating pretraining data at the grid-1/2 identity the generator declares irreproducible, and recomputing the whole reference set -- to record a lock those runs never used.

- 2026-08-23 `a233043d1` -- **The pretraining run opens the file of its RESOLVED parent.** The run named its data file at the PBE default and resolved the parent afterwards, so a meta-GGA architecture under the rung baseline (SCAN) opened the PBE-density file, or nothing, and every non-PBE campaign configuration failed at the worker. The parent is now resolved before the file is named, the name carries the parent's suffix through the generator's own naming function, and a missing parent file is refused naming the parent, the file sought and the PBE file that is present. **Why:** the rung baseline is what makes a meta-GGA architecture's parent SCAN rather than PBE; a run that trains against the wrong parent's rows cannot certify, and this defect made every meta-GGA campaign unrunnable regardless of the rest of the protocol.

- 2026-08-23 to 2026-08-24 `7fa8849d5`, `0688637db`, `eec2dc217` -- **The energy-term weight is measured, not assumed.** `hpcjobs/probe_pretrain_energy_weight.py` pretrains four architectures -- the GGA-rung ones against the PBE parent, the meta-GGA one against SCAN, since the certificate is per rung -- at five weights (0, 0.1, 1, 10, 100) over the protocol's set at def2-svp / grid level 3 under the training lock, with the production spin-polarized correlation patched on as the harness does and the parent file opened through the pretraining run itself. Per cell it reports the two point-wise losses beside both aggregations of the per-system XC energy error, reconstructed from the final network rather than read from the metadata, and gates on BOTH halves of the certificate: the maximum |dE_xc| over the 15 single-centre systems against `tol_atom` and |dAE| in kcal/mol over the molecules against `tol_AE`, with the gate quantity dE_x + dE_c pinned three ways including a run-time cross-check against the run's own recorded maximum. The recommendation is the smallest swept weight holding every architecture's worst system inside half of `tol_atom` without either point-wise loss rising by more than a factor of three from its weight-zero value; the verdict records and prints which architectures were measured, so a batched run cannot mail a whole-set verdict from half the set, and exit code 2 means the sweep completed and no weight cleared the gate. The table is written after every cell and resumed from, a resumed table of another identity is refused, an architecture whose rung flag and descriptors disagree is refused, and the interpreter teardown runs through the house hard-exit idiom after an abort at exit lost a completed table. The wall was derived from the probe's own path on the committed tree at four row counts (0.44 to 0.52 microseconds per row-step with no fixed per-step term, the 648 ms recompilation gone) with the row count corrected twice -- the rows come from the pruned grid the mean field integrates, not the bare Becke-Lebedev grid, and only the 12 open-shell systems contribute two exchange blocks -- giving 2.41e6 rows per step and an 8.0 h estimate against the kept 24 h request, which also covers the pre-rework path at 23.2 h. **Why:** the weight is dimensionful (inverse Hartree squared) and no argument fixes it. The objective minimizes an RMS while the certificate gates on the MAXIMUM over systems, so a set whose RMS clears the tolerance can still carry one system that does not; the recommendation therefore reads the maximum, and reads it against both gates, because a free-atom offset that cancels in the atomization fold and an atomization offset that no single atom shows are different failures. The default set was later widened from four architectures to the six with the largest recorded parent offsets, adding the two attention-carrying rung-3.5 forms (56.1 and 42.8 kcal/mol), thirty cells at 23.0 h against the kept 24 h request, so the weight is chosen where the gate is hardest to clear.
- 2026-08-24 `4065b875a`, `65d398676` -- **The campaign is documented and submitted in ordered groups.** `xcquinox/alec/CAMPAIGN_V6.md` records the design: every descriptor family defined as the code computes it with its sources, the training material (the pretraining set, the 26-point subset ledger and its 11 sizes, the atomization and barrier pools, the fixed 1/20 loss), and the question each cell group answers. The 341-cell single submission is replaced by five ordered standalone group files -- the size ablations (44 cells), the six production families (66) with their five meta-GGA parity forms (55), the repaired DM-carrying production-width forms (33), and the baseline width and depth twins with and without attention (22) -- 220 cells over 20 architectures, the GGA groups on the 40-core scheme at its recorded 48 h wall with the long attention cells recovering through the 96 h retry on the larger class, the meta-GGA group on the 96-core class at 72 h, and eleven architectures excluded by name with the pin holding the union of the axes and the exclusion list to the registry in both directions. **Why:** each group is an independently submittable, independently gated question, ordered from the cheapest sanity ladder to the ablations, and the width and depth question is answered on the baseline functional alone rather than on every descriptor variant. **Corrected 2026-08-24:** the long attention cells do NOT recover "through the 96 h retry on the larger class". A wall-kill that left a resume checkpoint classifies as a resume, which draws no partition and no wall override, so the cell is resubmitted into the SAME run directory at the SAME partition and 48 h wall and finishes in a second window (`python -m xcquinox.alec.cluster resubmit <run_dir> --submit`); the 96 h escalation applies only to a cell killed before its first checkpoint, which restarts from step zero and needs the whole run to fit in one window. That recovery could not execute at all as committed -- `cmd_resubmit` refused every inline-eval run, which is all five groups -- because it hard-coded the `train_array`/`eval_array` pair an inline run never renders; it now resolves the captured scripts on disk, resubmits `train_eval_inline.sbatch` alone (the eval runs in the same task, so no eval array is submitted or recorded), and refuses only when no train script is present at all, which is the failure the blanket refusal was standing in for. The four group files, the campaign document and the handover sheet carried the superseded combination "routes onto the 96-core queue at 96 h AND resumes from the checkpoint" and are corrected to the mechanics above; the same files' inheritance paragraphs now name `cluster.time` among the fields they do not inherit, and `deep_attn`'s size against the measured 3x16 cell is the measured 4.40x parameter ratio (15138 against 3442) rather than the 2.67x hidden-unit ratio.
- 2026-08-24 `ed401cc1a`, `c0eec1acd` and the closing change -- **The first cluster matrix run found two defects before any campaign cell was spent.** The single-architecture smoke (job 2134455, node dn024, exit 1 after 6 min 37 s) reported its pretrain stage at status -6: the worker had completed both pretraining phases, written the fidelity certificate and both networks and logged its success, and then died in the interpreter's teardown (glibc: corrupted size vs. prev_size while consolidating). Under a campaign that status marks every pretrain array task failed and the train array's dependency never fires. Every harness entry point -- the cluster stages, the worker scripts and the two rendered stages outside those directories, eighteen in all -- now leaves through a shared hard exit that flushes every stream and open file and exits with the verdict the entry returned, pinned by a source walk and by subprocess tests that inject an abort at exit (the verbatim exit form returns -6; the wired form returns its verdict with every stream intact); the matrix re-reads a certificate the stage wrote before aborting and names the case. The same run failed one closed-shell oracle case: the non-XC energy of the deep_3x16 record differed from the workstation-recorded fixture by three ulps (4.3e-14 Ha), the pinned summation order not reaching the BLAS kernels the cluster's CPU selects; the fixtures now carry a platform fingerprint, the bitwise comparison runs where it matches, and elsewhere every key is held to 1e-11 relative with the discrepancy printed. The certificate's own FAIL in that run (125 mHa at a 50-step, two-atom pretrain) is the matrix's expected, waived outcome. **Why:** the matrix exists to exercise the whole workflow on the machine that runs it; both findings were invisible on the workstation, and the first would have stalled the entire campaign after its pretrains. **Closed in the closing change:** a sweep of every `__main__` block under the package, `scripts/` and `hpcjobs/` found six further stages that a checked-in sbatch script launches and that still left through interpreter teardown -- `refinalize_verbatim`, three `hpcjobs` probes and the two pool precomputes -- and all six now leave through the same exit, with the source pin widened to cover them; two SLURM-launched readers stay on the stock exit, since they import neither the package nor JAX and write nothing, so wiring them would pull JAX and PySCF into processes that load neither and create the exposure it is meant to close. The compile smoke's throwaway checkpoint directory is disposed of in `main`'s own `finally` rather than through `atexit`, which the hard exit never runs (measured: one directory per `--smoke` run leaked). A pretrain task whose architecture already holds both networks and a certificate its own on-node gate releases -- PASS, or FAIL under a recorded waiver naming its reason -- now logs that the completed pretraining stands and exits 0 without repeating it; on a tiny identity the second run of a finished architecture takes 0.0 s against 4.2 s and leaves every artifact byte-identical, where the earlier code re-pretrained and rewrote them, and a missing network, an absent or unreadable certificate and an enforced FAIL each pretrain again. **Why:** a pretrain array task has no resubmit path -- `cmd_resubmit` reduces the train and eval kinds only -- so a pretrain-stage failure is recovered with `resubmit-preflight`, which re-submits the whole pretrain -> preflight -> train -> eval graph and now re-runs only what is incomplete; `status` names the pretrain stage in that remedy rather than the preflight when the pretrain stage is the incomplete one. **Closed in the second closing change:** the keep decision now also requires that the certificate describe what is on disk -- its recorded identity equal to the run's as the config states it (basis, grid level, density fitting and its auxiliary basis, orientation-lock strength), its recorded parent equal to the one this architecture's rung is pretrained against, and its two recorded SHA-256 digests equal to those of the networks now beside it -- and the comparison is the one `validate_run` already applied, factored into `cluster/fidelity.py` and called from both sites so that a single rule exists; a disagreement pretrains again and the log names the fact that moved. **Why:** a released verdict states that an architecture reached its parent, not which networks were measured or at which identity, and `resubmit-preflight` -- the only path that re-runs pretrain into an existing run directory -- reloads `resolved_config.yaml` precisely because that file can be edited between submissions, while none of its refusals covers an edited basis, grid level, density-fitting backend, auxiliary basis or orientation lock, and a redo interrupted between the two networks being written and the certificate being recomputed reaches the digest half with no edit at all; a certificate kept in either state survives the recovery and is refused only by `validate_run`, that is, after the whole train and eval graph has been spent, which is the cost the keep check exists to save. The `status` remedy for a stalled graph decides on the same on-node release rule the pretrain stage applies rather than on a PASS-only count, so a run whose FAIL carries a recorded waiver -- `fidelity.enforce: false` with its reason, the workflow matrix's own configuration and job 2134455's -- is reported as released with its waived architectures named instead of being blamed on a gating that did not happen, and the per-architecture scan behind that line is measured once per invocation rather than twice. `hpcjobs/probe_pretrain_energy_weight.py`, launched by its own sbatch script, leaves through the shared hard exit as well, keeping its four-way exit code and the partial table it owes on an escaped failure, which retires the last copy of the verbatim exit form outside the test harness. The compile-smoke disposal test's temporary-directory redirection was made effective: `tempfile.gettempdir` caches its root, so setting TMPDIR alone left the directory in the real `/tmp` and the containment check reading an empty glob of the test's own directory. The keep check carries every fact the record layer re-checks, five in all: beside the identity, the parent and the two digests, the architecture the certificate names (a file copied from another architecture's pretrain directory certifies nothing) and the code version that wrote it, compared with the running code's -- the value the recovered preflight stamps into the manifest `validate_run` compares the certificate with -- so a certificate written by an earlier deployment is redone at the recovery rather than refused after the train and eval graph. The energy-weight probe hands an interrupt on after writing its partial table, so the shared exit reports it as an interrupt like every other stage.
- 2026-08-27 (this change) -- **The model class round-trips through the resolved configuration, so an anchored run is anchored on the node.** `submit` writes `resolved_config.yaml` from `_config_to_raw_dict`, which every stage re-reads to rebuild its architectures; the serializer was written key by key and never gained the `model` block added with the parent anchor, so a group file stating `model: {parent_anchor: true, descriptor_coordinates: dfs}` reached datagen, pretrain, preflight and train as the field defaults -- unanchored, legacy coordinates. Measured on the first v6 group submitted (2026-08-27, four architectures at the production identity): every pretrain task began phase X at loss 1.1e-2 rather than at round-off, which is the unanchored start, and the certificate refused all four against PBE -- medium 1.043 mHa / 2.985 kcal/mol, medium_attn 0.786 / 1.861, shallow 3.009 / 11.279, shallow_attn 0.946 / 1.635 against the 1.0 / 1.0 gate -- with `pretrain_metadata.json` and each `fidelity_certificate.json` recording `parent_anchor: false` beside a source file stating true, and `parent_anchor` absent from the run's own `resolved_config.yaml`. The block is now serialized beside `fidelity`, and the round trip is pinned twice: on the model block by value, and on EVERY field of the loaded configuration after a write-and-reload, so a block added later cannot be dropped silently. Both fail against the previous serializer (`model did not survive the resolved-config round trip: ModelConfig(parent_anchor=True, descriptor_coordinates='dfs') -> ModelConfig(parent_anchor=False, descriptor_coordinates='legacy')`); 853 configuration tests pass with it. **Why:** the certificate gate held -- no training cell started on a network that does not reproduce its parent -- but the run it refused was not the run the configuration described, and a per-key serializer had already reverted `ae_as_reactions` once by the same mechanism; a whole-dataclass round trip is the check that does not need a new incident to extend it. The workflow matrix waives certificate enforcement, so its clean exit did not witness the anchor either.
- 2026-08-27 (this change) -- **The ablation figure suite renders a stated subset of architectures, and the merged v4 view is published for the PBE-parented rungs only.** `build_all` and its three sibling builders (`build_density_energy_figures`, `build_parity_variants`, `build_per_run_diagnostics`, and `build_bh76w411_suite`) take an optional `archs` restriction, applied to the collected row sets at the one point each is read (`collect_holdout_reaction_rows`, `collect_insample_ae_rows`, `collect_insample_density_rows`, `collect_holdout_density_rows` at both call sites, `collect_training_losses`), so every architecture axis, per-cell PBE anchor, coverage count, fidelity bound and CSV narrows from the same place rather than by post-processing; the coverage note states the restriction, and the SCAN comparator is withdrawn -- pooled line, per-cell spans, energy-density leg, footer sentence and CSV column -- when no rendered architecture is parented by it, rather than being relabelled against PBE (measured: 0 of 141 CSV rows carry a SCAN value and no legend names it). With `archs=None` the seven figures of the merged view are byte-identical to the previous code on the same run. The published set, `figures_dfs6311_v4_merged_val_best_gga/` (29 figures, 4 CSVs, 47 cells), carries deep_3x16, deep_attn_3x16, deep_cusp_3x16, deep_rung35_3x16 and deep_rung35_attn_3x16 on the validation-best checkpoints against 133 held-out reactions (38 BH76 barrier heights, 95 W4-11 atomizations, each cell scoring its own strict-holdout slice of 119 to 133 of them) with the density leg on 191 to 198 species against CCSD references. Best-cell held-out errors against the pooled PBE reference (BH76 8.15, W4-11 13.82, combined 11.82 kcal/mol): deep_3x16 3.53 BH76 at subset size 6 and 7.40 W4-11 at 1, beating its cell's own PBE anchor in 11 of 11 cells on both pools; deep_attn_3x16 3.96 and 4.85 (11 of 11, 9 of 11); deep_cusp_3x16 5.55 and 10.89; deep_rung35_3x16 4.62 and 12.39; deep_rung35_attn_3x16 6.39 and 21.67 on the three cells it has. 46 of the 47 cells beat the pooled PBE barrier-height line. **Why:** the merged view's meta-GGA cells are not readable against their parent -- the open-shell exchange spin scaling froze the iso-orbital indicator at the total density, putting every meta-GGA architecture kcal/mol per atom off SCAN -- so a set presented outside the group had to exclude them (and deep_rung35ms_3x16, whose NaN-inclusive cells and 7-of-11 coverage crowd the axes) without leaving pooled anchors, coverage counts and footers describing architectures the figures no longer draw.
- 2026-08-25 (this change) -- **Trained checkpoints carry their model class, and the spin floor of the PBE correlation parent is pinned.** Neither the parent anchor nor the descriptor coordinates changes a parameter shape, so a trained `.eqx` leaf stream written under one model class deserialised into the skeleton of the other without complaint -- the parent plus a correction trained as the whole factor, O(1) wrong everywhere, nothing raising -- through `evaluation.py`, `eval_holdout.load_trained_model` and the resume path of `train.py`, which fill their skeletons from `spec.arch`; the pretraining hand-off was already guarded by `pretrain_metadata.json`, the training stage's checkpoints were not. `checkpoint_class.py` records the class beside every trained checkpoint (`<name>.eqx.class.json`: `parent_anchor`, `descriptor_coordinates`, and as provenance the architecture name, its rung, the correlation polarization flag, the parent functional and the package version), written by the one serialiser every writer goes through (`train._serialise_trained_model`: `model.eqx`, `model_best.eqx`, `model_val_best.eqx` and the three resume checkpoints) under one invariant the readers verify rather than infer from the write order -- the record carries the SHA-256 and the size of the `.eqx` it describes, and every reader checks the digest of the file on disk against it before comparing classes, refusing a record that describes other leaves (`ClassRecordStale`) and treating a record without a digest as unreadable: one write path for every trained checkpoint serialises the leaves to a sibling temporary, fsyncs it, takes its digest, commits the record, then renames the leaves into place and fsyncs the directory, so a kill before the record commit leaves the previous checkpoint under its own record (or a legacy one under none), a kill between the two commits leaves the new record beside the previous leaves, which the digest refuses, and only the completed sequence is accepted (an order-based rule, record first for the plain writers and last for the atomic ones, was shown to relabel a surviving checkpoint when killed between the record write and the opening of the leaf file, and its atomic crossing state was held closed only by the resume set's state file); the digest costs 0.01 to 0.09 ms per checkpoint (14 kB to 131 kB) and the three fsyncs 20 ms; records are removed with their checkpoints at finalization, and a resume set refused for a class mismatch is deleted with its records before the run starts fresh; the three readers compare the record with the skeleton's class before deserialising and refuse a mismatch naming both, a checkpoint with no record reads as the legacy class (unanchored, legacy coordinates) and is refused by an anchored or `dfs` skeleton -- every run that writes such a checkpoint writes the record -- and an unreadable record raises rather than reading as legacy. Per-file records rather than one per directory because each reader is handed a checkpoint path, not a run layout; the per-directory pull of `cluster.sync` carries every record beside its checkpoint, while a bare copy of the `.eqx` alone leaves the record behind and reads as legacy -- refused by an anchored skeleton, accepted by a legacy one -- which is why the record must travel with the file. No anchored or `dfs` checkpoint predates the record (the v6 groups are unsubmitted); legacy checkpoints on disk load as before. The PBE correlation parent's test at full polarization had asserted a 3e-5 ceiling measured on the implementation before libxc's 1e-12 spin floor was reproduced (its worst residual, 2.725e-5 at rs = 20, s = 6, reproduces the pre-floor value exactly) and so could not fail on the floor's removal; the floored implementation's worst residual on the same mesh is 1.121e-10, at rs = 0.047, s = 6, and is round-off: there the floor is 4.5e-16 of the density, `pbe_fc` forms 1 - zeta as 2 rho_b / rho directly, 8.9e-16 (four units in the last place of 1, 2.2e-16 each), while libxc subtracts a zeta that sits one spacing below 1 (1.1e-16, the spacing just under 1) and lands at 7.8e-16 (rebuilt with that subtraction the row agrees to 0.0 and the mesh to 1.1e-11; at rs = 0.02 and rs = 20 the rows agree to 1.1e-12 and 1.4e-13); the residual is smallest at libxc's own 1e-12 and grows in both directions (5.0e-4 at 1e-10, 2.1e-5 at 1e-13). The ceiling is now 1e-9, nine times the measured value and four orders under the unfloored one, and a second case pins that the floor is load-bearing (the unfloored residual exceeds the ceiling).
