The spec's statement of the DFS exchange inputs is corrected to what the source's `X_L(n_input=1, use=[1])` selects and the implementation does -- no density coordinate, the enhancement factor invariant under uniform density scaling to round-off (within 5.2e-16 relative for lambda in {0.5, 2, 10}; bitwise only on particular row sets) -- with its V1 line restated as the measured tail behaviour (`F_c` at round-off in the far tail has no bounded relative error), its board at grid level 3, and its baseline as the polarized PW92 only. **Why:** a silently mis-classed checkpoint is the same defect the certificate was built to exclude, one stage later; and a tolerance measured on the implementation a regularization was later added to pins nothing about that regularization.
- 2026-08-25 (this change) -- **Every GGA-rung architecture is anchored to its parent at initialization, and the v6 model class reads the DFS descriptor coordinates.** The energy-weight sweep (job 2134963, def2-svp / grid level 3, 1000 steps) settled the pretraining protocol's one open value in the negative: deep_3x16 at w_E = 0, 0.1, 1, 10 and 100 reached maximum free-atom errors of 6.86, 3.96, 4.54, 4.87 and 3.24 mHa against the 0.5 mHa gate and maximum atomization-energy errors of 3.12, 1.68, 1.74, 6.81 and 8.00 kcal/mol against 0.5 kcal/mol, deep_cusp_3x16 sitting at 23 to 27 kcal/mol at every weight. The residual is the correlation network's fit (point-wise losses 5.0e-7 for exchange against 3.4e-4 for correlation; per-system correlation errors of 5 to 12 mHa, all of one sign, on Na2, AlCl3, F2, Na, F- and Cl2), which the energy term redistributes and cannot remove, and the atomization gate asks a point-wise fit for 2e-6 relative on the per-system energies where a fit at loss 5e-7 delivers 1e-4. The anchor removes the requirement instead of tuning it (`SPEC_parent_anchor.md`). `parents.py` evaluates the parent's enhancement factors in JAX on the networks' own conventions: exchange per spin channel on the doubled density (PBE eq. 14 at libxc's constants, kappa = 0.804 and mu = beta pi^2 / 3 with beta = 0.06672455060314922; the paper's rounded mu = 0.21951 is 2.6e-6 relative off libxc at s = 1), and correlation on the total density with the row's zeta RELATIVE TO THE MODEL'S OWN polarized PW92 baseline, F_c = eps_c^PBE / eps_c^base, so that rho eps_c^base F_c is the parent's correlation energy density exactly -- the convention the pretraining data forms its open-shell Fc targets in. libxc's PBE correlation is built on the modified PW92 parameter set (LDA_C_PW_MOD; agreement 2.2e-16 at sigma = 0) while the repository's baseline carries Perdew and Wang's original Table I values (6.5e-9 from LDA_C_PW, 4.4e-6 from the modified set), so the numerator carries the modified constants, the denominator the model's baseline, and F_c(s -> 0) is 1 - 5.8e-6 to 1 - 1e-8 rather than 1. libxc's floor of each spin density at its threshold, 1e-12, is reproduced at full polarization, since libxc is the oracle: without it the H atom's F_c sits 1.2e-5 relative off libxc on its rows and its E_c 1.3e-9 Ha; 1 -+ zeta is formed from the spin densities directly, since the difference 1 - (rho_a - rho_b)/rho loses eps_mach rho / (2 rho_b) relative at the floored channel. The anchored transform adds the gated network output in the pre-image of the networks' bounded map: F = 1 + L(z_parent + gated) with z_parent = ln[(limit - 1) F_parent / (limit - F_parent)] clamped to +-40, so gated = 0 returns the parent to round-off, F stays within the map's range for every output (the range's ends, 0 and the limit, are attained in float64 under saturation exactly as by the unanchored map), and an unanchored network is today's byte for byte; the final layer is zero-initialized whatever the registry entry says, so gated = 0 at initialization on the plain and the attention paths. Oracle, libxc 7.0.0 through pyscf 2.11.0 (the version the installed pyscf reports): exchange F_x within 1e-15 relative on stored rows (H2O at sto-3g, OH and H at def2-svp / grid level 1) and a (rs, s) grid, vrho within 1.1e-15 and vsigma within 4e-15 of libxc's deriv=1 values (the last digit of a stored row follows the BLAS worker count the record was computed with: 8.9e-16 and 3.6e-15 at one thread, 9.9e-16 and 3.8e-15 at four), the doubled-channel identity against libxc's spin-polarized exchange within 4.4e-16 for |zeta| < 1 (4.2e-9 at an empty channel, libxc's own floor of it, which the model never evaluates) and the exchange quadrature within 2.5e-11 Ha; correlation, rho eps_c^base F_c against libxc's energy density, within 1.5e-14 absolute in F_c on every row of the three systems (5.4e-13 relative where F_c > 1e-3; the relative figure grows only where F_c -> 0 by the cancellation eps_c^PW92 + H, which libxc's arithmetic shares), the E_c quadrature identical to 6e-16 Ha on all three, vrho and vsigma within 1e-15 of libxc's scale and 2e-10 relative on rows with F_c > 1e-3 for |zeta| <= 0.999. At |zeta| = 1 exactly the derivative comparison is bounded by libxc's own conventions at a floored channel -- its vrho and vsigma differentiate the floored-density energy rho' eps while pyscf integrates rho eps, a 1e-12 / rho effect, and 1 - zeta = 2e-12 / rho there conditions the zeta derivative at eps_mach rho / 2e-12 -- 1.3e-5 (vrho_b) and 9.5e-4 (vsigma) on the H atom's rows, a regime the production path never enters (`uks_zeta` clips at 1 - 1e-6). Every GGA-rung registry architecture anchored at initialization returns F_x and F_c within 2.2e-16 of the parent on 31,550 exchange rows (11 within 1e-6 of the 1.804 bound) and 15,789 correlation rows (down to F_c rounding to zero, where the clamp holds); the unanchored networks of all 31 registry architectures are identical to the committed code in every parameter and output (768 parameter leaves and 5952 output values at one seed), and the closed-shell fixtures are unchanged. Scope and refusals: PBE only in this change -- `scan_fx` and `scan_fc` raise NotImplementedError, a meta-GGA architecture with `parent_anchor` is refused by `create_network_pair` and by the login-node semantic check, both naming the SCAN commit, and a zeta-blind anchored correlation network is refused at construction (the unpolarized baseline disagrees with the data's targets by 14.9 mHa on the N atom's correlation term). `ArchitectureConfig.parent_anchor` (with `anchored`, which also forces the zero-initialized final layer, and `apply_model_block`) and the grid configuration's closed `model:` block (`parent_anchor`, `descriptor_coordinates`) are applied wherever a run resolves an architecture -- the training specs, the pretrain stage, the certificate, the run validator -- and recorded in the manifest, the pretrain metadata and the certificate; since neither changes a parameter shape, the checkpoint loaders (`train._require_matching_model_class`, the legacy loader's metadata check, the certificate's keep check, `validate_run`) refuse networks recorded under another model class with both states named, a record that predates the fields reading as the unanchored legacy class. The weight-zero refusal applies to unanchored configurations only: the five v6 group files and the reference state `model: {parent_anchor: true, descriptor_coordinates: dfs}` with `energy_term_weight: 0.0` exact, the four GGA groups now submitting as they ship and the meta-GGA group refused until the SCAN parent lands; the templates state the pre-anchor defaults. `descriptor_coordinates: dfs` gives the MLPs the coordinates of the vendored dpyscfl `get_descriptors` (PRB 104 L161109 eqs. 4, 7, 9, 10; the line of the source each is read from is cited in `networks.py`): the exchange MLP reads x_s = (1 - exp(-s^2)) ln(s + 1) of the doubled channel alone, and x_alpha = ln((alpha + 1)/2) of the raw indicator on the meta-GGA rung, at the legacy width, as the DFS exchange network never reads the density; the correlation MLP reads x0 = ln(rho^(1/3) + 1e-5), x1 = ln(spinscale) and x_s of the total density, and x_alpha on the meta-GGA rung, at width 3 + n_extra; the UEG gates, the extras and the parent term are unchanged by the choice, the raw indicator is recovered from the stored column by the exact inverse of its smoothing below the ceiling, and the legacy coordinates are today's layout bitwise. **Why:** with the anchor the certificate holds at initialization by construction and training starts from the parent rather than from an approximation of it, which is what the pretraining-fidelity program requires and what no point-wise fit reached; the correlation network's fit quality is investigated separately and is no longer on the campaign's path. Recorded in `SPEC_parent_anchor.md` Section 5.
- 2026-08-25 `b19c72ecb` and this change -- **Every standalone job script that drives PySCF in-process caps its pools.** The energy-weight sweep job exported the allocation (40 threads per pool on the 40-core node) to the OpenMP and OpenBLAS pools that serve the per-system SCFs its gates are measured on, the regime the workflow matrix's reference build ran ninety times slower in (job 2134488); it now carries the stage templates' shell rule (8 threads per pool from the allocation, with the case guard, decimal parse, floor and cap), as do the five sibling scripts that were found on the same setting -- the SCAN pool precompute, the CH/NO reference regeneration, the pretrained held-out evaluation, the non-empirical pool precompute and the C2 reference regeneration (the last two at 24 threads per pool on their 24-core allocation) -- and each script's log line states the allocation beside the cap; a sweep over every job script pins that no other one sizes the pools from the allocation, in the fallback and the no-default forms, the workflow matrix driver excepted since it does no numeric work and builds each stage's environment at the cap. The evaluation array's template is capped the same way: the held-out evaluation runs PySCF in its own process on its final serial tier and on the whole-serial fallback, while its shard workers set their own pools and the model's pool is XLA's; the train array keeps the allocation, its PySCF inputs coming precomputed from the preflight. One test evaluates every script's block under bash against `parallel.pyscf_pool_threads` for allocations on both sides of the cap and for the empty, unparseable, zero-padded and zero values only the guard lines handle. **Why:** the cap is one rule; a script that restates it must be held to the same evaluation, and a capped 40-CPU run must be distinguishable from an 8-CPU allocation in its own log.
- 2026-08-25 `0cbe015dd` and the correction that follows -- **The pretraining-data generator holds a record's rebuilt orbital gradient to what its precompute measured and to the ceiling pyscf applies to the density it returns.** The energy-weight sweep (job 2134711, the first run of the generator at def2-svp / grid level 3 with the SCAN parent over the whole pretraining set) stopped in its data generation: the generator's density check rebuilt the orbital gradient of the stored singlet-CH2 SCAN density from the record's Fock pieces and found 3.237e-5 against pyscf's default gradient criterion of 3.16e-5, on a record pyscf had stamped converged after 7 DIIS cycles. The record is faithful -- every stored piece matches an independent pyscf run to 1e-14, which reproduces the 3.237e-5 -- and the mechanism is pyscf's own, read off `scf/hf.py`: its loop converges on the gradient of h1e + vhf, with no DIIS, under sqrt(conv_tol), then runs one extra diagonalization and returns that density, accepting it when its energy moved by less than 10 conv_tol or its plain-Fock gradient is under 3 times the bar. The density a record carries is therefore one step past the converged point, held by pyscf's gradient branch to 3 times the bar and not to the bar (the energy branch bounds nothing on the gradient by itself, and the second-order kernel has no extra cycle, so 3 times is the DIIS kernel's stated ceiling; no converged case above it was found in some 250 small-basis configurations, and forced second-order records sit at 0.03 to 0.35 times): 1.02 times for the sweep's CH2, 2.26 times for the same molecule bent to 1.44 A / 102 degrees under PBE, 0.32 for the locked O atom. Converging the reference SCF a decade tighter was measured and rejected: CH2 then rebuilds at 4.6e-9 in 9 cycles, but the locked O atom of the pretraining set, which two processes converge to one density at 1e-9 (7 cycles, second moments 3.369559 / 3.803699 / 3.749466 in both), wanders 37 and 42 cycles along the lock's flat direction at 1e-10 and lands on different densities in the two processes (1.4e-3 relative in rho on 94 percent of the grid, energies 9e-8 Ha above the 1e-9 point) -- the lock's reproducibility was calibrated at pyscf's default. The reference SCF therefore stays at 1e-9, stated as a constant applied to both of its stages and stamped into every record, and the precompute now also stamps the orbital gradient of the density it returns, pyscf's own `get_grad` on that density's plain Fock. The generator holds a record's rebuilt gradient to that stamp first, at 1e-6 relative (measured agreement 9.2e-11), which is the record's integrity -- a density and Fock pieces from different SCFs are refused whatever their gradient, and a record carrying no gradient stamp is refused as one carrying no convergence stamp is -- and then to 3 times pyscf's bar, pyscf's own ceiling. A stopped SCF is settled by the convergence stamp, refused before the gradient is read: one stopped after one cycle rebuilds at 2e-3 (He) to 1 (F-), but an oxygen-atom SCAN run stopped at 20 cycles rebuilds at 1.36e-5, under the bar, so the gradient alone does not separate a stopped run from a converged one. The sweep's CH2 and the bent CH2 are the regression cases. **Why:** the check held a record to a bar pyscf does not hold the density it returns to, so a record could be refused for pyscf's own extra step rather than for a defect of the density; the campaign's data generation at the production identity runs the same check on every system and had not yet been exercised under the lock. Detection signal: the generator's refusal naming a rebuilt gradient of a few times 3.2e-5 on a record stamped converged. No density and no energy changes.
- 2026-08-25 job 2134695 -- **The first clean matrix run on the cluster.** With `d74e69a1c` pulled, the single-architecture matrix (deep_3x16, node of the `long-40core` partition, 40 CPUs) ran every stage clean in 10 min 38 s: submit, datagen, pretrain (the certificate FAIL at 125 mHa waived by the matrix configuration, as designed for a 50-step two-atom pretrain), certificate, preflight -- its reference build covering the seven species the two cells name (H, H2O, HLi, HO, Li, N2, N2O) at eight threads per pool -- two train cells, two evaluation cells on the six-species slice, and the record layer's refusal of the waived certificate at validate_run, which the matrix counts as the expected outcome; the oracle stage passed all 18 cases on the cross-platform branch, worst 1.78e-15 relative (4.6e-14 on V_rks_sq) against the workstation fixtures, the platform line naming the node's Xeon Gold 6148, its BLAS and its numpy 2.3.5. The two earlier runs of the same line were the finding runs: 2134455 (6 min 37 s, the pretrain worker's teardown abort and the three-ulp oracle difference) and 2134488 (40 min 36 s, the reference build at 40 threads per pool over the full canonical set, killed at the stage cap with 9 of 55 species). **Why:** this is the baseline the Phase 41 entry left to be recorded: the whole workflow, from a pretrain to a validated and evaluated run, executes on the machine that runs the campaign, in the time and with the outcome the matrix states.
- 2026-08-25 (this change) -- **The SCAN parent lands and the meta-GGA rung is anchored: every registered architecture now equals its parent at initialization.** `parents.scan_fx` / `scan_fc` evaluate SCAN (Sun, Ruzsinszky and Perdew, PRL 115, 036402 (2015) and its supplement) in JAX on the networks' conventions, at the constants and regularizations of the installed libxc (7.0.0 through pyscf 2.11.0), read off its maple sources (`mgga_x_scan.mpl`, `mgga_c_scan.mpl`, `gga_c_scan_e0.mpl`, `gga_c_regtpss.mpl`, `work_mgga_inc.c`, `functionals.c`) and measured: four constants differ from the paper's printed values by more than the 1e-12 the parent is held to -- chi_infinity = 0.12802585262625815 (the paper's 0.128026 puts F_c 3.3e-7 off at s = 6), the G_c coefficient 2.363 (2.3631: 3.8e-5 at zeta = 0.9), beta(rs) built on libxc's PBE beta 0.06672455060314922 (0.066725: 3.3e-6) and gamma = (1 - ln 2)/pi^2 (the supplement's rounded 0.031091: 1.2e-5) -- and the PW92 inside eps_c^1 is LDA_C_PW_MOD, as for PBE (the four deviations are pinned as bands about their measured values, 3.3e-7, 3.8e-5, 3.4e-6 and 9.1e-6 to 1.2e-5 on the test meshes, so that the constants are not returned to the paper's). Conventions: the exchange row is one spin channel doubled and its stored indicator, alpha of the doubled channel, is libxc's per-spin alpha_s identically (tau_unif is homogeneous of degree 5/3 in the density), so `scan_fx` reads it as it is; the correlation row's indicator is (tau - tau_W)/tau_unif(rho) of the total density WITHOUT SCAN's spin factor d_s(zeta) = [(1 + zeta)^(5/3) + (1 - zeta)^(5/3)]/2, which libxc's scan_alpha carries, so `scan_fc` divides by d_s (without it the correlation is 49 percent off at zeta = 0.9); `scan_fc` is eps_c^SCAN over the repository's polarized PW92 baseline, exactly as `pbe_fc`. The row carries the smoothed, capped column; the parent is evaluated at the raw indicator `networks._raw_indicator` recovers below the ceiling and at the ceiling above it (514 to 546 of OH's 6800 rows per channel, 526 of H2O's 8939; F_x there is 1.05e-3 relative from its value at alpha = 1000, the stated saturation floor). libxc's input sanitation is reproduced -- each spin density floored at 1e-15, sigma at 1e-40 (sigma_threshold = dens^(4/3), squared), tau at 1e-20, then sigma capped at 8 rho tau per channel (the von Weizsacker bound, alpha >= 0) with sigma_ab held inside the mean of the capped diagonals -- with tau reconstructed from the row as alpha tau_unif + sigma/(8 rho), the correlation's per-channel sanitation applied under the proportional split of the total quantities, the value taken at the sanitized point and the derivative there unscaled; the indicator handed to the functional is the row's own exactly when nothing is clipped and libxc's where something is, which is what makes the fully polarized rows agree (the empty channel at 1e-15 shifts tau_W by 2e-15/rho and the tau - tau_W difference amplifies it into the indicator; at zeta = +-1 and alpha = 0 the parent is G_c(zeta_f)(eps_c^LDA0 + H_0)/eps_c^PW92 of order 1e-12, the one-orbital limit as libxc reaches it, which the pre-image clamp takes as a parent at its bound). The switching function is libxc's: each branch is zero where it falls under DBL_EPSILON (alpha in [0.98183, 1.02206] for exchange, [0.98255, 1.04203] for correlation) and is evaluated on an argument held inside its domain, so value and derivatives are finite at alpha = 1 and for negative alpha. Oracle, `pyscf.dft.libxc.eval_xc` at deriv 0 and 1: F_x within 2.6e-15 relative of MGGA_X_SCAN on the (rs, s, alpha) grid (936 points, alpha in {0, 0.5, 0.99, 1, 1.01, 2, 10, 100}); F_c within 1.5e-13 of MGGA_C_SCAN on the grid at zeta in {0, +-0.5, +-0.9, +-(1 - 1e-6)} wherever |F_c| > 1e-5 (8424 points with +-1 included), 3.8e-12 at zeta = +-1 exactly, where libxc forms 1 - zeta from the rounded zeta of the floored channel (the effect the PBE parent's test bounds at 3e-5), and 4.3e-15 absolute on the 468 rows at alpha = 0 with |zeta| -> 1, where G_c vanishes and both evaluations sit on the Fermi-hole cap; first derivatives against deriv=1 within 2.1e-13 (exchange) and 5.6e-9 (correlation, at the same small-F_c rows; 2.8e-13 elsewhere). On the stored rows of H2O (sto-3g) and OH (def2-svp) at grid level 1 above the 1e-10 tail threshold and below the ceiling (8413, 6283 and 6324 exchange rows; 8413 and 6324 correlation rows) the agreement is set by the conditioning of the indicator itself: with kappa = tau_W/(tau - tau_W), F_x is within 1.1e-14 for kappa < 1e2 (5.5e-15 to 1.1e-14 across channels and SCF solutions), 3.7e-13 for kappa < 1e4, and up to 1.3e-10 on the 130 to 286 tail rows beyond (rho below 3e-6, s above about 100, kappa up to 5.6e12), 3.2 kappa ulps at most over the rows with kappa above 1 (6.5 for F_c; 0.2 on the tail bin itself), since the indicator libxc recomputes from tau is determined only to kappa ulps there (the tail figure moves between 7.2e-11 and 7.9e-11 across two SCF solutions of the same system); F_c within 3.1e-14, 1.3e-12 and 1.6e-9 on the same three classes; the energy densities within 1.1e-12 (exchange) and 1.1e-14 (correlation) Ha/bohr^3 absolute; vrho, vsigma and vtau within 1.9e-9, and every derivative finite on every row, the zeta = +-1, sigma = 0 and alpha = 0 edges included. The anchored meta-GGA path is the PBE commit's transform unchanged: the five meta-GGA registry architectures anchored with the zero final layer return F_x = scan_fx within 2.8e-16 and F_c = scan_fc within 2.2e-16 on 31,550 exchange and 15,790 correlation rows of OH and H2O (the rows at the ceiling included, where the derivative with respect to the stored column is 7.4e-6 against 0.26 below it), and unanchored they are identical to the previous module in every parameter and every output (120 leaves, 30 arrays at seed 0). The certificate at initialization for the anchored deep_mgga_3x16 on the SCAN density reads 7.4e-5 mHa on the free atoms and 9.2e-5 kcal/mol on the atomizations at def2-svp / grid level 1 (7.7e-5 / 9.7e-5 at the board's sto-3g / grid level 3), PASS with the parent named scan, against 22.3 mHa / 6.0 kcal/mol (30.7 / 15.7 at sto-3g / grid 3) for the unanchored control; the local pretraining board (`scripts/pretrain_board_local.py --include-meta-gga`, which now writes one dataset per parent density) holds each of the five meta-GGA architectures at 5.7e-7 mHa / 2.9e-6 kcal/mol at initialization and within 0.005 mHa / 0.0011 kcal/mol after 300 steps, against 578 mHa / 51 kcal/mol at initialization for the unanchored DFS-coordinate fit of deep_mgga_3x16 and 19.9 mHa / 8.7 kcal/mol after them. The refusals of the PBE commit are lifted: `create_network_pair` builds an anchored meta-GGA pair, `validate_grid_semantics` accepts the meta-GGA group file and the reference file (all six v6 files pass the login-node check as they ship; `ParentAnchorNotImplemented` stays importable and is no longer raised), and `parent_fx` / `parent_fc` refuse a missing indicator by name. The workflow matrix template now states the v6 class (`model: {parent_anchor: true, descriptor_coordinates: dfs}`), so the cluster smoke is the workflow check of the anchor (spec V6) and its certificate is expected to PASS at the oracle floor rather than FAIL under the waiver; the legacy class the recorded campaigns ran under is rendered by the two keys' other values. **Why:** the meta-GGA group of v6 was held until its parent existed; with SCAN at libxc's own constants and regularizations the certificate holds for that rung by construction as well, and the residual differences from libxc are the conditioning of the indicator on rows that carry no energy rather than any freedom of the implementation -- the paper's rounded constants, or an indicator convention missing d_s(zeta), would have anchored the rung to a functional 1e-5 to 0.5 away from the one the certificate measures against.
- 2026-08-24 `d4b377bf8` and the correction that follows -- **The closed-shell fixtures are recorded and compared under one pinned process.** The whole not-slow suite at the pushed state failed the closed-shell byte-identity comparison for three attention architectures (medium_attn, deep_cusp_attn, deep_notransform_attn_3x16) by one ulp of the potential, on the platform the fixtures were recorded on, where the comparison is bitwise. The cause is the process, not the tree. The recorder pins itself when run by path -- XLA's Eigen contractions single-threaded, the BLAS pools at one thread -- and the archived fixtures reproduce under those two pins, at the machine's CPU count, in every one of their 248 values; the test computed its live record inside the test process, where Eigen ran multi-threaded, and the three architectures' potentials moved by one ulp exactly as the suite reported, while adding the Eigen flag alone brought every architecture back. Two further dependences of the record on the process were measured while closing it: with the pins in force, medium_attn's `V_rks_trace` still read -23.66647650686008 on 20 CPUs, -23.666476506860086 on 2 and -23.666476506860075 on 4 -- XLA partitions its other parallel operations over the CPUs the process may run on -- while the energies and the density digest did not move; and run as a module rather than by path, the package imports load numpy and PySCF before the pin block, the BLAS pools then run at the machine's count, and every energy and the density digest move (`E_non_xc` -67.0032708185235 by path against -67.00327081852345 as a module). The recorder now confines itself to one CPU before any numeric library loads, sets its pins rather than defaulting them, records them beside the platform fingerprint together with what it found before pinning -- whether any numeric library was already loaded, and the CPU it pinned to -- and warns when its pins came late. The comparison computes the live record by running the recorder by path under `taskset` with the pins set from outside, once per architecture, refuses a fixture that states no pins, other pins or pins that came late, and, when the recorder cannot be confined to the recording CPU (it prefers that CPU whenever the process may run on it), still tries the bitwise comparison and holds only a failure of it to the cross-platform floor, since on the recording workstation's hybrid part CPUs 0, 5 and 12 agree and CPU 19, an efficiency core, moves the potentials. Both fixtures were re-recorded under the pins, the pre-program one from a worktree at `ae204537e` as it was first produced: 114 and 112 of 248 values moved against the earlier fixtures, all in the four potential keys and none in an energy, the largest by 1.76e-15 relative, which is the one-CPU partition against the 20-CPU one. **Why:** a bitwise comparison that reads a property of the process as a change in the tree fails on the machine it was written for and would pass or fail on the cluster by the CPU count of the node; the cross-platform branch had already been reporting the same class of difference (1.2e-15 relative on the 40-core node) as a platform effect. The energies were never affected, and the campaign's physics is untouched.
- 2026-08-24 `1837caca3` -- **The reference build runs at a measured thread count and covers the species the run's cells name.** The second cluster matrix run (job 2134488, node dn024) passed the pretrain stage cleanly under the hard exit and the certificate stage as designed, and was then killed at the preflight's 1800 s stage cap with its CCSD reference build at 5 of 55 species: the three atoms took about 3 s each and the first two molecules about ten minutes each, against 8 s for the same C2H2 build on the workstation. Two causes. First, every stage ran PySCF's OpenMP pool and numpy's OpenBLAS pool at the allocation, 40 threads each on the 40-core node, and the production preflight, datagen, pretrain and benchmark-refs templates set the same. Both pools spin-wait between operations, and a reference build is a loop of small dense operations (nao 38 for C2H2 at def2-svp; the OEP inversion runs an inner SCF per L-BFGS-B iteration), so at the core count either pool alone makes the loop crawl and the two together stall it. Measured on the 20-core workstation for C2H2 at def2-svp / grid level 1, (OpenMP threads, BLAS threads) against the SCF, CCSD and OEP walls: (1,1) 0.3, 0.4, 10.9 s; (4,4) 0.2, 0.4, 7.5 s; (8,8) 0.9, 0.9, 11.1 s; (10,10) 1.1, 1.7, 14.4 s; (1,20) 0.9, 2.3, 60.1 s; (20,1) 5.3, 18.1 s and the OEP still running when the process was stopped at 120 s (past 96 s of OEP); (20,20) 6.0, 18.0 s and the OEP still running at 240 s (past 216 s). The cluster's own per-species run log for the killed stage reads C2H2 739.5 s, C2H4 489.2 s, C2H6 377.7 s and CH3 56.2 s at 40+40 threads, with the atoms and the CH and CH2 radicals under 9 s: the closed-shell molecules run hundreds of L-BFGS-B iterations at the 2e-3 RKS tolerance and pay the per-iteration overhead in full, while the radicals converge in a few at the 1e-2 UKS tolerance. Each pool is now capped at 8 threads (`parallel.PYSCF_POOL_THREADS_MAX`, the largest count within 1.5x of the measured optimum, taken over the optimum itself so a production-basis CCSD(T) keeps some parallelism) in the matrix's stage environment and in the four templates, the shell form pinned to the module rule under bash for allocations on both sides of the cap; the train and eval templates are unchanged: the training's work is XLA's, and the held-out evaluation's per-species PySCF SCF runs inside shard workers that already receive their own thread environment of about one thread each. Second, the DFS domain built the full canonical set of 55 species for every run. Read off the real pool and ledger, the v6 campaign's cells name 30 of them at subset size 26 and the matrix's two cells name 7 (H, H2O, HLi, HO, Li, N2, N2O); the other 25 -- 23 probe-set species and the two hydrogen-bond and proton-transfer dimers -- are held-out species whose references the evaluation reads from `benchmark_refs_dir`, and they include the OEP tails of the original build (O2S 1869 s, H2O2 1195 s, F2O once 11623 s), which sat on every preflight's critical path for species no cell trains on. The build now covers the canonical species the run's own (metric, subset_size) cells name -- behaviour-preserving for every species that has a reference -- and the external-pool domain is scoped to the run's cells rather than every cell the ledger file carries; the staging reports what it built, and the preflight log names the cell species outside the canonical set (the N and Na atoms the AE-as-reactions pool names), which train without a density target as in every run before. The species a run's specs carry are not only the cells' own: `spec_builder` injects the neutral H and Li anchors of the Dick regularizer into every spec whose chosen subset lacks them as single atoms, so a single-cell run on a point without lithium carries the Li anchor, and the scoped build, taking only the cells' species, left it without a reference on a cold cache, where the anchor reaches training with no density target and the density channel drops it with a warning (`4f323b521`); the anchors, built by the helper the specs use, now join the cells' species in both domains, pinned by a single-cell DFS test and an external-pool test, and the real-data counts are unchanged since H and Li are among the 30 and the 7. The templates' shell cap treats an allocation that is not a non-negative integer as unset, reads a zero-padded one as its decimal value and floors at one thread (`0923a8613`). **Why:** the campaign's own reference rebuild under the orientation lock has never run on the cluster; at the production identity it would have entered the same thread regime with the same 25 unused tails on its critical path, inside a 12 h preflight wall. Whether N and Na should acquire references is a methodology decision, recorded here rather than taken.

## Phase 43 -- Exact open-shell spin scaling: every descriptor evaluated on the density its exchange channel refers to (2026-08-20 to 2026-08-24)

- 2026-08-20 to 2026-08-23 `fff74e94d`..`387239443`, `f653e3c86`/`0ac3239e1`, `b8f3e1306`/`131a861d3`, `f77090e62`/`f06900d90`, `b1b777e3f`/`56c811a86`, `69fdd4546`/`773fb9b2b` -- **Every density-matrix-derived descriptor is evaluated on the symmetric doubled density of its own spin channel, and the open-shell exchange energy and potential are assembled from three feature blocks.** Exchange spin scaling, `E_x[rho_a, rho_b] = (E_x[2 rho_a] + E_x[2 rho_b])/2` (Oliver and Perdew, Phys. Rev. A 20, 397 (1979)), is exact only when each channel is evaluated on the spin-unpolarized system it names, so the descriptor block of channel s is now computed on `diag(P_s, P_s)`: the meta-GGA iso-orbital indicator becomes `alpha_s = alpha(2 rho_s, 4 sigma_ss, 2 tau_s)`, the rung-3.5 occupancies and the DM statistics are those of the doubled matrix, the cusp descriptor is geometry-only and carries no per-spin content, and correlation stays on the TOTAL density with `zeta`, where its spin dependence already lives. `descriptors.doubled_spin_dm` builds the doubled matrix; every density-matrix descriptor exposes `compute_for_spin_channel` with its own per-spin `mol_data` keys and `assemble_descriptor_features` takes `spin_channel=None|0|1`. `precompute_fixed_density_data` stores `dm_features_a/b`, `rung35_features_a/b`, `rung35ms_features_a/b`, `metagga_features_a/b` and `tau_spin_a/b` for unrestricted records at a measured open-shell cost of +2.5 MB and +0.01 s (Li, def2-svp, grid level 3), with closed-shell records untouched and 144 of 147 stored-block digests equal before and after (the three exceptions a repr carrying a memory address); `solver._reassemble_features(spin_channel=)` and `make_uks_feature_fns` rebuild the same block from the LIVE density matrix, pinned at matrices other than the precompute's -- 0.6 `dm_pbe` and a one-cycle UKS matrix on Li, where a stored-block implementation fails by 0.398 -- and `spin_channel=None` leaves every previous caller bit-identical; and `split_exc_energy_uks`, `_uks_spin_resolved_vxc`, `fixed_density_total_energy`, the one-shot Fock build and `losses._vxc_term` take the triple `(features_a, features_b, features_tot)`.
  - **The three-block energy is the spin-polarized functional, and the potential is its derivative.** With libxc `MGGA_X_SCAN` in place of the network on the model's own evaluation surface, the three-block energy reproduces PySCF's `spin=1` SCAN exchange to 1.8e-15 Ha on O and on OH; the GGA path reproduces `spin=1` PBE to the same 1.8e-15 Ha. The assembled potential is the finite-difference derivative of that energy to 1.0e-10 Ha worst case -- Richardson extrapolation from h = 1e-4 and 1e-5, one straddle mask per case so every difference is of one functional -- over six architectures, both the fixed-feature and the live-feature path, and perturbations in the alpha channel alone, the beta channel alone and both. The superseded two-block evaluation, the total-density block fed into both exchange channels, is -47.9 mHa = -30.1 kcal/mol on the O atom, -40.5 mHa = -25.4 kcal/mol on N and -26.8 mHa = -16.8 kcal/mol on OH for SCAN exchange, and exactly 0 for descriptor-free and geometry-only architectures, whose three blocks are the same array. Closed-shell energies, potentials and one-shot density matrices are bitwise unchanged.
  - **The production wiring is pinned, not only the contract.** Every open-shell test that existed built the descriptor-free `deep` architecture, whose three blocks are the same empty `(n_grid, 0)` array, so a one-block wiring was indistinguishable there, and the descriptor-carrying tests called the contract functions directly rather than through the entry points. On the O atom with a descriptor-carrying meta-GGA network whose channel blocks sit 43 and 95 away from the total block in the iso-orbital column, `fixed_density_total_energy`, the three-block potential, `oneshot_dm_prediction_fast` and the V_xc loss term are each pinned against a reference assembled from `model.eval_ex`/`model.eval_ec` with no shared code, the one-block evaluation excluded by measured gaps of 8.85e-4 Ha in the energy, 2.1e-4 in the one-shot density matrix and 1.7e-8 in the loss; seven single-fault variants of that wiring each fail a named test. The correlation potential is pinned to the total block -- a per-channel correlation moves the beta potential by 5e-5 to 2.9e-4 -- and the live final layer is load-bearing, since with the warm-start zero initialization every separation is exactly 0.0 and a one-block wiring would satisfy each assertion.
  - **Ingredient reductions are the kernel's, not a second summation.** The gradient invariant of each block is contracted through `_contract_dm_to_grid_with_nabla` rather than `jnp.sum`, so the `4 sigma_ss` the energy reads and the `4 sigma_ss` the block reads are one array: the two reductions differ by 2-3e-16 relative on 16-22% of the grid under `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`, the production configuration, and the tail ratio `tau/tau_unif` amplifies that to 4.3e-12 in the indicator. `optimize=True` on the doubled contractions was measured and rejected, the matmul route moving rho by 1e-15 relative and the tail indicator by O(1e-7) -- 1.6e-7 at grid level 1, 2.7e-7 at grid level 3, on 47% of the points. The doubled rho/sigma contractions run only when a meta-GGA indicator is requested, so open-shell records of other rungs do not pay for them.
  - **Pretraining rows stand on the same footing.** `spin_channel_exchange_rows` poses each open-shell exchange row per spin channel on `diag(P_s, P_s)`, descriptor block included, reproducing libxc spin-polarized PBE exchange to 3.6e-12 Ha on O and 7.0e-14 Ha on H and the DFS zeroed-channel recipe to 1.3e-15 over 4504 alpha rows; the empty beta channel of H contributes no row, and `_atom_columns` gained `exchange_footing='total'|'spin_channel'` with the default byte-identical to the previous output. The sigma, `Fx_scan`, cusp and DM columns are pinned against oracles built without the row builder -- PySCF `eval_rho` on the doubled density, libxc SCAN over libxc LDA exchange with tau from `eval_rho`, the geometry-only cusp on each channel's mask, `compute_dm_features_array` on the doubled matrix -- each failing against exactly its own single-defect variant, with the energy and DFS-recipe tolerances tightened to 1e-9 and 1e-12 against measured 3e-12 Ha and 1.5e-15 residuals. Column non-reproducibility is threaded BLAS for every atom, exact at one thread, with O's degenerate 2p hole the only intrinsic source.
  - **The PBE anchor refuses a footing it cannot represent.** `losses._anchor_term` evaluates the network at a FIXED slice of feature space -- the PBE row set with descriptor columns at a pinned value -- which no density matrix backs, so the per-channel block of `diag(P_s, P_s)` is undefined there and the slice pins one arbitrary feature point. A descriptor-carrying architecture at non-zero `pbe_anchor_weight` is now refused by name, the message stating the weight and the descriptors, instead of being silently anchored to a different function: with libxc SCAN behind the network the pinned slice is 405-446 mHa of exchange away from the physical per-channel blocks on O and OH. The descriptor-free path is byte-identical, and with libxc PBE behind the network the anchor is exactly 0.0 on every row of O (4504), OH (6846), H2 (4616), H2O (9146) and of the 200-point synthetic sample -- exact rather than residue, since the XLA-formed and numpy-formed rows agree bit for bit and both divide by the same `np.power` LDA denominator. Every shipped configuration already carried `pbe_anchor_weight` 0.0; the step-6 notebook builder requested 1e-3 for two descriptor-carrying architectures (`deep_combined`, `deep_combined_attn`) and was set to 0.0, which turns that notebook's L2/L4 arms into anchor-free duplicates of L1/L3 and flattens its L3-vs-L4 anchor-effect panel.
  **Why:** the 2026-08-20 finding (Phase 39) was that the meta-GGA iso-orbital indicator was evaluated on the TOTAL density inside the open-shell exchange scaling, so a descriptor architecture's open-shell exchange was a different functional from the one its own closed-shell path defines -- tens of mHa per open-shell atom, 30.1 kcal/mol on the O atom alone, against the 1.0 kcal/mol the fidelity certificate now demands -- and that no stage of the pipeline had ever compared a pretrained network with its parent. Open-shell atoms anchor every atomization energy in the pools, so the error entered every AE the campaigns report. The remedy is the exact scaling applied at every layer that consumes a descriptor -- precompute, solver closures, energy, potential, loss, pretraining rows and anchor -- with an executed oracle at each of them rather than a derivation that shares the code's own assumption.

- 2026-08-23 `7d30cdb9e`, `200a06d02` -- **Both SCF backends carry three live per-channel blocks through the self-consistent loop.** The manual UKS loop recomputes each channel's descriptor block on `diag(P_s, P_s)` from the live closures every cycle -- stored precompute blocks only under `FROZEN`, and three of them -- evaluates exchange per channel and correlation on the total block, and carries a three-contraction feature response, one per feature map, into the Fock matrix; the pyscfad backend's on-grid reassembly gained `spin_channel`, its feature holder carries three full-grid arrays refreshed from the live density matrix, and its UKS callback slices the three blocks and refuses to run when a per-channel block is missing. With SCAN exchange in place of the network, each solver's fixed point is a fixed point of the other to 3.4e-9 Ha on O and 1.5e-9 Ha on OH, the residual being the 6e-10-electron deficit of the eigensolver's `DEGENERACY_REG = 1e-10` overlap regularization, on a grid whose weights, coordinates and `deriv=1` AO tensor were checked bitwise against the record's AFTER PySCF's own first-`get_veff` pruning (5184 points to 4504). The cross-seeded statement is the meaningful one on O, whose 2p hole is degenerate: started from the same `dm_pbe` the two solvers reach two members of that manifold, 9.7e-5 Ha apart. The converged Fock pair is the finite-difference derivative of the reported energy to 7.4e-10 Ha over a random symmetric direction, one diagonal element and the two largest off-diagonal element pairs in EACH channel; pyscfad's numint integrates the same three-block functional to 2e-14 Ha, and the two backends agree to 4.6e-10 Ha in FULL mode on Li. The self-consistent two-block error on the O atom is 48.5 mHa (30.5 kcal/mol) for SCAN exchange and 0.87 mHa for a fresh meta-GGA network. Closed-shell paths are bitwise unchanged, 16 of 16 energy and density-matrix digests over two molecules, four architectures and two manual modes, once the precompute record is pinned: the reference PBE SCF is itself not bit-reproducible above one BLAS thread -- two identical processes gave the O atom at -74.91466456861156 and -74.91466434572143, 2.2e-7 Ha apart -- so the comparison writes the record in one process and reads it in the other.
  - **A one-electron spin channel has no indicator response, and zero is the exact value.** A channel holding one electron is a single orbital, `tau = tau_W` identically, so the iso-orbital indicator is constantly zero on the loop's manifold and its derivative at the then-hard clip returned a floating-point-selected 0/0: Li's beta-channel feature-response term measured 1.13 Ha and moved 0.93 Ha under a 1e-14 density change. Dropping the indicator's response columns on integral one-occupancy channels restores the manifold value, after which the Fock moves 4.8e-11 and three independent H and Li SCFs agree in energy and density. The gate was keyed on the integer occupancy deliberately, because a clip-state or `tau - tau_W` condition would be decided by the same floating-point noise it removes. Along the iteration the dropped term is a real part of the mixer output's derivative -- indicator up to 2.6 on Li's beta channel, Fock norms 0.4% apart at cycle 5 -- so it alters the path and not the fixed point, converged Li energies agreeing to 8e-15 Ha; the docstring states that scope. The derivative check was extended to H and Li along rank-preserving rotations of the gated channel (7.8e-10 and 8.7e-8 relative), to Li at its own fixed point in the alpha direction (5.0e-10) with the unconstrained direction pinned at 5.6e-2 as the magnitude of the dropped response, and to N in both channels on diagonal and off-diagonal elements for three architectures (3.8e-13 to 6.6e-11). The total-density ingredients agree with the kernel contraction of the summed density to 1.4e-15 relative, stated and bounded. The gate itself was retired later in the phase, once the clip it compensated for was gone.
  **Why:** a fixed-density evaluation can be exact while the self-consistent path is wrong, because only the loop rebuilds the descriptor block from the current density matrix; the campaigns train THROUGH the SCF, so the functional must be the same at every cycle and not only at the reference density. On the O atom alone the superseded footing costs 48.5 mHa self-consistently -- and O is one of the free atoms that anchor the atomization energies the campaigns are scored on.

- 2026-08-23 to 2026-08-24 `96fb36fc3`, `7fffa800c` -- **The oracle set: 566 cases at first landing and 570 after the ceiling pin, 18 per architecture, selected exactly by the workflow matrix.** `test_spin_scaling_oracles.py` holds four families. O1 replaces the network with libxc's own enhancement factors on the model's evaluation surface and reproduces libxc's spin-polarized exchange on H, Li, N and O to 7.0e-14 Ha and correlation to 5.6e-17 Ha, with the superseded two-block evaluation 25.4 kcal/mol away on N and identical on H; a 2-ppb defect in the assembly's exchange prefactor fails all 124 O1 exchange cases at the 1e-10 bound and passes all 124 at 1e-6, so the bound is load-bearing. The per-channel block's kinetic-energy density is checked against PySCF's own evaluation to 1.2e-15 relative, which is what stops the meta-GGA case from closing a circle: with that closure removed, the superseded two-block contract passes all 372 O1 energy comparisons. O2 checks the assembled UKS Fock matrices against the energy for all 31 architectures on four species -- worst 7.4e-10 relative at def2-svp and 7.5e-8 at the production identity on O -- with the straddle-mask rule stated in integrand mass rather than point count, since on N it drops 2106 of 9616 points carrying 1.8e-3 of the density and the smallest residuals. O3 pins every closed-shell energy and potential of all 31 architectures to the tree at `ae204537e`, the state before the program's first change, on all eight keys exactly. O4 is the H atom at `alpha = 0` exactly: indicator 8.3e-11, block and energy identities 0.0. The selector expression excludes every registered name that CONTAINS the target name, so `deep` does not collect `deep_attn`, and the workflow matrix's 33 formerly skipped selector cases now run.
  - **The indicator ceiling carries the whole exchange residual.** Against libxc at PySCF's OWN per-spin kinetic-energy density the library's exchange differs by 5.3e-10 to 6.3e-10 Ha on the O atom and 6.9e-11 Ha on N. All of it sits on the 510-602 grid points at the indicator ceiling `_ALPHA_MAX = 100`, 1.5e-4 of the density, because the shipped reference inverts the block's own indicator and therefore shares the ceiling; removing those points from both sides closes the gap to 1.8e-15 Ha. The share is stated in the docstrings and pinned by a four-species test that names the ceiling constant when it moves, and the two-block contract fails that test by 4e-3 to 4.8e-2. The three margins are quoted from 12-30 draws rather than a single one: the kinetic-energy closure keeps 67 times against its 1e-13 bound, the scaled ingredient identity 8.5e3 against 1e-11, and the raw ingredient identity on resolved points 8.9 against 1e-9 -- the last flagged for its under-ten headroom, with its worst draw located at the smoothing shoulder. The reference kinetic-energy helper derives its molecule from the record under test, shown bitwise inert over seven basis sizes, instead of assuming def2-svp; the correlation clip bound states the identity it was measured at and its 1.4x headroom.
  - **O3's record had to be made reproducible before it could be a pin.** The closed-shell fixture is a statement about the tree it was recorded from, and two hazards made it a statement about the process instead: PySCF's thread count, and the memory-dependent grid blocking that changes the quadrature summation order with process history (a 400 MB process and an 800 MB one give different digest tails). Both are recorded in the fixture, after which it is invariant over 0.7 to 4.5 GB, and two architectures regenerate from `ae204537e` bitwise.
  **Why:** every correction this program made is worth exactly as much as the statement that holds it, and a check that re-derives the code's own assumption holds nothing -- the kinetic-energy closure is the single check separating a genuine meta-GGA exchange oracle from one the superseded contract also satisfies. The closed-shell fixture is what proves the change reached only the open-shell path, and per-architecture selection is what lets the workflow matrix certify one architecture end to end rather than the whole registry at once.

- 2026-08-24 `8e75f59f8`+`97c0ba76d` (one change), `2c9d8780e`/`f9017c96f` -- **The iso-orbital indicator's hard lower clip replaced by a smooth positive part; the one-electron gate and the exchange oracle's straddle mask retired.** `metagga.compute_alpha` lower-bounds the indicator with `(x + sqrt(x^2 + w^2))/2` at `w = 1e-5` in indicator units -- `w tau_unif` in kinetic units, so the definition remains scale-invariant to 1.2e-15 -- in place of the hard clip whose derivative is discontinuous exactly on the manifold every one-electron spin channel occupies. The width is anchored to measurement, not chosen: the largest raw-indicator floating-point residue on any point the energy reads runs 1.3e-6 to 3.7e-6 over four draws, so the margin is 2.7 to 7.7, and the width sits 8.56e3 below the fidelity certificate's 1.0 mHa free-atom tolerance. The SCAN energy cost is 1.1684e-7 Ha on H and is LINEAR in the width to five digits (10.0000 per decade), which is why a 1e-12 Ha cost is unattainable at any width above the residue floor -- recorded with the measurement rather than as an aspiration. Because the derivative is now continuous everywhere, two exemptions go: the manual solver's occupancy-keyed gate, after which the H Fock moves 3.6e-12 Ha under a floating-point-scale density change where the ungated clip moved it 4.2e-3, and the H and Li fixed points reproduce across perturbed seeds to 0.0 and 1.9e-14 Ha; and the exchange oracle's straddle mask, now empty on every cell (124 cells, worst residual 6.6e-8 against the 5e-7 bound, probing random rotations of every populated channel, which is the SCF's own manifold). `metagga.ALPHA_DEFINITION` names the definition and is an ingredient of the pretraining-data manifest identity, so a file computed with the clipped definition is stale for a run at the smooth one. Both recorded references were re-recorded with their deltas documented: closed-shell, 26 of 31 architectures bitwise against the pre-program tree with the five meta-GGA carriers within 1.7e-10, the moved set being exactly the indicator set; pretraining reference, only the two meta-GGA columns moved and by at most 5e-6. The certificate's parent-as-model control passes at 1.8e-15 Ha for PBE and 2.4e-9 Ha for SCAN, a margin of 4.2 against its 1e-8 bound.
  - **What the smooth definition does not fix is a rank-one property of the channel, not a width problem.** Unrestricted density-matrix directions on Li and N cannot pass the derivative check at any width, and the cause is not departure from the positive semidefinite cone: Li's two-electron channel leaves the cone by -2.2e-7 and still reproduces the derivative at 1.2e-10, while the rank-one channel fails flat. The indicator's tail response `d alpha / dP` is shell-peaked rather than a power law -- 4.07e11 on the outermost shell, falling to 1.15e1 below it, log-log slope -0.43 -- so an unconstrained direction out of a rank-one channel is not a direction the loop can take; the rotation-manifold scope of the shipped check is legitimated by an unrestricted probe at the mixer's own rank-raised density, which reproduces the derivative at 5.4e-11. The training-gradient consequence is named by the route it travels: the feature-response term carries 6.4e-3 relative indeterminacy before any eigensolver, against 2.6e-14 for the non-indicator columns -- 264 times better than the clip and falling as `1/w`. That response, and the training-gradient measurement its closure needs, are recorded as deferred work 30; deferred work 27 is CLOSED by this change.
  **Why:** a hard clip is a kink, the kink sits on the manifold of every one-electron spin channel, and each exemption that followed -- an occupancy-keyed gate inside the solver, a straddle mask inside the oracle -- was a place where the implementation and its check agreed to look away from the same point. Removing the kink at a cost measured in energy units, with the width anchored to the residue floor it must clear and the residual failure mode named as a rank-one property rather than a tolerance to be loosened, lets both exemptions go and leaves one definition of the indicator. Recording that definition in the data identity is what stops a file computed under one definition from pretraining a network evaluated under the other.

- 2026-08-24 `880b13c91`, `a9ffdaf97`, `2927d77dd`, `a37428534` -- **The reference SCFs are pinned against process memory, so a reference is a function of its specification alone.** PySCF sizes three loops from the memory the process has left: the XC grid loop, the incore-against-direct J/K choice, and -- with density fitting on, which every campaign turns on -- the auxiliary-block sums. The same specification therefore gave energies and densities differing at the 1e-13 level with the history of the process that computed them: one grid block against 54 for the O atom, incore against direct for a 3.6 GiB process, and 4, 8 or 222 auxiliary blocks for C5H8 at 888 auxiliary functions. `pyscf_determinism.pin_reference_scf` fixes the grid block at 12544 points (224 x 56; 35-158 MB per block over the pools' basis sizes), the integral path from the system size alone (incore iff `nao^4/1e6` is below PySCF's own 2000 MB, i.e. `nao <= 211`, with 40 workers per node bounding the choice), and the auxiliary loop at fixed 240-vector blocks with an in-memory single-pass build, stamped `df-aux240`. Every mean-field object the reference paths build carries all three pins -- the precompute's PBE and SCAN references, the CCSD seeds and cached SCFs, the OEP baseline and its inner solves. Across processes holding 0.7 GB to 3.6 GiB the density matrices, energies, potentials and grid columns are now bitwise equal at one PySCF thread, four OpenMP threads still differing through the reduction order so that bitwise work pins OMP at the caller; a density-fitted case at the production basis, methane at 288 auxiliary functions, went from two different Hartree-Fock energies across memory histories to hexadecimal-identical results. The pins are stamped in the record metadata as `reference_xc_blksize`, `blas_threads` and `eri_path`, and enter NO cache identity.
  - **Cost, ownership and the claims that had to be scoped.** Forcing the auxiliary build in memory is the recorded decision, and its true cost is now stated everywhere the tensor-only figure had stood: 354 MB of tensor plus two scratch buffers of the same size, 1061 MB allocated and 700 MB peak resident at the largest pool species, paid once on the reference stage whose coupled-cluster step dwarfs it. 156 of 214 pool species bind, the smallest at 242 auxiliary functions. The pinning closures at first held their owner alive -- a pinned mean field survived deletion with collection disabled, and eighty build-and-discard cycles at the production basis peaked 197 MB above baseline against 34 MB unpinned -- and now hold it through a weak reference, the predicate answering from the system size if the owner is gone and the loop wrapper refusing by name if it outlives its integrator, which restores the unpinned footprint exactly. A deep copy preserves the pins and the predicate but integrates through the ORIGINAL object's pinned grid loop, so a clone outliving its original refuses by name on `kernel()` instead of silently running unpinned. Two claims were over-broad and are scoped: def2-svp fitting bases stay under one auxiliary block for the probe systems only (over the pools the auxiliary count runs 18 to 519 and 87 of 214 species exceed one block), and the one-grid-block statement is likewise def2-svp-specific, the production O atom spanning two.
  - **Two currency defects on the same reference paths.** The reference run log's finalisation raced between shards sharing one directory -- 52 to 79 of 200 synchronised trials raised, and every trial lost a log to a same-second name -- and now unlinks its partial file tolerantly, names each log uniquely and finalises idempotently, 0 of 200. And a reference `.npz` was judged complete without regard for the identity it was built at, so an unlocked reference could be served to an orientation-locked run; `_npz_is_complete` now compares the recorded orientation lock (a missing key reading as 0.0) and, when recorded, the basis, against the requested identity. Deferred work 29 is CLOSED by this change.
  **Why:** the reference density is the input to every pretraining target, every fidelity certificate and every V_xc and density loss in the program, so a reference whose quadrature order depends on how much memory its process happened to hold is not a reference: the same specification does not reproduce, and a 1e-13 difference propagates through an SCF. Pinning the three memory-sized loops makes a reference a function of its specification, which is the precondition for a 1.0 mHa certificate to mean anything; keeping the pins out of every cache identity is what stops the fix from invalidating the caches already on disk.

## Phase 44 -- v6 first-group operations: the two broken chains diagnosed, the alkali reference rescued, the eval pool bounded, the size ladder rendered (2026-08-30)

- 2026-08-30 `(this change)` -- **The size ladder renders, and a default pull carries the certificates the figures require.** The four v6 size-ladder architectures (shallow, shallow_attn, medium, medium_attn) join `ARCH_ORDER` with explicit palette entries (the greens -- no other rung or descriptor family uses the hue; each attention twin the lighter shade of its base), the width-twin colour inheritance is scoped to names carrying the `_3x16` suffix -- the unguarded five-character strip resolved "medium" through `ARCH_COLOR.get("m")` and painted every size arch the unknown-base default -- and the `summaries` pull profile gains `pretrain/*/fidelity_certificate.json`, whose absence made the figure suite refuse every architecture of a default pull as uncertified. The five v6 group files' architecture axes are pinned renderable by a test that reads the YAMLs themselves (all 20 in `ARCH_ORDER` and the palette, colours distinct within each group, the only union-level repeats the two by-design base/width-twin pairs), beside the canary that drives real rsync against the filter and now asserts the certificate lands while the `.eqx` blobs stay remote. **Why:** the first v6 group's evaluations reach the local figures through exactly this path, and the size ladder was the campaign's remaining unrendered axis -- recorded pre-submission as part of the 91-cell figure gap (six architectures plus the ss=26 column, of which the subset axis was already data-driven). **Corrected 2026-08-30 `(this change)`:** the hue-family claim was false -- tab10[2] `#2ca02c` (deep_cusp, its width twin, and the rung-3.5 accent) is itself a green, and the first `medium` value `#31a354` sat at CIEDE2000 5.79 from it, half the palette's own light/dark twin separation, reachable on any merged or cross-group axis; the medium pair is restated as `#98df8a` / `#c7e9c0` (worst cross-family separation 16.90, twin 10.79; the shallow pair's worst is 13.22 -- shallow_attn against the same tab10 green, misquoted as 15.91 in the first correction, which is that pair's distance to deep_cusp_mgga -- and stands, above the palette's pre-existing closest pair). The ladder description is corrected: shallow/shallow_attn are the reduced size (depth 2 x 8), while medium/medium_attn ARE the production 3x16 width and differ from deep_3x16/deep_attn_3x16 only in `descriptor_log_transform` and `zero_init_final_layer` -- a transform/initialization ablation at fixed capacity, to be read as such on the G1 axes rather than as a size step. Also recorded here because the extension changes rendered text everywhere `archs=None`: the coverage footer's denominator moves from /25 to /29 archs, the enhancement-factor "NO FINAL WEIGHTS in this run" clause gains the four ladder names on republished v4/v5 figures, and the methods-box no-transform line folds them in (they carry `descriptor_log_transform=False`). The group-roster test separates palette membership from colour collision so each failure names its own defect.

- 2026-08-30 `(this change)` -- **Pool workers run single-thread Eigen, and the XLA intra-op policy is an explicit argument.** The BLAS thread variables never bounded XLA's Eigen intra-op pool, which sizes to the node's hardware concurrency in every member of a process pool: the inline train-eval task 2138032_15 ran its held-out eval tier (40 workers x 1 BLAS thread on a 40-core node) at a 442 percent node load (HPC operations report, 2026-08-29), the spin-wait class measured on job 2134488 at about ten minutes per molecule at 40 threads against 8 s at four. `parallel._thread_env` now requires `eigen_multi` at every call site -- the three pool launchers state False; the preflight compile-smoke probe states True, since it mirrors the train array's own sbatch environment, whose exported flag is unchanged -- the four worker scripts' `setdefault` fallbacks match the pool policy, and the two superseded `scripts/parallel_*_worker.py` launchers drop the multi-thread flag together with the mis-prefixed `intra_op_parallelism_threads` token XLA had silently ignored. Results are unchanged by construction (thread count enters only the documented last-bit reduction-order envelope); the running G1 array keeps its submitted scripts under the additive campaign rule -- the overload degrades pace, not correctness -- so the bound applies from the next submission on. **Why:** an eval tier designed as N single-thread workers saturating N cores was in fact N node-wide spin-waiting pools; the node monitor flagged it, and the same arithmetic taxes every wall the remaining cells pay. **Corrected 2026-08-30 `(this change)`:** the `--xla_cpu_multi_thread_eigen` flag is measured INERT on jaxlib 0.7.0, the version `environment-cluster-parity.yml` pins -- the thunk CPU runtime no longer gates the intra-op pool on it: a 900x900 jitted matmul loop runs at the same load either way (1574 against 1551 percent at one BLAS thread), a per-thread CPU census counts 22-23 active threads of about 90 at both values, and the computed results are bit-identical between the two policies while genuinely restricting parallelism moves the last bits, so the previous change asserted a bound the code did not impose. The bound that works is the worker's CPU affinity: TSL sizes the pool from NumSchedulableCPUs, i.e. sched_getaffinity, and the same matmul loop measures 733 percent / 93 OS threads unbounded on a 20-core host against 100 / 196 / 279 percent at affinity 1 / 2 / 4. The pool launchers now request the bind through the environment (`WORKER_BIND_CPUS`), `run_workers` stamps each bound worker with a recycled pool slot, and every worker pins itself to a slot-strided, mutually disjoint slice of the allowed CPUs (`parallel.apply_worker_cpu_bind`) before its first JAX import; the eval ladder's tiers already satisfy workers x threads = total CPUs, so the slices partition the node. The preflight probe's environment is byte-identical to what it was before either change (the historical flag string, no bind), the inert token is dropped from the pool env for the reason the mis-prefixed token went earlier, and the tests are behavioural where the previous ones pinned the emitted string -- a real subprocess is measured pinned to exactly its budget, and the slot assignment is exercised through real worker processes. The affinity pin does enter the reduction-order envelope (the last bits of a bound worker's floats can move with the schedulable-CPU count), which narrows the previous "results unchanged by construction" to the envelope the repository already documents for thread-count changes. **Corrected again 2026-08-30 `(this change)`:** the re-review measured the pin ineffective as shipped -- the workers reached it through ``from xcquinox.alec.parallel import ...``, and the package __init__ chain stands up the JAX CPU backend at import time (``xcquinox/utils.py``'s ``device=jax.devices('cpu')[0]`` default argument), so 41-43 pool threads predate the pin, ``sched_setaffinity`` binds the calling thread only, and a one-CPU worker still ran at 694-990 percent load with every result bit-identical to unbound; the same pin applied before the first JAX import measures 100 / 195 / 334 percent at budgets 1 / 2 / 4 with every thread on the slice. The bind logic now lives in ``workers/_cpu_bind.py`` -- stdlib-only, loaded by the path-launched workers as a top-level module so nothing of the package runs first, by the two superseded ``scripts/parallel_*_worker.py`` launchers through an explicit file-path load, and by the parent-side API the same way -- and the behavioural test now checks the quantity that was wrong: every OS thread of a worker-shaped subprocess (over ``/proc/self/task``) confined to the slice AFTER the JAX backend spins up. The slot gate reads the job's own thread_env rather than the inherited environment, the modulo wrap of an over-allowance slot is pinned as the documented degradation (unreachable from the shipped ladders, which keep workers x threads within the allowance -- the earlier "partition the node" wording overstated the ladder identity, which is at-most), and the parent-side delegator is pinned equal to the worker module's environment names.

- 2026-08-30 `(this change)` -- **The reference SCF's second stage starts from the best point of the DIIS trajectory.** The v6 meta-GGA datagen (job 2138034) refused on the Li atom: the reference SCAN SCF at 6-311++G(3df,2pd) / grid level 3 under the 3e-5 lock reaches the solution basin early (cycle 5: E = -7.478697644723, |g| = 7.5e-4), the DIIS extrapolation then throws the density to an unphysical state (E about -4.07, |g| about 1) and wanders to the cycle cap, and `_converge_reference_scf` handed the second-order stage that final density -- from which SOSCF climbs back to -7.47869 and stalls at |g| about 4e-3 for all 50 macro-iterations, so the whole two-stage ladder returned unconverged. The stage now starts from the lowest-gradient density the DIIS trajectory visited, recorded by a callback and cleared before return: from that point SOSCF converges Li in 2 macro-iterations to E = -7.4786979415 with the stamped gradient 5.97e-6 against the sqrt(1e-9) = 3.16e-5 criterion, and the full precompute path succeeds end to end at the failing identity. Level shift (0.25 / 0.5), damping (0.5 with DIIS deferred to cycle 6), a finer grid (level 5) and removing the lock were each measured and do not converge it -- the failure requires SCAN together with the diffuse ++ set (PBE converges in 5 cycles at the same identity, SCAN at def2-svp in 7) -- and Na, the protocol's other alkali, converges at baseline in 7 cycles and is untouched. DIIS-converged paths are unaffected (the recorder changes no iterate and the early return stands); a previously-rescued system's second stage now starts from a better point than the end point, so its converged endpoint may move within the flat-direction slack the gradient criterion leaves (documented above at 2.3e-8 to 9.8e-7 Ha). Four tests pin the rescue at the failing identity, the untouched DIIS path, the lowest-gradient selection with its tie rule and fallback, and the recorder's detachment; all four fail against the previous code. **Why:** the second stage's documented rationale -- start near the solution, not from the guess -- was implemented as "start from the last DIIS iterate", which is only near the solution when DIIS degrades gracefully; a DIIS explosion is precisely the case the rescue exists for, and there the last iterate is the worst starting point of the whole trajectory.

- 2026-08-30 `(this change)` -- **Pretrained enhancement factors drawn against the anchor's own parent.** `notebooks/analysis/pretrain_fx_fc.py` renders, for every architecture with a pretrained pair under a pulled run, F_x(s) and F_c(s; r_s in {0.5, 2, 5}, zeta = 0) of the PRETRAINED network over the parent's curves with difference panels (per-arch 2x2, a cross-arch difference figure, and a long-form CSV), loading through the production builder (`fidelity.build_certified_model`) so the plotted network is the class the checkpoint was pretrained as. The baseline is `parents.pbe_fx` / `parents.pbe_fc` -- the anchor's own parent at libxc constants -- because the rounded-constant analytic helper differs from it by up to 4.553e-6 in F_x on the plotted grid, which under the anchor reads as a spurious learned correction (measured: an anchored zero-initialized build agreed with the analytic helper only to 4.553e-6 and with the parent functions to round-off). Pinned by tests: the anchored fresh build reproduces the parent (F_x under 1e-10, F_c under 1e-8) while an unanchored build differs by more than 1e-2, so the identity is discriminating; the G1 measurements: medium 9.2e-6, medium_attn 4.2e-6, shallow 8.7e-7, shallow_attn 4.0e-6 max |dF_x| after the 2500-step pretrain. **Why:** the campaign's claim that pretraining lands on the parent is a curve statement, not only a certificate scalar, and the difference panel is where the learned correction is visible at all.

## Open / in-progress (as of 2026-06-20)

- **Full DFS-pool training still does not beat PBE.** The 2026-06-20 review (`reports_local/dfs_discrepancy_review_2026-06-20.md`) verified the cause is a *compound* generalization gap -- network over-capacity (4x32 vs DFS 3x16) trained with none of DFS's anti-overfit machinery (no L2, no disjoint-validation early-stop / validation-best selection) -- with the loss/SCF/density deviations capping the achievable ceiling rather than driving the decoupling. The DECOUPLING FIXES are now implemented + verified + committed: 3x16 capacity (`ac4de863d`), L2 weight decay (`ac4de863d`), held-out validation/early-stop + validation-best (`a8f0c45a7`), plus harness resilience -- resumable training (`498e35640`) + resubmit-resume (`60a831a40`) -- and the submittable v3 A/B job (`f48e5f1a3`). The v3 job's first launch (`run_20260621T140311Z`) crashed ALL 88 train_eval specs on the adamw optimizer-update tree mismatch, now fixed (`2a6266215`). **Next gate (USER):** rsync `train.py` + `resubmit --submit` the v3 run (cached datagen/pretrain/CCSD-refs skip), then compare the held-out TEST-slice MAE vs the existing 4x32 run (`run_20260611T022820Z`) -- expect the in-sample<->held-out gap to shrink. The `full_25` SCF ceiling fix + a tzvpd-DF variant are the follow-ups once the gap closes.
- **Held-out CCSD density references** are being generated on-cluster (`a0f8e396a`, `054fc9412`); held-out density figures depend on those refs landing.
- **Best-checkpoint vs final eval** (`ac07c6aa2`, `3fd886669`) separates the single late-instability arc (deep_attn ss6) from the generalization-gap cohort; cross-basis (def2-svp vs def2-tzvpd+DF) coverage is partial (tzvpd `deep_cusp`/`deep_dm`/`deep_notransform` pending).
